Allah never just tells us something and then not give us a reason to believe it. So why should we not lose hope in the mercy of Allah?

إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَغْفِرُ ٱلذُّنُوبَ جَمِيعًا
Because Allah is capable of forgiving all your sins and transgressions. How is that possible? I have spent an entire lifetime in his disobedience! How is it possible for him to forgive so many sins?
إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلْغَفُورُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ
Because Indeed! Without the shred of a doubt, he and only he, most definitely he is الغفور. - the constantly forgiving. The word غفور follows the same linguistic pattern as رحیم. He is not just forgiving; he is constantly forgiving, and constantly merciful. Nothing is beyond his capacity, and there is nothing that is out of his reach, Allah can, and will forgive all your sins. This word غفور is so amazing - it really gets to the essence of his concept of forgiveness.

### 3 words for forgiveness

In the Quran, when Allah talks about his ability to forgive, he uses three different attributes for himself in terms of forgiving- and they all from the same root which is غَفَرَ. The word غَفَرَ literally means - to cover something up. The word for helmet in Arabic is مِغفَر. When you put on a helmet, can you see the head? No! So مغفرۃ means to forgive - or to cover up the sins such that it is completely out of sight. As if it never existed.

GHAFARA

So Allah calls himself غَافِرِ الذَّنْبِ in the Quran

> ‏تَنزِيلُ ٱلْكِتَبِ مِنَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْعَزِيزِ ٱلْعَلِيمِ ‎۲‏غَافِرِ ٱلذَّنۢبِ وَقَابِلِ ٱلتَّوْبِ شَدِيدِ ٱلْعِقَابِ ذِى ٱلطَّوْلِۖ لَآ إِلَهَ إِلَّا هُوَۖ إِلَيْهِ ٱلْمَصِيرُ ‎۳
>
> ( 2 ) The revelation of the Book is from Allah the All-Mighty, the All-Knower. ( 3 ) The forgiver of sin, acceptor of repentance, severe in punishment, owner of abundance. There is no deity except Him; to Him is the destination.

Linguistically the word غافر means the one who immediately forgives. Someone who does not hesitate in forgiving; who does not have to think about it before he forgives. That's غافر.

Then he calls himself غفَّار

> ‏فَقُلْتُ ٱسْتَغْفِرُوا۟ رَبَّكُمْ إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ غَفَّارًۭا ‎۱۰
>
> And said, 'Ask forgiveness of your Lord. Indeed, He is ever a Perpetual Forgiver.

So غفَّار is the one who forgives abundantly. The one who has no limitations in terms of forgiveness. The one who can continue to forgive regardless of how much sin exists. You know how we say, I can forgive everything, but I cannot forgive betrayal, or I cannot forgive lies, or I cannot forgive rudeness. We all have certain things that we consider unforgivable. But Allah غفَّار, as in he has no limitations in what he can and cannot forgive. He can forgive everything if one truly repents.

The third variation is غفور - which, as I said, is on the same phonetic pattern of رحیم and means constantly or permanently forgiving, - and this the form that is used in the verse we are talking about

39:53

إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلْغَفُورُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ

And Indeed he is constantly forgiving and constantly merciful.

So unflinching loyalty; provision of infinite hope; a never ending permanent relationship; an intense, immediate and abundant forgiveness are some the essential manifestations of his رحمۃ.

### Rewards

Then another manifestation of that expansion of his رحمۃ (like the expansion of the womb) manifests in the form of his infinite rewards for trivial acts. You know how we have these holy nights in which Allah offers a ridiculous amount of reward. (Thum Vasheq - 20 Hajj). Today I want to share with you a perspective of why Allah offers such rewards. Hopefully you'll get a real appreciation for the intention behind these rewards.

The holy month of Ramadan is round the corner. It is the سنۃ of Rasul Allah ﷺ to do اعتکاف in this month. It is the act of retiring oneself to the مسجد for a period of time, exclusively for the remembrance of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى in silence. In India Pakistan, people spend at least 3 days and nights in اعتکاف, in which they pray, they recite Quran, they do تسبیح, they reflect on their lives - etc, and Allah loves that! Rasul Allah ﷺ stated the reward of اعتکاف in a HADEES. He said:

مَنِ اعْتَكَفَ يَوْماً ابْتِغَاءَ وَجْهِ اللهِ بَاعَدَ اللهُ بَيْنَهُ وَبَيْنَ النَّارِ ثَلَاثَةَ خَنَادِقَ كُلُّ خَنْدَقٍ كَمَا بَيْنَ الْخَافِقَيْنِ

Whoever stays in the state of اعتکاف for one day - just one day, Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى will distance him from hell-fire by 3 trenches - each trench will be of the distance between the east and west - SubhanAllah! That's for spending one day in the مسجد in the state of ذکر. This reward illustrates how beloved this act of اعتکاف is to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

So one day, during the month of Ramadan, اِبْنُ عَبَّاسٍ رَضِیَ اللَّہُ تَعَالَیٰ عَنْہُ the cousin of the prophet, enters a مسجد with the intention of spending a day in اعتکاف; in order to earn that immense reward that Allah has promised. Soon after he begins his اعتکاف, he sees a Muslim brother in the مسجد looking very sad. So he goes up to this man and inquires about his sadness. The man says, o cousin of the prophet ﷺ, I owe a financial debt to someone that I need to pay, but I am not able to pay it because I don't have any money. And the weight of that debt, that obligation is crushing my soul. ابن عباس says to his man: Would you like me to go to your lender and talk to him on your behalf? Maybe I can convince him to forgive your debt, or reduce it, or extend the timeline. Would you like me to do that? The man replies, of course یَا ابْنَ عَبَّاسٍ, I'd be delighted if you did that! So ابن عباس immediately gets up, walks outside the مسجد and begins putting on his slippers. Suddenly this man remembers, OMG ابن عباس was here for his اعتکاف. Now he's breaking his اعتکاف for me. What have I done! He came to the مسجد for an act of worship and I became the reason for him to break his اعتکاف. That's the last thing I wanted! He rushes towards him, and says, یَا ابْنَ عَبَّاسٍ don't you realize what you've done? Don't you remember what you were here for? You've broken your اعتکاف!

So ابن عباس looks at this man and the HADEES narrates that tears begin to roll down from his eyes. He says: I remember my prophet ﷺ telling me, that if one of you was to strive for the need of his Muslim brother, Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى rewards him with the blessings of 10 years of اعتکاف. SubhanAllah! Not one day! Not one month! Not one year! 10 years ofاعتکاف! What did I just tell you? What was the reward for one single day of اعتکاف? A distancing from the hellfire by 3 trenches - each trench of the distance between the east and west. That's the reward for 1 day of اعتکاف. Are we able to do that? Can we do a week ofاعتکاف? When was the last time we did a week of ofاعتکاف? We can't! One day is challenging enough! And what's the reward for striving for the needs of a Muslim brother? 10 years ofاعتکاف! SubhanAllah!

Now after hearing this HADEES what do we naturally think? OMG I should never miss an opportunity to help anyone, right? This is such a great opportunity to gain the closeness of Allah! And that is true! Sometimes the fastest way to Allah's blessings is creation itself! Other people! Doing good to them. So generally when people hear this HADEES, they rush to help others. They keep their ears open for any opportunity to be able to help someone and that is an extremely beautiful thing! But I want to point out something much higher and much deeper in this HADEES. I want to switch your perspective, and focus it on the intention of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى's رحمۃ. This HADEES is actually amazing! I'll give you an example to explain why.

Let's say I walk into a room and I see that someone in that room is very sad. Let's say its my niece Mariam. I find out that for some reason Mariam is looking very sad today. So I say, I'll give 10 million dollars to anyone who makes her smile. What do you think is going to happen? 10 million bucks!! Everyone in the room is going to try to make her smile. Everyone is going to flock around her, throwing their best jokes at her to make her smile, right?

Now most people will look at that gesture and say, Oh you're so rich! You must have loads of money that you're willing to give 10 million dollars just to make her smile. But then there are a few who will get to the essence of that gesture and say, You must love her so much! You must love her so much that her one smile is worth 10 million dollars to you! She must mean the world to you! You see the difference? The reality is that I love her so much that I'm willing to pay 10 million dollars to the one who makes her smile!

So when Allah says, I'll give the reward of 10 years ofاعتکاف to the one who fulfills Asif's need - even though the general understanding of that HADEES becomes a

motivation to do it for that reward - what most people fail to see is that how much Allah loves me!! Ya Allah! You love me so much that you'd give that much reward to anyone who fulfills my needs and removes my constriction for me? SubhanAllah! You see that?

So another way to interpret this HADEES is - Oh you who is feeling low! Oh you who is feeling depressed! Oh you who is feeling burdened and constricted because of your troubles, it is you I am looking at! It is you that I'm opening doors for! Because I want to see you happy! And for your happiness I will reward anyone with this amount of ثواب if they strive to bring you ease! This is the رحمۃ he has for us! AllahuAkbar!

This transforms my perspective entirely when I see another person in need! I see that this is the person Allah loves! So much, that he's willing to reward me with such such generosity, such immense blessings to help **him **out - so then how beloved would I become in his eyes for helping him out! If someone did something really nice for a person that you love, how do you feel about that person? He comes closer to your heart doesn't it? So after understanding this perspective, that closeness towards Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is what motivates me to help another person, and not so much the reward itself.

So I hope you really begin to appreciate the reason for these immense rewards for the most simple acts. It is the love he has for us that he's willing to shower us with his blessings for the most trivial acts of kindness. He'll give us the reward of 10, 20 Hajj for a couple of hours of worship. You can do it for the reward, or you can do it in appreciation of that divine love.

### Responding in afflictions

I'll give you another example. There is a HADEES in which Rasul Allah ﷺ tells us that when you visit a sick person, ask him to pray for you and your forgiveness. Now if think about it, that sounds completely backwards, doesn't it? I mean shouldn't you be the one praying for the sick person? That may Allah grant him health! He is the one who seems to be in need of my prayers, but the HADEES says - ask him to pray for you and your forgiveness. What is that? Let me make this more relatable by giving you an example.

Imagine my daughter falls very ill (God Forbid). May Allah protect our children from trials. But just as an example, to help you understand this concept better, let's say my daughter falls very sick, and she is admitted into the ER, The doctors come and tell me that she is in a lot of pain. She's not being able to breathe properly. As a father, I can't even imagine what state I would be in if that ever happened. There is nothing more painful for a parent than to see your own child suffer - whether it is physical, mental or spiritual. So the doctor tells me that she cannot breadth and needs to be put on a ventilator. How would I react to that? Is there any other place in this world that I would want to be at that moment? Of course not! I would not leave my daughters side even for a second, right? I'd sit right next to her because I love her so much. I'll be constantly by her bedside - praying for her.

Finally Allah answers my prayers. She wakes up. She says, Babu! I want ice cream. What do you think I'll do? You want ice cream? Of course! What flavor do you want? If I can't find it there, I'll drive wherever I have to get her that ice cream, right? Isn't that what I'd do? Can you imagine how close I would feel to her at that point? What else do you want sweetheart? Is there anything else I can do for you? Is there anything else I can get for you?

It so happened that right before she fell sick we had a huge fight . She disobeyed me and was very rude to me. I was very angry with her when this whole thing happened. Now she wakes up and remembers what she did. She says, Babu I'm sorry for what I did and said. Please forgive me! What do you think I'd say? Forgive you? Sweetheart! There's nothing to forgive! I'm not at all upset with you anymore! Don't even think about it! Right? Isn't this what we would do as parents? Is there anything unnatural in what I just said?

Now if this is how much we love our children, can you even imagine how Allah must love his creation? According to a HADEES, Allah holds the love of 70 mothers for every single one of his creation. If in this kind of an affliction I feel so close to my daughter, when a creation of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is going through his trials and afflictions, can you imagine how close Allah would be to him at that moment?
Look how ready I was to respond to anything that my daughter would have asked for in that moment. I am extremely limited by my capabilities! But when a human being, a creation of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى - when he is in pain, and he asks for something - whether it is for himself or for somebody else - do you think Allah would refuse him in that moment?

When we are in a state of an affliction, in that moment, Allah is closest to us - more than any other human being, In that moment when we turn to Allah for forgiveness with true repentance, there is nothing that he would not forgive! No matter what sin he or she may have committed! No matter how horribly that person might have behaved, Allah would forgive that person instantly. I do not hesitate for a second in forgiving my child, can you even imagine the mercy of Allah - the one who identifies himself with love! Who holds the love of 70 mothers in his heart for his creation, do you think he would refuse to forgive?

If I was mad at my son, and my daughter after waking up says to me, Babu! Please forgive my brother - do you think I would refuse? Oh my God, You are in such a difficult state; you are in so much pain; you are suffering so much right now; and even in this state you are concerned about your brother? You want me to forgive him?! Your request will surely be granted!!! Of course I forgive him! I'll give you anything you want right now. All you have to do is ask! SubhanAllah

So can you appreciate the beauty of that HADEES now? As I'm going through my affliction, when I raise my hands and ask Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى to fulfill the needs of another person, or if I ask for my forgiveness; or if I ask for someone else's forgiveness, there is no way Allah turn down my request. Because I am extremely close to Allah in my moment of trial. He is right by my bedside, holding my hand. That is how his رحمۃ manifests! This is why trials become the perfect opportunity for us to ask Allah for our needs. Trials could become one of the best experiences one can have - an experience of the awareness, and the closeness to the love of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

If Allah is so loving why does he afflict people in the first place?

‏وَمَآ أَصَبَكُم مِّن مُّصِيبَةٍۢ فَبِمَا كَسَبَتْ أَيْدِيكُمْ وَيَعْفُوا۟ عَن كَثِيرٍۢ ‎

## Linguistic Meaning Of Rabb

Today InshaAllah we are going to look at the linguistic meaning of the word رب. But before we do that, I want to show you something very cool from the جمال- the beauty of Quran. This is related to the symmetrical beauty that I explained in the Miracles of Quran sessions. I want to share with you the Quranic symmetry in the use of the words Allah and RAB.

### Symmetry of Allah and RAB

> ‏بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلرَّحْمَنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ ‎۱‏ٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِرَبِّ ٱلْعَلَمِينَ ‎۲‏ٱلرَّحْمَنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ ‎۳‏مَلِكِ يَوْمِ ٱلدِّينِ ‎۴‏إِيَّاكَ نَعْبُدُ وَإِيَّاكَ نَسْتَعِينُ ‎۵‏ٱهْدِنَا ٱلصِّرَطَ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيمَ ‎۶‏صِرَطَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَنْعَمْتَ عَلَيْهِمْ غَيْرِ ٱلْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا ٱلضَّآلِّينَ ‎۷
>
> ( 1 ) In the Name of Allah, the Most Beneficent, the Most Merciful. ( 2 ) All the praises and thanks be to Allah, the Lordof the *'Alamîn *. ( 3 ) The Most Beneficent, the Most Merciful. ( 4 ) The Only Owner of the Day of Recompense ( 5 ) You we worship, and You we ask for help. ( 6 ) Guide us to the Straight Way ( 7 ) The Way of those on whom You have bestowed Your Grace, not of those who earned Your Anger, nor of those who went astray. ,,

In the very first Surah of the Quran, which is سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ, we see that Allah is mentioned twice, and then RAB is mentioned once after that. The فاتحۃ begins with بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. So Allah is mentioned in the first verse; and then we have اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ; again the mention of Allah, followed by رب العلمین. So the name Allah is mentioned twice before the word RAB is mentioned once, right? And you can see that after that, in the remaining verses neither Allah nor RAB is mentioned again. This is what we find in the beginning of Quran.

Now if we move to the end of the Quran, to سُورَۃُ النَّاسِ and if we work our way backwards what do we see?

> ‏قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ ٱلنَّاسِ ‎۱
>
> Say: "I seek refuge with the Lord of mankind,

What's mentioned in سُورَۃُ النَّاسِ? Allah or RAB? RAB is mentioned قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ ٱلنَّاسِ.

> ‏قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ ٱلْفَلَقِ ‎۱
>
> Say: "I seek refuge with the Lord of the daybreak,

And if you go to the سورۃ before that which is سُورَۃُ الْفَلَقِ, what is mentioned?. Allah or RAB? قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ ٱلْفَلَقِ; RAB is mentioned again.

> ‏قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ أَحَدٌ ‎۱
>
> Say :"He is Allah, the unique!

Now if you go the سورۃ before that, which is سُورَۃُ الْاِخْلَاصِ what is mentioned? Allah or RAB? قُلْ ھُوَ اللَّہُ اَحَدٌ - Allah is mentioned. SubhanAllah!

In the beginning- سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ, Allah is mentioned twice, followed by the mention of RAB. If you look at it from the end, RAB is mentioned twice, followed by the mention of Allah. SubhanAllah! And also in the last two Surahs, we see the word RAB used, but there is no mention of the name Allah! And in سُورَۃُ اِخْلَاصِ, we see only the name Allah mentioned, and there is no mention of the RAB. Perfect Symmetry between one surah in the beginning and multiple surahs at the end! Not bad for impromptu spoken text, huh?

### Meaning of RAB

The root for the word رب is actually رَبَبٌ. What the Arabs do is that they combine the 2 با and put a شدۃ on top of the با and make it ربّ. Now if you look any English translation of the word رب you'll find it generally translated as lord. I don't really understand what the word lord means. The only lord I know of is the Lord of the Rings. So lord is not a good translation for the word رب in the eyes of the Arab linguists, because it does not reveal the true meaning and power of this word.

RABB

So let's understand this word رب from a linguistic perspective. The word رب is a composite word, just like the word الہ that we studied in the previous session. We learnt that the word الہ is not exclusive to Allah. It can be used for many other entities in our lives, right? Similarly, the word رب is also not exclusive to Allah. It has several linguistic implications. The word is used for Allah, but it can also be used for people, such as parents. teachers, guardians. So the first thing to understand is that رب is not specific to Allah. In Classical Arabic the word **رب **can be used for Allah, as well as human beings and other things.

Image

![](images/78/b8244e3f-11ba-4e9b-a182-4022d0ce982f.jpg)

The classical definition of the word رب is:

الرَبُّ ھُوَ المَالِکُ وَ السَّیِّد وَ المُرَبِّی وَ المُرْشَدُ وَ المُنعِمُ وَ القَیِّمُ

A true رب is someone who is 6 things. Or I can say, Allah as رب is 6 things. He is مالک; He is سید; He is مربی; He is مرشد; He is منعم and he is قیم. And he is all these 6 things at the same time! You understand? The meaning of the word RAB is a combination of all these 6 meanings. Now let's look at each of these meanings individually.

### al-MALIK - Owner

MALIK

![](images/78/b16edd18-ca65-470d-bddc-f05a0725a60e.jpg)

The first meaning is مالک, which means owner. Someone who has ownership of something or someone is called رب. And the thing that he owns is called مِلک; not the English milk that cows produce. In Arabic, مِلک or ملکیۃ means property ; and the owner of that property is called مالک.
Now do we own things in life? Do we own a phone? an iPad? a computer? Cars? Houses? Yes. So that makes us مالک; and because "owner" is one of the definitions of the word رب , by virtue of that ownership, it makes us a رب as well.

Do teachers have some ownership over their students? Sure! Do parents have ownership over their young children? Sure! Despite how kids might feel - that *It's MY life mom! You can't tell me what to do. You don't own me!* Despite these sentiments that kids have today; and to be fair; the sentiments we had as kids growing up; the truth is that parents have an ownership of their children until they become adults. But do parents have **ultimate** and **absolute** ownership? NO. Because even though parents gave birth to their children, they did not create them. Allah created them! Allah created the child and the parent - Therefore ultimate and absolute ownership belongs only to Allah. So even though parents and teachers are رب in a sense of the word, Allah is the absolute owner and the absolute رب of his creation. He is مالک. And if he is مالک what does that make us? That makes us his property. That makes us his مِلکِیَّۃ.

### SAYYED - AUTHORITY

SAYYED

![](images/78/0b293ce1-5d98-44aa-a320-7be45f893cae.jpg)

The second meaning of the word RAB can be visualized by the word سید which complements the previous meaning of مالک. The word مالک means owner; whereas سید means someone who has authority. سید is the one who is in-charge. You understand the difference? مالک is someone who has ownership. سید is the one who has authority.

Is it possible to own something, and not have complete authority over it? Sure! it's possible. For example I own a car; but do I still have to get it registered? Do I still have to get it inspected every year? Do I have to drive within the speed limits? Do I **have **to drive it on the road? Can I just drive it on the side walk if I felt like it? No! Unless I'm in Pakistan. So even though I own my car, I do not necessarily have complete authority of it. You understand?

And the reverse of this holds true as well. I may have complete authority over something. I may be able to do whatever I want with it; but I may not have complete ownership of it. Like for example the computer in my office. I can do whatever I want with the software that is installed on it. I can install new stuff on it if I want. I can uninstall stuff I don't like. Nobody is going to stop me or question me about it. But do I **own** that Computer? NO? My company owns it. Right? You understand the difference?

I can own things but not have complete authority over it. Or I can have complete authority over something but not necessarily have ownership of it!

But Allah as رب, he has ABSOLUTE ownership; **and **He has ABSOLUTE authority. He has both at the same time. He can do whatever he wants; whenever he wants with his property. If he wants to burn it he can burn it. If he wants to tear it apart, he can tear it apart. If he wants to drown it, toss it, discard it. He can do so at his will. You and I, do not get to question him about it. Why? Because HE has complete ownership **and **HE has complete authority over us and everything else in the universe. And even if we do complain and cry about it - Guess what? He does not have to care. It makes not difference to him.

When parents ground their children; if I take my daughters phone away, she can cry and complain as much as she wants. She's not getting it back. Because I am her مالک and in that context she and her phone are my ملکیۃ. And if our crying and grumbling doesn't get us anywhere with our parents, who are limited in their capacities as رب , it surely won't matter with the one who is the ABSOLUTE and ULTIMATE رب. We are creation. He is our creator.

What we constantly seem to forget, is that he is not the one on trial! We are! We've got it completely backwards. Today mankind has put Allah in the stands and taken the seat of the judge; and we shamelessly question his every move. If you are so powerful then why don't you eliminate evil from this world? If you are so loving and merciful then how could you do this to me? I prayed to you! Why did I not get the job or the promotion?! Right? I'm a good person! I honestly paid my taxes! I paid my زکواۃ ; I brush my teeth twice everyday! Why did you do this to me?! You see? We've got it completely backwards. The reality is, that he is the judge! He is the مالک. He is the سید. We are his property and his subjects. He has every right to question us! We have absolutely no right to question him!

And this is why, what I find amazing about Allah is that he actually allowed us understand the wisdom of his design through the Quran and his messengers. I feel that is an incredible act of mercy and generosity on his part. Because he is not obligated to do anything for us. He is not there to serve us! But he does it anyway. Because that is what a true رب does.

### MURABBEE - The One Who Nourishes

The third meaning of the word is مُرَبَّی. Now this is a very powerful word. مُرَبَّی means someone who ensures growth. Someone who takes care of something; who nurtures something so that it can grow. In other words, a مُرَبَّی is someone who is responsible for the growth and development of the one placed under his care. Now is it possible for us to own something, have authority over it, but not take good care of it? Sure! Backyards in some houses is a good example! People own cars but never get them serviced. We own our bodies but we abuse it. We have ownership and authority over it, but rarely do people take good care of their bodies. Right? So it is possible to have ownership and authority over something, but that does not necessarily mean that we will also take good care of it. A رب is someone who not only owns; who not only has complete authority over what he owns; but he also takes complete care of it. He ensures its proper growth. He nurtures it. And this process of taking care of his property and nurturing it is called تربیۃ. And this is a phenomenon we find everywhere in the world.

TARBIYAH

![](images/78/c49b7488-8dd5-4b26-bd91-725c35cffb79.jpg)

The poet says:

مِن دُرَّۃٍ بَیضَآءَ صَافِیَۃً || مِمَّا تَرَبَّبَ حَائِرَ البَحرٍ

The depth of the ocean nurtures the white clear pearl. He calls the ocean a رب because it helps develops the pearl within its shell. A tree is also called a رب because it nurtures the fruit it bears. So there are all these different kinds of تربیۃ that we find in the world. The concept of تربیۃ is all about setting limits. Setting boundaries. That's what تربیۃ is. The تربیۃ of a plant would be to take care of it. Watering it at the right time. Make sure it gets enough sunlight; protecting it from too much sunlight. Protecting it from insects and bugs. In other words, تربیۃ is a bunch of dos and don'ts, until the plant matures into a tree and starts bearing fruit. You understand? If you just leave the plant to grow by itself that's not تربیۃ because you aren't setting boundaries.
Similarly تربیۃ of children is taking care of their physical needs; their diets, their clothing, their mental needs; educating them; protecting them. Basically monitoring their progress from birth, until they mature into a healthy sound, decent human being. From a physical point of view; from a psychological point of view; and from a social point of view. This is تَرْبِیَۃُ الْاَوْلَادِ.

Now is تربیۃ a quick and easy process? Or is it a very long, very time consuming process? It's very hard work, isn't it? In fact, it is the most difficult process there is. It's a never ending, thankless, full time job. And this is why, when we recite التحیات in every prayer, we say اَللّٰھُمَّ مَغْفَرٌلِی - Oh Allah forgive me و لوالدی and my parents. وَالرَّحْمُھُمَا کَمَا رَبَّیَانِی صَغِیرَۃً and have **mercy **on them just as they had **mercy **on me during the period of my تربیۃ when I was young. In this statement we find the primary ingredient of ربوبیۃ - Mercy! (رحمۃ) . The primary component of ربوبیۃ is mercy. If there is no mercy then there can be no ربوبیۃ. What is mercy? It is a combination of love, care and concern. That's what ربوبیۃ is made of.

Have you seen people who shower all this TLC on plants? They'll actually spray each individual leaf with water and then clean them gently with a soft cloth? That kind of love; and that kind of care; with that level of gentleness is the image of ربوبیۃ. It is not harshness. It is not beating. It is not psychological, emotional and physical abuse. It is not guilt tripping into submission. It is love. It is patience. It is kindness. That is the Islamic way of ربوبیۃ. Contrary to what we see in movies and society today, the Islamic way of ربوبیۃ has always been رحمۃ. And the proof of that is in the life of Rasul Allah ﷺ.

Our prophet ﷺ took the most viscous people; he took the most barbaric people and without a single slap; without a single taunt; without a single sarcastic remark; without a single insult; he turned them all into رَضِیَ اللَّہُ عَنْہُ. The greatest title possible for a human being رَضِیَ اللَّہُ عَنْہُ. We don't mention the name of these صحابۃ without say رَضِیَ اللَّہُ عَنْہُ. And our prophet did that! How? With love, gentleness, kindness, forgiveness, patience; with his أخلاق. That's ربوبیۃ. And what did he ask us in return? After devoting 23 years of his life to our تربیۃ , in his last sermon after حِجَّۃُ الْوَدَاعِ he said...

یا أُمَّتَی لا تُسَوِّدَ وَجْھِی یوم القیامۃ

My people! Do not embarrass me before Allah on the day of judgment! When a result does not meet exceptions; if majority of the students fail in class, doesn't the principal question the teacher? What's going on? Why are there so many failures in your class? And our prophet ﷺ, after 23 years of service and تربیۃ asked us for one thing in return... یا أُمَّتَی لا تُسَوِّدَ وَجْھِی یوم القیامۃ. Do not embarrass me before Allah on the day of judgment. May Allah make us among those who make our prophet proud!

### MURSHAD

MURSHAD

![](images/78/f84f0843-7482-421c-8a2b-9747e6ab9960.jpg)

The fourth meaning of the word رب is مُرشَد. He is the one who sets things in the right direction. The one who sets on the right path; puts to proper use. That's مُرشَد. The word مُرشَد comes from the word رُشد which in classical Arabic means **to ripen**; which is the final stage for a fruit. That's why رُشد also means rationality, or reason, or maturity - because those are the final stages of intellect.

So مُرشَد is the one who takes something in its raw form, and works on it till it ripens; till it matures; and then he puts it to its proper use. He sets it in the right direction. So let's say if you have a teacher who trains you in computer sciences; and then also finds you a job - so that you can now apply what you've learnt; that would be a مُرشَد. A مُرشَدis not someone who just trains you to maturity and then leaves you to rot. He actually puts you on the path; guides you forward from your point of maturity; provides you with the opportunity to practice what you've learnt so that you, and others can benefit from what you've learnt - that's a مُرشَد.

So what we're learning is that Allah as رب is not only someone who own us; who has complete authority over us; who nurtures us with love and care; but he is also the one to creates opportunities for us so that we can put our training to use. He trains us by putting people in our paths that will help us grow and develop. He'll find the perfect friend for us; who will help us grow and mature!And then he will also guide us in putting our training to use, by becoming good friends **ourselves** to other people. That is مُرشَد. Even parents and teachers cannot do that sometimes. They will complete the تربیۃ of their children, but then they cannot always put them to proper use. Kids have to find their own path. But Allah as رب goes the extra mile, and sets us on the right path **after** our training has been completed. SubhanAllah!!

### MUN'IM

MUNIM

![](images/78/80747d2d-bd3b-4e95-a510-940e9d5f1391.jpg)

The next meaning of the word رب comes from the word مُنعِم. The word مُنعِم is used for the one who bestows gifts. The one who gives blessings. It comes from the root نِعَم or نعمۃ which means gifts or blessings. We don't have slavery today. But imagine for a second that if we owned a slave. Now it is one thing to own a slave; and it is an entirely different thing to take care of the slave; nurture the slave; put him to proper use; and then on top of everything, also bestow gifts upon the slave. And the word نعمۃ suggests gifts and benefits that are on top of what he deserves. Remember! Gifts are not deserved things. Gifts by definition are always beyond what is deserved. It is an expression of the love and mercy of the benefactor. If you deserved it, then that's salary, or pay or earning - that's not a gift. You understand? Interestingly the Arabic word نَعَم which means YES, also comes from the same root word as blessing! Why? Because what's the natural human response to receiving blessings? Its نَعَم. Yes! Sure! Isn't that interesting! This is what I love about this language. Every word is so perfectly connected and related to its root.

So now imagine that you own a slave, or an animal - let's say a goat. You own a goat, and so you take care of it. But you don't buy it gifts. You don't buy it jewelry, right? Why? Because you don't need to. it's a goat!!!! Its part of the property. So in the old days, slaves were no different than a goat or an animal. They were barely taken care of, let alone given gifts. Who is Allah? He is مالک and سید - he is our absolute owner with complete authority over us - which makes us his ملکیۃ- his property! But Allah as رب ; is an owner, who not only takes good care of his property; who not only has complete authority and control over it; who not only ensures its growth to maturity; who not only looks after it, protects it, sustains it, puts it on the right path; but then, on top of all of that, he also gives it numerous undeserved generous gifts.

Think about that for a second... Allah as our master does not owe us anything. Therefore anything and everything he gives us, has to be a gift. It is not something he is obligated to give us at all. I didn't do anything to deserve my eyes. I did not pay for my hand. How much would you charge if I asked you to sell both your eyes to me? Forget both eyes. How much would you sell me one eye for? How much will you sell your hands for? How much is your ability to walk worth to you? We just can't put a price on it. These are priceless gifts. So Allah gave us all these precious gifts - but he says he will question us about these gifts on the day of judgment.

ثُمَّ لَتُسْـَٔلُنَّ يَوْمَئِذٍ عَنِ ٱلنَّعِيمِ

On that day you will be asked about the greatest of all gifts - which is ایمان. The word نعیم is the superlative form of نعم and it means the greatest gift of all. What is that gift? It is that gift, for which all these other gifts had been given to us! ایمان!! THAT is the ULTIMATE gift - ایمان. On the day of judgment he will ask us about our ایمان. The greatest blessing of all! Otherwise what was the purpose of giving us these gifts and making us different from animals? So that we could see the creations, reflect upon them, recognize him and try to get closer to Him.

So now we understand that Allah as رب is someone who not only owns us; who not only has ultimate authority over us; who not only trains us, nurtures us with love kindness and patience until we reach maturity; who not only puts us on his chosen path; but he then also showers us with tons and tons of undeserved gifts.

### QAYYIM

QAYYIM

![](images/78/f63ba209-ecbb-4b20-844c-b4731e0c1d62.jpg)

The final meaning of the word رب comes from قَیِّم who is the one that makes sure that things stay together; that they don't fall apart. The one who has his constant attention and focus on things. Because when you're not constantly focused on something important, what happens? You drop the ball! Yes? Things fall apart. People who enjoy gardening know that some plants are extremely delicate. If you don't take care of them for even one day, they die. Some keep fish as pets. If you forget to feed them even for a single day, what happens? Belly up! Then you have NEMO KABAAB for dinner that night! Ladies! If you leave you house for one day - what happens?

So قَیِّم is the sustainer; the maintainer; the one who has constant focus and attention on things. That meaning is also embedded in the word رب. That's why Allah says in the Quran:

اللَّہُ لَا اِلَٰہَ ھُوَ الْحَیُّ الْقَیُّومُ

Allah is the only الہ - who is الحی the giver of life and القیوم the one who then also maintains that life by his constant attention on things. The word قیوم is the superlative form of قَیِّم. The one who constantly sustains; who constantly maintains without taking any breaks.

In other words, I am not alive because I take care of myself. One breadth to the next is not because I eat healthy and exercise, and follow a good diet; or because I live in a healthy environment. That is not why I am alive. I am alive because Allah allows me to be alive. Because he sustains me; not because of my efforts, you understand? There are plenty of ways I can cease to exist in a second. Just crossing the road. One little earthquake! One snow blizzard.

That Indian actress Sri Devi just died. Perfectly healthy woman! Drowned in a bathtub. She probably had lots of plans for the next day just as I do. I'm sure she had lots of short term goals. long term goals. ALL GONE in a second. We exist. because the قَیِّم allows us to exist. We are COMPLETELY and CONSTANTLY dependent on Allah.

Another thing in this ayah, we see two attributes of Allah are mentioned. الحی and القیوم. The one who gives life, and the constant maintainer. Now why are these two attributes used together? Because the one who gives life also knows how to sustain it! he knows how long to sustain it; what stages and steps to take it through. How and when to end it. What happens after it ends. The entire plan of action for each individual creation - not just human beings - but for all of creation is already and completely planned out by the قیوم. That's part of the meaning of قَیِّم - to have a plan! You can't start something or maintain something without a plan, Can you?When you decide to start a business. you don't just choose what business you're going to do by playing rock paper scissors! You have to have an entire business plan right? What your initial investment is going to be. How will you keep afloat! How will you grow the business. Cost and expense analysis! Budget allocations! You plan the whole 9. And you do all of this BEFORE you actually even begin the business. You think Allah just created us without a plan? That's not possible right?

### RAB

So now let's look at the complete definition of the word Rabb.

الرب ھو المالک والسَّیِّد و المُرَبِّی والمُرشِد وَالمُنعِم والقَیِّم

RAB

![](images/78/7a08c24b-14c5-4e03-baea-0851a90c77f9.jpg)

رب is someone who is مالک who has ABSOLUTE ownership. At the same time he is سید. He has COMPLETE and UNCONDITIONAL authority. At the same time he is مربی. He ensures growth, takes care of us, nurtures and protects. with love, care, concern and mercy. At the same time He is مرشد who brings us to physical and spiritual maturity and then sets us on his straight path; puts us to proper use. At the same time he is منعم who showers us with undeserved and innumerable gifts and blessings. And at the same time is قیم who sustains us for the extent of our duration.

Allah as our رب is the sum of all these meanings; and all at the same time. He is not one of these things at different times of our lives. He is all of these things ; and he is all of these things at the same time. His ownership includes his authority. which includes his nurturing, which includes his guidance and direction, which includes his blessings, which includes his sustenance, provisions and maintenance; all at the same time. SubhanAllah.

Now I want to tie this understanding of رب to an earlier session. In one of the earlier sessions I talked about this man of pure essence - someone from the اُولُو الْاَلْبَابِ - who is deeply reflecting on the creations of the heavens and the earth; and the conflict between night and day.

> ‏إِنَّ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱخْتِلَفِ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَارِ لَآيَتٍۢ لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَلْبَبِ ‎۱۹۰‏ٱلَّذِينَ يَذْكُرُونَ ٱللَّهَ قِيَمًۭا وَقُعُودًۭا وَعَلَىٰ جُنُوبِهِمْ وَيَتَفَكَّرُونَ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ ‎۱۹۱

Then he says...

رَبَّنَا مَا خَلَقْتَ هَذَا بَطِلًۭا سُبْحَنَكَ فَقِنَا عَذَابَ ٱلنَّارِ

After recognizing that there has to be a creator for the heavens and the earth and for himself, the word he uses for that creator is Rabb. You see? Because when he reflected on the creations of the heavens and earth, this is what he saw. He saw an absolute owner, who also has complete authority, who is nurturing and developing his creation with love, and care and mercy. Who is setting everything to its purpose. Who is showering every creation with numerous gifts and blessings; And who is sustaining, maintaining and providing for them continuously through time. This is why he said.

رَبَّنَا مَا خَلَقْتَ هَذَا بَطِلًۭا

Our رب, you did not create all of this without a purpose. And this was an extremely beautiful and appropriate conclusion, because Allah validated that conclusion in his revelation. What is the very first ayah that was revealed to Rasul Allah ﷺ?

ٱقْرَأْ بِٱسْمِ رَبِّكَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ

Recite with the name of your رب who created you. Not ٱقْرَأْ بِٱسْمِ اللہِ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ‎. In this verse, Allah is introducing himself to his Messenger for the very first time. Isn't that true? And when you introduce yourself, how do you do that? Do you use you name or do you use your attribute? If I met you for the very first time, and I said: السَّلَامُ عَلَیْکُمْ I am a teacher; or السَّلَامُ عَلَیْکُمْ I can do 200 push-ups. What would you think? You'll be like: وَعَلَیْکُمُ السَّلَامُ, You're also a weirdo! right? Because that's not normal. When you introduce yourself, you always use your name. So I would imagine, Allah should have said ٱقْرَأْ بِٱسْمِ اللہِ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ. Read with the name of Allah who created you? Right? But he says. Read in the name of your رب who created you. Allah chose to introduce himself as رب . What does this tell you?

When I introduce myself as a teacher, what am I telling you? How am I characterizing myself? I'm characterizing myself as a teacher. In other words, I saying that my being a teacher is way more important to me than all my other attributes? including my name! Even though Allah is glorious beyond comprehension. he is magnificent beyond comprehension; he is powerful beyond comprehension; what he's saying to Rasul Allah ﷺ; and to us by extension, is that when I interact with you, the **most** important relationship that's going to exist between us - is RAB. That's how I want you to think of me. not as جلیل; not as عظیم; not as قوی; not as کبیر ; not as متکبر - but as what? As رب .

Now I want us all to take a moment to let this really sink in. When a king introduces himself does he introduce himself in a humble way? Does he say. I'm your neighbor! I'm your dad's friend! I'm your caretaker. No!! He says I AM YOUR KING. KNEEL. BOW DOWN BEFORE ME! Right? If Allah chose to do that in the Quran, would it befit him? Would that have been OK to do? ABSOLUTELY! But what word does he choose to use instead? رب ! Can you imagine how important this word must be to him; and after understanding the meanings of this word, can you appreciate the kind of loving relationship he wishes to have with his creation?

This is very important from a thematic point of view. Because if you look at the sequence of revelation in the very first verse he says:

ٱقْرَأْ بِٱسْمِ رَبِّكَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ

But the very last revealed verse says to the messenger ﷺ.

ٱلْيَوْمَ أَكْمَلْتُ لَكُمْ دِينَكُمْ وَأَتْمَمْتُ عَلَيْكُمْ نِعْمَتِى وَرَضِيتُ لَكُمُ ٱلْإِسْلَمَ دِينًۭا

On this day I have perfected your religion for you, and I have completed my blessings on you, and I have selected Islam as the way of life for you.

فَمَنِ ٱضْطُرَّ فِى مَخْمَصَةٍ غَيْرَ مُتَجَانِفٍۢ لِّإِثْمٍ

Now here is the final part of the ayah.

فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ

Have no doubt about it, **Allah** is All forgiving and All merciful. He begins the revelation with the word رب and ends it with his name Allah. Which exactly matches the pattern of بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. In that statement you have the name Allah, followed by his two attributes of mercy - الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. And how does he end revelation? With غَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ the constantly forgiving and the constantly loving. He begins with his intense and constant love and mercy; and he ends with his constant forgiveness and constant mercy. SubhanAllah!

## Master Slave Relationship

### What is RAB?

In the previous session we looked at the linguistic meaning of the word رب. We learnt that a رب is a combination of 6 attributes:

المالک والسَّیِّد و المُرَبِّی والمُرشَد وَالمُنعِم والقَیِّم

A رب is someone who is مالک who has ABSOLUTE ownership; and at the same time he is سید, meaning he has complete authority over us. Then within that space of complete ownership and unrestricted authority, he is مربی; he is the one who ensures growth. He takes care of his creation, nurtures them, protects them. He is مرشد, which means he not only nurtures his creation until maturity, he also sets them to purpose. He sets each creation on the path of its destiny and supports them through it. In doing so, he is also منعم; He showers his creation with numerous, undeserved gifts and blessings. Finally he is قیم; the one who sustains and maintains them for the extent of their duration. So all these meanings come together to define the word رب. We also learnt that the primary ingredient in Allah's recipe of ربوبیۃ is the ingredient of love. All 6 meanings are grounded in that one fundamental emotion of رحمۃ - of intense and constant love and care, right?

الرَّحْمَٰنُ عَلَّمَ الْقُرْآنَ

The loving, The intensely merciful taught the Quran. Not اَلْجَبَّارُ عَلَّمَ اَلْقُرْآنَ. So رحمۃ is the most essential element of Allah's ربوبیۃ. And this is why we saw that when this person of sound essence reflected on the creations of the heaves and the earth, the word he used to call out to his creator, was رب.

رَبَّنَا مَا خَلَقْتَ هَذَا بَطِلًۭا

رَبَّنَآ إِنَّكَ مَن تُدْخِلِ ٱلنَّارَ فَقَدْ أَخْزَيْتَهُۥ

رَّبَّنَآ إِنَّنَا سَمِعْنَا مُنَادِيًۭا يُنَادِى لِلْإِيمَنِ أَنْ آمِنُوا۟ بِرَبِّكُمْ فَـَٔامَنَّا

He used the word رب, because he saw all of creation reflecting the various attributes of his creators ربوبیۃ.

Let's say you meet a very skilled electrician. You see this guy working, and just by observing his knowledge and skill, you conclude that he went to a good electrical engineering school; or he had a very skilled teacher who taught him this electrical work. Right? You haven't actually seen the teacher, nor do you know the name of his school he went to; but you are still able to conclude that just by observing his work. Why? Because this electrician **is **his teachers creation! And by his skill and knowledge, he is manifesting the attributes and the training of his teacher. Right?

That is exactly what all creation is doing. It is constantly manifesting the ربوبیۃ of its creator. This principle holds true at microcosmic level, and it certainly holds true at the macrocosmic level - that this universe, being a creation, is constantly reflecting the attributes of its creator.

‏تُسَبِّحُ لَهُ ٱلسَّمَوَتُ ٱلسَّبْعُ وَٱلْأَرْضُ وَمَن فِيهِنَّ ۚ وَإِن مِّن شَىْءٍ إِلَّا يُسَبِّحُ بِحَمْدِهِۦ وَلَكِن لَّا تَفْقَهُونَ تَسْبِيحَهُمْ

The seven heavens and the earth and all that exists within are constantly glorifying Him; and there is not a thing that does not sing His Praise. But you do not understand their glorification.

In other words, the signs and آیات of who Allah is, has been placed in the very fabric of his design. The more we reflect on creation, the more we will be able to appreciate the beauty of his design.

In the last session I said that رب is commonly translated as "Lord" in English. But linguists agree that a more suitable one word replacement for رب is actually master. Because a master not only represents ownership, authority and control over something or someone; it also represents perfection of knowledge and skill; which is essential for nurturing, guidance and maintenance, right? So **master** is much closer in meaning to the word رب than lord.

### Relationship

Now once we understand the meaning of the word رب, the next thing to understand is that the word رب implies a relationship. There are certain words that inherently imply relationships. They need a counter part. For example when I say teacher; who does a teacher have a relationship with? With students! It automatically implies that relationship to exist. I can't be a teacher if I don't have any students to teach, right? When I say parent, what relationship is implied? Children! When I say employer, it implies a relationship with? Employees! There are certain titles that necessitate the existence of a relationship. But when I say "Intelligent" it doesn't necessarily have a personal relationship with anyone, right? It's generic! When I say "tall", it's a generic attribute, not a personal relationship with someone.

In the same way Allah has certain names that do **not **imply a personal relationship. For example. Allah is الأعلی. He is lofty! It's a powerful attribute; but it's a generic state in relation to creation. It does not imply a personal relationship. But then he also has certain names, which **do** establish a relationship. For example, خالق the creator. If he is a creator, there has to be what? مخلوق. There has to be creation. You cannot be a creator if you have no creation. You can be powerful by yourself! You can be الأعلی on your own, but not خالق, you understand? غفور is another one. Allah is extremely forgiving, which means there has to someone **to** forgive. Yes? So there are some attributes of Allah that are generic in nature, and there are others that imply a very personal relationship.

Therefore when Allah begins his revelation with the word رب; when he says اِقْرَأْ بِسْمِ رَبِّکَ الَّذِی خَلَقَ. When he introduces himself as رب to Rasul Allah ﷺ, and by extension - to us, in doing so, what he's essentially saying, is that when you think of me, I want you to put all my other attributes aside, and think of me first and foremost as your رب. The one relationship that you should never let go of, **even if you** forget all the other relationships I have with you; that one relationship that you should **always** be mindful of; every single waking moment of your life; is that "I" am your رب. My ربوبیۃ will take precedence in all my dealings and interactions with you - and the primary ingredient of my ربوبیۃ is رحمۃ - love, care and concern.

But then if you look at this statement, he did not just رب - he said ربک - "your" رب. Isn't all the master of all creation? Isn't he مالک and سید - one who has absolute ownership and complete authority over everything? So he could have just said: اِقْرَأْ بِسْمِ رَبِّ الَّذِی خَلَقَ. Read in the name of the master who created. No! He makes it very personal - اِقْرَأْ بِسْمِ رَبِّکَ الَّذِی خَلَقَ- Read in the name of **your **master who created you. As if he's saying I'm **only **your master and nobody else's. He makes his relationship that intimate with each and everyone of us. SubhanAllah!

Now does the relationship of رب require a counterpart? It does! So if Allah is رب! If Allah is master! then what does that make me? And what does that make you? What completes the relationship with a master? A Slave!! Right? If Allah is master, that makes us all his slaves; and the Arabic word for slave is عبد So when we accept Allah as our رب, just by virtue us accepting that relationship, we are also accepting the fact that we are his slaves.

Which is OK if you think about it! Because we have already established that he is مالک He owns us. We do accept that Allah has complete authority over us because he is سید. We know we are in submission to his training and nurturing because he is مربی. We know that we need his guidance in the fulfillment of our destiny because he is مرشد. We know that we constantly enjoy the countless, undeserved gifts that he has bestowed upon us because he is منعم. And we know that he is our ultimate sustainer and provider. We understand that we exist because he allows us to exist - because he is قیم. Therefore it's not the most unimaginable thing to accept this notion that we are slaves to this gracious master. And the simple truth is, whether we accept it or not, it doesn't really matter - we are his property and slaves anyway. My children can tell me: We don't accept you as our father! Well tough luck! Because guess what?! I'm still your father - you understand?

So I just want to set the stage by making sure everyone understand that we are slaves of this master whether we like it or not; and whether we want it or not. That's just what the relationship is!

### Slave - ABD

Having said that, today I want to show you how beautiful this relationship is with our master. Because unless and until we appreciate the beauty of this relationship with him, we will not be able to reap the benefits of his ربوبیۃ. This is really important to understand, because that is how relationships work.

A teacher cannot benefit a student, until the student acknowledges and accept him as his teacher! A student will only benefit from the teacher when he accepts that relationship and submits to it.

A doctor cannot help a patient until what? Until the patient accepts and submits to the prescribed treatment of the doctor. If a patient has diabetes and the doctor is asking him to stop eating candy; but every time he sees candy, he says, Oh I know the doctor asked me not to eat it, but what does he know anyway. It's only a little bit. It's not like I'm eating the whole bag; He's never going to find out anyway. Besides everyone else is eating candy why can't I have it! If the patient does that, he's not truly accepting the doctor-patient relationship, is he? Doesn't matter if he addresses him as Dr. So-and-so. It doesn't matter how many visits and appointments he keeps with that doctor, He is simply not going to benefit. Why? Because despite what he's saying, he is not really accepting himself as a patient. Only after he completely accepts that relationship, and submits to the doctors treatment - only then will he be able to gain the benefits that the doctor wants him to have. Its a two way street. And what's interesting, is that doctor is completely helpless in this relationship - despite his vast knowledge and skill. The doctor **has **the knowledge to benefit the patient. The doctor truly **cares **about the patient; he **wants** to benefit the patient; but despite all his knowledge, will and desire to help and benefit the patient, he is helpless - he cannot do anything - because the patient has not yet completed the relationship. You see?

I'm not really talking about doctors and patients, Am I? We all claim that Allah is our رب. We all know about his infinite رحمۃ. We all know he wants the best for us. We all know the treatment he has prescribed for our souls. We all know the do's and don'ts! We even pray and fulfill our religious obligations - but then in our daily lives, we do everything that goes against his prescribed treatment. So then how is our رب supposed to help and benefit us? Isn't that interesting? Despite the infinite powers of our رب , we render him helpless by not completing the relationship.

So if I truly want to gain the benefits that my رب has to offer, what do I need to do?

![](images/82/cfa7ce2f-61a4-4cb1-9479-ff4021731839.jpg)

I have to complete the relationship. How do I complete the relationship? By submitting myself as his عبد. That is how I can complete my relationship with him. You understand? His relationship with me is that of ربوبیۃ. I complete that relationship, with my عبودیۃ and my عبادۃ - My submission, my servitude and my worship.

### Worship - IBADAH

Now let's understand these words عبادۃ and عبودیۃ properly. The word عبادۃ is generally translated as worship - which is an action; and عبودیۃ is the sentiment that makes me want to worship. You understand the difference? عبادۃ are the various forms of worship, عبودیۃ is the feeling of servitude that acts as the driving force behind my various acts of worship.

IBADAH

![](images/82/11062766-b962-4709-ae7b-87ee1d9d0120.jpg)

Now what's interesting is that linguistically the word عبادۃ means two things. It means عُبُودِیَّۃُ وَ الطَّاعَۃُ it means worship **and **it also means obedience - both at the same time. Let's break it down. What is worship? When we think of the word worship, what generally comes to mind? 5 prayers, fasting. ZAKAT etc. right? Those are all different forms of worship. And we think that's it! We stop at those activities. That's the problem with this translation of the word عبادۃ meaning just worship. It does not necessarily include the concept of obedience .

The word عبادۃ includes obedience. And it's the kind of obedience that comes out of love - not force! Because the word obedience can have a very negative connotation attached to it. People can be forced into obedience. Children are often bullied into obedience by the use of force or worse. Historically slaves have been beaten and tortured into obedience like animals. So the word obedience can have a very negative emotion attached to it. But this is not the kind of forced obedience we're talking about here. That would not be عبادۃ. The word for that that kind of forced obedience is رِقَّۃ in Arabic, which is equivalent to غلامی in Urdu. You understand the difference? رقۃ is forced obedience; عبودیۃ is willing obedience. Worship, is actually a manifestation of that obedience. Think about that! The 7 forms of worship - وِلَایَۃٌ، طَھَارَۃٌ ، صَلَوَاۃٌ، زَکَوَاۃٌ، حَجٌّ، جِھَادٌ these are all manifestations of obedience, are they not? We do that because Allah has commanded us to do that. So we execute all these various forms of worship in obedience to his command, yes? Now if you are obeying willingly and happily - then you are in a state of عبودیۃ - which is the desired state. If you are doing it with a grudge; if you're not actually happy to do it, you are in a state of رقۃ, which is **not **the desired state. It's the same forms of worship - You're performing صلواۃ in both cases! ٰBut if you're praying unwillingly, it's no longer عبادۃ, it's رقۃ. You understand?

So then, in the final analysis, عبادۃ is nothing, but a willing obedience of the beloved, generated from the emotion of passionate and unconditional love. This is why, when اِمَامُ جَعْفَرٍ الصَّادِقِ was asked, یا أمام! ھَلِ الدِّینُ؟ - Oh Imam! What is the essence of religion? What did he say? اِلَّا الطَّاعَۃُ- nothing but obedience. Then the man asked: ھَلِ الطَّاعَۃُ؟ What is obedience? He replied الا الحُب - nothing but love. So the essence of all forms of worship is willing obedience, and the essence of willing obedience is love.

Worship & Obedience

![](images/82/281db322-3bb2-41ea-8b21-09f2524569b1.jpg)

Now we learnt that Allah is ال الہ - He is "The الہ". He is the one who takes precedence over all forms of الہ in our lives. But what kind of relationship does he have with us? He is our رب. The word Allah describes who he is, and what he is **generally **supposed to mean in our lives; but the word رب describes his **personal **relationship with us. In other words, he is the entity that is to be worshiped because he is اللہ- and he is also the entity that is to be willingly obeyed, because he is رب.

To put it in practical terms, why are these two things important? Lets say we are about to pray in 15 minutes. Let's say it's time forفجر in 15 minutes and we have to do our عبادۃ. Now what kind of عبادۃ is صلوۃ ? Worship or obedience? Worship, right? But then from فجر until ظھر we may not be worshiping him, but we should be doing what? Obeying Him! You understand? If we truly consider him an الہ and if we truly accept his relationship with us as رب, then between two prayers you and I might not necessarily be worshiping Him; but we still need to be in obedience to him! This is why the concept of عبادۃ is so comprehensive in Islam. All forms of obedience are considered عبادۃ in Islam. Therefore if you really want to understand the word عبادۃ, I prefer the word obedience **over **worship. Because unconditional willing obedience, **is **worship! You go and earn a حلال income at your job, that's an act of عبادۃ. You go to school and sincerely accomplish the purpose of learning, that's عبادۃ. You obey, love and respect your parents and teachers, that's عبادۃ. You are kind to your siblings; generous to the needy; respectful to your elders, that's عبادۃ. You wash your hands before you eat, you cover your head before you go to the toilet, you smile at someone, you do وضو before you sleep - those are all acts of عبادۃ because they are being done in the willing obedience, and out of sincere love for the beloved. So you can see that the word عبادۃ in Islam is a very comprehensive term, and is not just restricted to the forms of worship.

### Slave - ABD

Now that we understand the word عبودیۃ, let's understand the word عبد which means a slave. Now what is a slave? A lot of Muslims take offense to this word slave - because it creates a very negative image in their mind, based on how masters have historically treated slaves. But I want to use these same negative examples to draw a comparison between the kind of masters the world has seen - and the kind of master our رب is

### Freedom of choice

Let's begin with the term slave - What is a slave? What is the most defining characteristic of a slave? A slave is someone who does not have free will. A slave does not have a choice in any decision that his master makes. He must obey the will of his master without question. A slave has no existence of his own, other than fulfilling the desires of his master. Isn't that right? A master completely denies and prohibits free will for his slaves - which is the ultimate form of humiliation! Taking away an intelligent persons freedom of choice is the worst form of humiliation - because it says: I do not think you are capable of making the right choices! It says, you are no different than an animal to me! Just as I choose and make decisions for my cattle and my dog, I am going to make your decisions for you! Denying the freedom of choice is the ultimate form of humiliation and disgrace for a human being.

Now when it comes to our master, what is the most precious gift he gave us? The ultimate gift that distinguishes us from all other creations of this world? Free will! Did he need to do that? No! He is مالک. He is my owner. He is the owner of every single creation in this universe. That fact alone makes every single creation his property! His slave! Doesn't it? That means being his property, or his slave, has nothing to do with being Muslim or not. Because everything is his property and slave. The sun is his property, the moon is his property, the galaxies are his property. This entire universe, and everything within it, including you and I are his property. Does the sun has free will? Does the moon has free will? Does the night and day have free will? No! They're all in complete submission to the will of their creator

وَکُلٌّ فِی فَلَکٍ یَسْبَحُونَ. They are all floating in their orbits. They have been bound by gravity. They have no choice, but to float in their orbits! And Allah has bound them and taken away their freedom of choice. Could he have done that to us? Could Allah have bound us? Could he have forced us into submission? Could the one who has compelled and enslaved the sun, moon, solar systems and galaxies, could he have forced us into submission? Absolutely! But did he? No! He gave us an intellect, and the freedom of choice. Something that he did not give to any other creation! SubhanAllah!

So right off the bat, we can see that Indeed Allah is our master! He is our رب but his ربوبیۃ is unlike any other master we know of. The first thing every master we know of will do, is take away our freedom of choice! Teachers, Parents, Bosses, Judicial systems, Doctors - Any worldly رب you can think of, will try to control us by restricting our free will. But our Allah is the only رب who has granted us complete freedom of choice. Now if denying freedom of choice is the ultimate humiliation, then allowing complete freedom of choice is what? The ultimate honor! SubhanAllah! Allah honored us above and beyond every other creation by granting us freedom of choice.

وَقُلِ ٱلْحَقُّ مِن رَّبِّكُمْ ۖ فَمَن شَآءَ فَلْيُؤْمِن وَمَن شَآءَ فَلْيَكْفُرْ

And say: "The truth has been revealed by your master." Then whosoever wills, let him believe, and whosoever wills, let him disbelieve

### Expectations

Now what kind of gifts do masters give their slaves? Nothing! They barely feed them, they barely clothe them, they barely pay them. And when they do feed and clothe them, they give them the bare minimum. Just enough to keep them alive, yes? And for the very little they give them, how much do they expect back in return? A lot!! Every master we know of, gives the bare minimum, and expects the maximum from his slaves, Yes?

What has Allah given us? We talked about the numerous undeserved gifts that the منعم has showered upon his creation! Precious gifts that are above and beyond the reason and logic of economics. Any company that gives its employees so much would bleed red. Now in comparison to the quantity and quality of gifts that our رب bestowed upon us, how much does he expect in return? The bare minimum! The very least. SubhanAllah! Of all creations, our master gave us the most! The rest of creation is constantly in a state of عبودیۃ to him. They're all working round the clock, in constant obedience to Allah, without taking a moment's break. To serve us! But then despite giving us the most, he expects the least from us in return. 1 hours of صلواۃ a day. 1 out of 12 months of fast - that too only from sunrise to sunset. 2.5% of our wealth in زکواۃ - the wealth that he gave us in the first place. It's incredible!

And even then, you missed the صلواۃ no problem - do قضاء. You can't find water, no problem! Do تیمم. You're sick or travelling? No problem - complete your fasts later. You don't have a saving that has been left untouched for a year? No Problem! You don't have to pay زکواۃ. You don't have the money for حج, or travelling is not safe? No problem! I life the obligation of حج from your shoulders. I mean this is crazy! How easy has he made this for us?! What kind of a master does that? Do you know of any other master in this world who would make it this easy for a slave? Do you know of any other master in this world who gives so much, and asks for so little in return? We've never heard of such a master!

### Explanations

On top of that, if that wasn't generous easy enough, he also sent us revelation and messengers to explain his reasons to us. Does a master every explain his reasons to his slaves? Can any slave tell his master, Explain the purpose of this command to me! Can a slave negotiate with its master? Can a slave say, I'll obey your command only if and when you explain it to me! Can a slave do that? No! But did this master explain things to us in the Quran? He did. Did our master through the life example of Rasul Allah ﷺ show us how to practically execute our obligations? Why? To make it easy for us to accept his relationship. To make عبودیۃ easy for us.

يُرِيدُ ٱللَّهُ بِكُمُ ٱلْيُسْرَ وَلَا يُرِيدُ بِكُمُ ٱلْعُسْرَ

Allah desires ease for you. He does not desire hardship for you.

### Who does it benefit?

Now here's another question. Who are the acts of worship meant to benefit? Him or us? Who is slavery always designed to benefit? The master? or the slave? Always the master! But this master, he designed the universe for us; He gave the most to us! He asked the least in return from us! Explained the reasons of his commands to us! But the question is, everything he asked us to do, who does it benefits? It benefits us and only us. SubhanAllah! Every slave in the world is bought and put to work to benefit the master. Every command issued to a slave is for the master's benefits. What kind of a master is this? Whose every single command is meant for the benefit of his slaves. Not one single command is for his own benefit. If that is not love. If that is not care. If that is not mercy. If that is not ease. If that is not رحمۃ then you tell me what is! AllahuAkbar! He is in constant service to us! And what he asks from us in return, is also to benefit only us - not him!

### Judgment

Now here's another question. Is the work of a slave judged according to the slaves capabilities, or according to the masters exceptions? A slaves work is always judged according to the level of the masters standards and expectations. How does Allah judge our actions? According to our individual levels of knowledge and capabilities. No where in the Quran does Allah demand perfection! The only thing he asks for in the Quran is effort!

### Service

Do you know of any master in the world that rushes to fulfill the needs of his slaves? Have you ever watched a movie in which a slave calls out to his master in need and the master drops everything and rushes to his aid? What does our RAB do?

> ‏وَإِذَا سَأَلَكَ عِبَادِى عَنِّى فَإِنِّى قَرِيبٌ ۖ أُجِيبُ دَعْوَةَ ٱلدَّاعِ إِذَا دَعَانِ‎۱۸۶
>
> And when My slaves ask you concerning Me, Indeed I near him in that very moment. I immediately respond to the invocations of the supplicant when he calls upon Me.

No master every does that. From what history tells us, slaves are always rushed to fulfill the needs of their masters; never the other way round! So then what kind of a merciful master is this who rushes to fulfill the needs of his slaves?

So the more we reflect upon the ربوبیۃ of this master, the more we realize that his رحمۃ and mercy is absolutely incredible! We see that he is extremely loving; extremely caring and extremely merciful. He is unlike any other master that we know of. He is unlike any other master we will ever come to know of in this world. No master does for his slave what our رب does for us. But the problem is that because of his subtle love and mercy, we forget our place before him. We forget that we are his property. We forget that he is مالک! He is owner. Because if he is owner, then by definition. that makes me property. Which means he can do whatever he wants with me. He can break me. He can rip me apart. He can burn me. drown me. He can do whatever he wants with me. And when he chooses to do that, there is absolutely nothing that I can do. or you can can do; or all of us put together can do; or everything in this universe combined can do to protect me against his wrath.

إِن يُرِدْنِ ٱلرَّحْمَنُ بِضُرٍّۢ . لَّا تُغْنِ عَنِّى شَفَعَتُهُمْ شَيْـًۭٔا وَلَا يُنقِذُونِ

If the Rehman decides to harm me. then absolutely nothing in existence will be able to help me. Look at that.

إِن يُرِدْنِ ٱلرَّحْمَنُ بِضُرٍّۢ

If the REHMAN decides to harm you. SubhanAllah! Not

إِن يُرِدْنِ الجبار. إِن يُرِدْنِ القھار. إِن يُرِدْنِ ذوالنتقام

If the conqueror. the powerful. the avenger. decides to harm you. NO NO NO.

إِن يُرِدْنِ ٱلرَّحْمَنُ

If the REHMAN decides to harm you. Even in his ضُر. Even in his harm, he is merciful, in that there is a hidden benefit. SubhanAllah!

And yet! we constantly disobey Allah. We constantly fail in our عبودیۃ, in our obedience and servitude to him. But he still allows us to exist. Just think about that for a second.

You know my phone was causing problems. It was acting up! freezing up on me. Every now and then I would have to restart it again. You know what I did? I went to the store, and I got a new phone. I got rid of the old one. Why? Because occasionally, not all the time - occasionally, a few times it acted up on me. So I got rid of it. Because that's what you do with property that is no longer doing its job. You discard it. We are all Allah's property, and we are not doing our jobs. Not occasionally, not sometimes; but in some cases - constantly! All the time! So what should my owner do with me? What should my owner do with such a useless property as myself? Discard it right?. But does he do that? AllahuAkbar!

So you can see that even words such as رب and عبد(master and slave) are used, he is unlike any other master we can imagine in this world. This is a master who loves us intensely and constantly. And so when we realize that, what is our natural response to him? We love him back! And that is yet another unique relationship between a master and a slave.

### Love

There is not a single master in this world, where a slave would love its master. In fact, historically, slaves have always hated their their masters. The first chance they get, they want to run away from their masters. Isn't that true? But we wholeheartedly love our master; and so the only way, the only direction we want to run, is towards this master, not away from him. And how do we do that? By submitting ourselves to his love and mercy. Through our willing obedience. Not a forced obedience as we experience in the world. But a willing obedience. An obedience that is based on the highest stages of love that we learnt earlier. An obedience that manifests in the desire of service and servitude; like the service of a passionate lover (an عاشق)for his معشوق - for his beloved! That is عبودیۃ! That is worship! And there in lies our greatest challenge!

Now listen to me very carefully... The greatest challenge! The greatest difficulty! The greatest hardship! that you and I constantly face in life; every single waking moment - is to accept Allah as رب. I'll say that again. The greatest and constant challenge you and I; and everyone else in this world faces; is not in accepting our creator as Allah. Everyone is fine with that part. Even the جاھلی عرب accepted him as Allah. There is no one in this group here that has a problem with accepting him as Allah! The real problem, and the real challenge is in accepting him as رب- as master! You know why? Because if we accept him as رب, that means we have to become what? We have to become slaves. We have to accept his relationship; give up the freedoms of our passions and desires; an put on the collar of his عبودیۃ to become his slave. And that is where we all struggle.

Because we all live in the times of absolute and unrestricted self indulgence. We live in the times where I am in charge. I am in control. I am the master of my own destiny. I am powerful. I am wealthy. I don't have to listen to you. You're not the boss of me. I can do whatever the hell I want. whenever I want. I am the king of my domain. This is MY life. If I wanna do it I'll do it. If I don't wanna do it. I won't!. In other words. I am my own رب! I am my own master!

And Allah is saying. No! I am your master! I gave you this life. I gave you this authority. I gave you these gifts of parents. I gave you these gifts of children. I honored you with intellect and free will. I gave you position and power. I gave you health and strength. I gave you all these things so you could recognize me and come closer to me because I am your master - and I am a master unlike any other master you will ever come to know of! Submit to me and I will show you what true happiness is!

Willingly and happily accepting our عبودیۃ and submitting to our master in sincere obedience and true worship, is the greatest challenge that you and I will ever face in this life. And the only way to overcome this challenge, is by recognizing and appreciating the love and mercy of our master; and by completing the relationship of ربوبیۃ by a willing submission and obedience to this magnificent master - because love engenders love!

If this incredible رب does so much for us disobedient and ungrateful slaves, what would he do for us if we actually did become willingly obedient to him?!

‏وَإِذْ تَأَذَّنَ رَبُّكُمْ لَىِٕن شَكَرْتُمْ لَأَزِيدَنَّكُمْ ۖ وَلَىِٕن كَفَرْتُمْ إِنَّ عَذَابِى لَشَدِيدٌۭ ‎

And when your ‏master said: "If you become grateful, I will give you more, but if you are ungrateful, then indeed my punishment is severe."

### Q&A

### If Our Relationship With Allah Is Of Master And Slave, Then What Is The Concept Of Freedom In Islam?

What is freedom? حریۃ . In Arabic freedom is very different from the definition of freedom in modern English. For instance, freedom in the pre-Islamic culture, the حر was in contradistinction to the slave. So a free person was someone who was not a عبد. They had the حر and the عبد. Now one of the amazing things about Islam is that it makes you عبد اللہ. Now if you're عبد اللہ - if you are the slave of Allah, then what does that make you in relationship to everything else? It makes you free! Because now your عبدیۃ - your obedience and servitude is only to Allah; it is not to anything or anyone else; and so it sets you completely free. So the حر- the Arabs say

الحُرُّ عَبْدٌ مَا طَمَع

The free man is a slave as long as he is covetous - as long as he is greedy; as long as he wants

والعَبْدُ حُرُّ مَا طَمَع

and the slave is a free man if he is content, which gets to the essence of the Arab understanding of freedom, before Islam. That freedom in reality is moral freedom. It's freedom from want - it is not freedom to! In our culture, freedom has been almost entirely defined by freedom "to" - Freedom to do whatever I want. Freedom to say whatever I want. Freedom to have whatever I want. To the Arabs it was freedom from - freedom from desires; freedom from wants; freedom from needs - that's how they perceived freedom.

So the Islamic concept of freedom, comes from that powerful paradigm. Enslave yourself to the one, to free yourself from everything. Or move away from the one, and become a slave of everything. And even enslaving oneself to Allah, is essentially just accepting the relationship of his ربوبیۃ - because we are slaves regardless. We are his property regardless of whether we accept it or not. So the concept of freedom is essentially just recognizing that relationship and draw towards him and in the process achieve complete freedom and happiness.

### If Allah is so merciful then why did Imam Hussain and his friends suffer in Karbala?

That is a matter of perspective. Did they really suffer?

What are the signs of someone who is in a state of suffering?

fear - regret - depression - excuses to escape

Example of the black slave John - played the racist card
