The more the heart understands revelation, the more we grow in our belief - which then nourishes and develops our character. And so character is nothing more than absorbing these divine attributes into the heart from revelation, and then reflecting those same divine attributes into the rest of Allah's creation. That is what character is! It is internalizing, developing, and training the heart in the light of these divine attributes; and then reflecting them in our interactions with Allah's creation.

### Interactions In Islam

That is أخلاق and that is Islam. Islam is all about Interactions. It is معاملات. There are 3 types of interactions in Islam

1. Interaction with Allah - حُقُوقُ اللَّہِ

1. Interaction with the self - حُقُوقُ النَّفْسِ

1. Interaction with the creation of Allah - حقوق العباد

Huqooq Allah

![](images/86/d8bc7f29-420b-4720-94a6-45362c80bbb2.jpg)

What right does Allah have over us? That we recognize his attributes in the heavens and the earth, and we come to love him, for everything he has done for us! And then out of that love, we willingly obey and serve him as faithful slaves - as عبد. That we worship him recognizing the fact that only he deserves to be worshiped! That is the right of the creator over his creation. What is the right of the self?

Huqooq Nafs

![](images/86/097a919e-1633-48a4-9509-d2ca569658fb.jpg)

To reflect on the attributes of Allah in the عالم, through the teachings of Quran. To learn the attributes, the qualities, the character of our master, and to then internalize and harvest those qualities within our own hearts to develop our character; our أخلاق. Because we have been created in the image of our master. We have been created to represent our master on this earth - which means we have to adopt the qualities and characteristics of our master! That is what a خلیفۃ is - a representative! You can't represent unless you become like the one you are representing!

So for instance, Allah is کریم. He is generous. Therefore it is the right of my soul that I train it to be generous. What is the greatest generosity that I can show myself? To protect it from sins, and put it to work in good deeds. Allah is الحق, he is truthful! it is the right of my soul that I train it in being truthful. With who? Truthful with myself. What is the greatest falsehood that the self is trapped in? This delusion of the reality of دنیا. Therefore I must train myself to wake up from my delusions about this world. I need to wake up to the reality that I am only here for a short period of time. Everything that I'm working for in the worldly sense will be left behind. I will be left with nothing other than the title of میت in this world when I die. My loved ones, the people that I am slaving for day and night, they will be the first to dump me in a grave and throw dirt in my face. So I better not sacrifice my afterlife for this temporary life. That's being truthful to the self. So the right of the self, is that I internalize the attributes of Allah, and train myself in those attributes in order to develop my character, so that I can realize the potential I was created with. I was created in the image of god, to act like God. And God is nothing other than just! This is why one of the divine names of Allah is المؤمن, which essentially means - the giver of tranquility. The one who brings peace - comes from the root امن which means peace.

Huqooq Ibaad

![](images/86/951440aa-8e7c-46e7-a2f8-04c4aed6c38b.jpg)

And finally there is the right of others. What are the right of others? What are حقوق العباد.

Right of Allah, was recognizing his divine attributes in creation, and submitting to him willingly and out of love in عبادۃ. Right of the self, was to internalize those divine attributes to build our character, aligning it with the character of Allah. To become like the creator! Once that character has developed, only then the final right of حقوق العباد comes into play, which is the idea of reflecting those divine attributes of Allah in interactions within his creation!

We trained ourselves to be generous with our own selves; now it's time to reflect that generosity on to others in our dealings with them. We trained ourselves to be truthful and sincere with our own selves. Now it's time to reflect that truthfulness. that صدق, that sincerity in our dealings with others. We've recognized how Allah is forgiving with us; how he is patient with us. We now have to reflect those attributes by being patient and forgiving towards others.

This is not an easy task! This is why one has to go through the training of fulfilling the previous 2 rights before he can fulfill this 3rd right! A man once came to the messenger of Allah and said.

یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ. اَصِلُ اَخِیہِ وَ یَقطَعُنِی ' وَ اُعطِیہِ وَ یَمنَعُنِی

Oh messenger of Allah, I give him but he withholds from me. I come to him but he cuts me off. What should I do? the Prophet replied, that is صلۃ الرحم. What you doing is the right thing! Continue meeting him even though he is cutting you off. That's Islam! This is Islam. And its hard work. Don't think this is an easy religion. But we want to be people that strive! We want to be people that honor ourselves, because Allah honored us when he said:

وَلَقَدْ كَرَّمْنَا بَنِىٓ آدَمَ وَحَمَلْنَهُمْ فِى ٱلْبَرِّ وَٱلْبَحْرِ

We have ennobled the children of Adam. We've made you leaders over the land and the oceans. We gave you a heart. We gave you an intellect. We nourish them with our revelation. We give you the ability to overcome the worst aspect of yourself; and even if you capitulate from time to time, we've given you the means to renew and restore your soul through توبۃ , through repentance. What a gift from Allah! So this is what we have to do, and we have to do it with أخلاص. with sincerity! Only for the sake of Allah. But it's not easy.

3 Dimensions Of Islam

![](images/86/0f665992-7d40-40ca-833b-ab1b223125cd.jpg)

So these are the 3 dimensions of Islam. The rights of Allah, the rights of the self and the rights of others. Recognizing the attributes of Allah in the heavens and the earth help us understand the rights of Allah (حُقُوقُ اللَّہِ). Internalizing those divine attributes to build character is the right of the soul (حُقُوقُ النَّفْسِ). Reflecting those divine attributes on to the creation of Allah is the right of others (حُقُوقُ الْعِبَاد). And because this last one is the most challenging of all, it cannot be accomplished unless and until the soul itself has been illuminated with the character of Allah. This is why Rasul Allah ﷺ said in a HADEES:

أتخذو من أخلاق اللہ

Adopt the character and the qualities of Allah!

There is a narration in which Rasul Allah once saw a man talking to Maulana Ali in a very disrespectful manner. In in return, Maulana Ali responded back to him with kindness. Despite his kindness, this man continued to act in a disrespectful and manner with him. Yet Maulana Ali kept responding with kindness over and over again. When the man left, the prophet asked him, یا علی! I saw this man talk to you in such a disrespectful manner, treat you in a horrible way - over and over again. Yet you kept showing him kindness and respect in return. Why is that? Maulana Ali replied: یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ If this man is not willing to give up his wretched and evil nature to interact with me, then why should I give up my good character to interact with him? SubhanAllah! What a beautiful statement! If this man is not willing to give up his wretched and evil nature to interact with me, then why should I give up my good character to interact with him? In other words, if this man continues to hold on to something ugly, why should I let go of something beautiful that I possess. That's the beauty of أخلاق.

This is why we follow the example of Rasul Allah ﷺ who according to Quran reach the pinnacle of اخلاق. Through his character, and nothing but his character, he purified the most barbaric and the most vile group of people that ever existed. Read the history of Islam. Understand the mentality of the people he was sent to reform. They were worst than animals. There was not a single virtue that existed within them. They would eat the flesh of elephants and crocodiles. When they ate a goat, they even boiled and ate its leather. They would burn their enemies alive. They would rip out their skin and wear it like a scarf. They would crack open their skull and use it as a cup to drink wine in. Their women would dye their clothes in the blood of their enemies. Not only would they kill the enemy. they would tie that persons camel to his grave and starve it to death. Sons would marry their mothers; Daughters were buried alive. They would use the children of their enemies for target practice. Even their prayers were were barbaric.

یا ربِّ فَلا تَجعَل حَیَاتی مَذِلَّۃُ ولا مَوتَتِی بَینَ النِّسآءِ نوائِحِ

ولاکن قَتیلاً تَمزَقُ الطَّیرِ حَولَہُ . و تَشرُبُ غِربانُ الدَّم من جوارح

Oh lord, do not disgrace me in my life by letting me die on a bed between grieving women
Give me a death, where birds are tearing out my flesh and crows are drinking the fresh blood gushing out of my limbs.

This was the state of the Arabs when Rasul Allah came between them! This is the group he was sent to reform! And look what he did! He converted these people in to رحمۃ اللہ علیہ. We don't even mention their names without saying رحمۃ اللہ علیہ- May the mercy of Allah be with them. This is why on the day of غدیر when Rasul Allah asked:

اَلَسْتُ اَوْلَاکُمْ مِنْ اَنْفُسِکُمْ؟

Am I not more beloved to you than your own souls? What did they reply.

بلیٰ یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ

Indeed you are oh prophet of Allah

كنا الحيوانات! كُنتُ جعلنا البشر

We were animals! You made us human beings.

So how did he do that? Through his impeccable اخلاق. By internalizing the divine attributes of his creator, by internalizing them to develop an immaculate character, and finally reflecting those very same attributes in creation - with such intensity, that the creator himself acknowledged:

‏وَإِنَّكَ لَعَلَىٰ خُلُقٍ عَظِيمٍۢ

Indeed! No doubt about it, You have achieved the utmost, and the most exalted level of character!

## The Womb Of Afterlife

Now that we understand these meanings of رب and الرحمن and الرحیم, I want to share with you a beautiful spiritual representation that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has created for us through the example of the the womb. To quickly reiterate, a رب is a combination of 6 things. In classical Arabic

الرَبُّ ھُوَ المَالِکُ وَ السَّیِّد وَ المُرَبِّی وَ المُرْشَدُ وَ المُنعِمُ وَ القَیِّمُ

He is an owner and the one with complete authority over his property. He is the one who nurtures his creation, guides it, benefits it by bestowing generous and undeserving gifts upon it; and he is the one who maintains it. That is who Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is. That's his primary role with his creation. All his other attributes center around and stem from his ربوبیۃ. So if someone asks, "Who is Allah?", the answer is that "Allah is رب". Now as for his nature, his nature is الرحمن and الرحیم. The manner in which he carries out the functions of his ربوبیۃ is through intense and consistent love, compassion and mercy.

### Womb is the first master

So now that we understand these concepts, I want to share this example that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has created for us through the womb. We can understand a lot about our master's plan for us, by studying the relationship of the womb with its fetus. Because the womb is the رب of the fetus and it demonstrates the exact same principles of رحمنیۃ and رحیمیۃ with it. There is a HADEES in which Rasul Allah ﷺ said:

قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّہِ صَلَّی اللَّہُ عَلَیْہِ وَ آلِہِ وَ سَلَّمَ

> الدُّنْیا رَحْمُ الآخِرَۃ
>
> This world is a womb for the afterlife

In other words, the story of our lives in this world, tells a tale about the kind of life we're going to have in the afterlife. Because this world (this دنیا) that we're living in, is the womb and we are the fetus in that womb. Just as the brief and temporary residence in a womb is preparation for this worldly life; our stay in this temporal world is nothing more than a duration of preparation for the after life.

If you look at the رحم - the womb, you'll see that all 6 principles of ربوبیۃ apply. It has ownership and authority over the embryo. It nurtures the embryo. It helps it grow and develop into a fetus. So the womb مالک, it is سید and it is also مربی. Then we see that the womb bestows priceless gifts on the fetus through the development of its eyes, its nose, ears, hands and feet; the heart, the intellect and all of its internal and external organs. So the womb is also the منعم. We also know the womb manages the enter development process of the embryo from its conception until the time it is fully developed. So the womb is also a قیم. In other words, the womb acts as the very first physical رب that we come into contact with; and the very first act this design demonstrates, is the act of ربوبیۃ by carrying out the 6 acts of:

مَالِکُ وَ السَّیِّد وَ المُرَبِّی وَ المُرْشَدُ وَ المُنعِمُ وَ القَیِّمُ

Now what happens once the baby is delivered into this world? Who is the baby handed over to? Its loving mother who becomes the next immediate رب of that baby - not the father! the mother. It's the mother that becomes the next رب why? Because she feeds it. So there is a transference of ربوبیۃ that takes place. The mother now takes ownership of the baby; the mother now takes authority over it; the mother takes on the responsibility of nurturing it; helping it grow and develop; teaching and guiding it, setting it to purpose. Show shower gifts and blessings upon it; She becomes the one who maintains that baby's sustenance in its various forms - physical, emotional, psychological. So we can see that our mothers becomes the next immediate رب , right after the ربوبیۃ of the womb (the رحم) is complete. Father is also a رب , but his ربوبیۃ gets executed through the mother, you see?

### Mother

Now let's study this HADEES to better understand the underlying principles at work. Rasul Allah ﷺ said: اَلدُّنْیَا رَحِمُ اَلْاَخِرَۃِ. This temporal world is the **womb** of the after life. So let's examine the fetus in the womb of its mother. Is it the womb actually doing all the work, or is it the mother doing all the work behind the scenes and letting the womb act as an intermediary between herself and her child? We know it's always the mother. The mother is the one taking care of the baby's needs. She is the one eating the right things; making sure she doesn't bump into things so that her baby doesn't get hurt. She's the one getting all the ultra sounds done, taking the painful but necessary blood tests, injections. She is the one suffering for her baby. We all understand that it is the mother all along doing the hard work. But from the perspective of the fetus, who is doing all the work? The womb! Not the mother. The fetus has absolutely no knowledge of the mother. All it knows, all it recognizes; all it understands, is the womb. If I asked the fetus, who do you think is feeding you? nurturing you? Taking care of you? Sustaining you? Doing all the work that is necessary for your health and development, what will the fetus reply? The womb! The fetus has absolutely no knowledge of the existence of its mother, you see? What mother? Where's the mother. I don't see a mother. All that exists for the fetus, is this womb. There is no mother. It's just cause an effect. When I say to this fetus, No it's not the womb, it's the mother. She is the one feeding you; she is the one taking care of you; this womb is only an intermediary, the fetus says, what are you talking about? Its the umbilical chord that's feeding me. That's what gets me this blood that is carrying nutrients. I get fed by it - **that** is what is responsible for my development. There is no mother!

I explain, No listen! There is a mother! She is all around you. You are within the love, the care, the concern and the mercy of your mother. This womb wouldn't exist if she didn't exist. And this womb that you're in - it's temporary. This is not the world you were created for. This world is filthy; it is dark; it is immensely constricted. There is an amazing and a beautiful world that exists outside this womb that has been created just for you. That world is so magnificent that you cannot even imagine its glory right now within this womb. This womb is filth compared that that world! If you think this womb is kind and merciful to you, let me tell you... it doesn't even compare to the love and mercy of your mother. Once you are delivered in that next world, your mother will take such incredible care of you. She will shower you with so much love.

The fetus says, what are you talking about? I'm REALLY REALLY happy here. Everything I need is being taken care of for me - right here. Why do I need to have another world? I don't know why you keep talking to me about a mother and another world, an afterlife. There is no mother. There is no world outside of this womb. You're crazy! Show me!!. Show me this world that you talk about right now!! I wanna see it. Prove to me that there is another world. Prove to me that there is actually a mother. I am not not going to believe you, unless and until I see that world and that mother.

I say, but I can't show it to you right now. But I promise - when you transition out of this world you'll see both of them. Oh so now I'm supposed to trust you? I'm supposed to trust your intellect? your belief system? What if you're wrong?

This is us with Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. This is so many of us denying the existence of the mother living in the comforts of the womb of this دنیا.

I explain, look! you have been given these eyes, but there is so little to see here! You've been given these ears, but there's so little to hear. You've been given taste buds, but there is so little to taste here. You have a nose with the ability to smell, but there isn't a single fragrance here. You have arms and feet; you have all of these amazing gifts; but you cannot benefit from them within this womb. Think about it! These are of no use to you inside this womb - which means there must be another world out there. A world where you will be able to use and benefit from these gifts. Your only purpose in the womb; in this world; is to draw your sustenance from your mother and develop these organs. You're here so that you can **prepare** them for the next world, where you will actually be using them; where you will actually benefit from them. But the fetus doesn't understand that. We don't understand that. To the fetus, the concept of another world, and the existence of a mother is ridiculous, just as the concept of a God and an afterlife is absurd to so many of us.

### Afterlife

So now what happens? This fetus lives in its ignorance and its arrogance, until 9 months complete. Now what happens? Contractions begin. The fetus starts panicking. What's going on here? What's happening? This is what was promised to you! I told you this day would come. I told you this world is temporary. So now with those contractions, that beautiful world of the fetus; the world it was living happily and comfortably in; it start falling apart all around it? Isn't this exactly the description of the day of judgment?

إِذَا زُلْزِلَتِ ٱلْأَرْضُ زِلْزَالَهَا

When the earth is shaken with earthquakes

وَأَخْرَجَتِ ٱلْأَرْضُ أَثْقَالَهَا

And the earth discharges its burdens

وَقَالَ ٱلْإِنسَنُ مَا لَهَا

And man says, "What is wrong with it?" -

يَوْمَئِذٍۢ تُحَدِّثُ أَخْبَارَهَا. بِأَنَّ رَبَّكَ أَوْحَىٰ لَهَا

On that Day, it will report the news of which your master has commanded it.

يَوْمَئِذٍۢ يَصْدُرُ ٱلنَّاسُ أَشْتَاتًۭا لِّيُرَوْا۟ أَعْمَلَهُمْ

On that day, people will separate out, and they will be shown the results of their deeds.

فَمَن يَعْمَلْ مِثْقَالَ ذَرَّةٍ خَيْرًۭا. يَرَهُ

So whoever did an atom's weight of good will see it,

وَمَن يَعْمَلْ مِثْقَالَ ذَرَّةٍۢ شَرًّۭا. يَرَهُ

And whoever did an atom's weight of evil will see it.

So the world of this fetus now comes to an end, and it is forced out of it. It is pushed out of it against its will. Does the fetus have a choice in whether it wants to stay in the womb or not? No! Whether it wants to or not, once its time is up, it is pushed out into the next world. It dies in the womb, in order to be born again in the next world, you see? That's what the word انتقال means. It comes from نقل which means transference. It also means a copy or a replica - why? Because the next world is going to be a copy; it is going to be a replica of the first. If you did good in that world, you will find good in this next world. If you did evil in that world, you will face evil in the next world. If the fetus has not properly developed its eyes, its ears, its brain, its limbs in the womb of its mother, it is going to suffer the consequences in this after life of دنیا.

So now this temporal world of the fetus is destroyed, and it comes into this huge world that he was informed about; this world that was promised in the womb. He is put into this world, this afterlife that he chose **not** to believe in. And then, as soon as it is out of the womb - who is that baby handed to? The mother! The mother that he said did not exist; the mother he denied his entire life in the womb.

So you can see that the fetus goes through that same confusion of seen and unseen, while it's in the womb of the mother; that so many of us go through in this life. The fetus that accepts the ربوبیۃ of its mother **through** the womb, comes out fully developed; and is now capable of enjoying all the benefits that its after life in this physical world. But the fetus that rejected the ربوبیۃ of the womb, comes out under developed; blind, deaf, mute, physically challenged; incapable of benefiting from the joys and pleasures that this world has to offer. We are all going through the exact same process in life! Why? Because الدُّنْیَا رَحِمُ الْآخِرَۃِ.

Now we have our رب telling us, look! Follow the شریعۃ - keep yourself clean, keep yourself pure. Do your صلوۃ, complete your fasts; pay your زکوۃ. Fulfill your obligation of حج. What do we say? Why? How is this going to help me? Well, it's not going to help you here. The obligations of شریعۃ is not meant for this world. It's the sustenance for the development of our spiritual limbs; are spiritual organs, that are going to help you live in the next life. That's where we will benefit from these fully developed spiritual organs. All we're doing in this دنیا, is developing them in order to use them in the next world - which is جنۃ. A world that is incredibly beautiful. A world that is governed by a رب who is intensely merciful; who is constantly merciful. The شریعۃ is the umbilical cord, providing the sustenance for the development of our spiritual being. And whether we believe it or not, whether we want to or not, the next world is inevitable.

الْجَنَّۃُ حَقٌّ وَالنَّارُ حَقٌّ وَالْمَوْتُ حَقٌّ وَالْبَعْثُ حَقٌّ وَالْمِیزَانُ حَقٌّ وَالصِّرَاطُ حَقٌّ

Paradise is a reality, hellfire is a reality, death is a reality, rising from the graves is a reality, the scales in which our actions will be weighed is a reality, the straight path is a reality.

وَاَنَّ السَّاعَۃَ آتِیَۃٌ لَا رَیْبَ فِیھَا

and that appointed moment is coming - there is absolutely no doubt about it. That is the world in which justice will be exacted. Every incident, for you which we said - where is justice? On that day we will all see justice. The innocent people that suffered momentarily in this world, their momentary suffering will will be balanced out with never-ending gifts and pleasures. The one who caused those sufferings for fleeting gains and pleasures, their pleasures will be counter balanced with grievous pain and ever lasting torment.

إِذَا ٱلشَّمْسُ كُوِّرَت

وَإِذَا ٱلنُّجُومُ ٱنكَدَرَتْ

وَإِذَا ٱلْجِبَالُ سُيِّرَتْ

وَإِذَا ٱلْعِشَارُ عُطِّلَتْ

وَإِذَا ٱلْوُحُوشُ حُشِرَتْ

‏وَإِذَا ٱلْبِحَارُ سُجِّرَتْ

These are all descriptions of the day of judgment. When this universe will be destroyed.

وَإِذَا ٱلنُّفُوسُ زُوِّجَتْ

On that day when souls are paired together. When you finally meet those loved ones who left you behind in this world.

وَإِذَا ٱلْمَوْءُۥدَةُ سُئِلَتْ. بِأَىِّ ذَنۢبٍۢ قُتِلَتْ

And when that little girl who was burred alive will be asked. For what sin was she killed? This is the day where Allah will exact justice. That is is the day of justice. NOT this world that we're living in.

وَإِذَا ٱلصُّحُفُ نُشِرَتْ

And when books of deeds will be made public!.

وَإِذَا ٱلسَّمَآءُ كُشِطَتْ

And when the sky is stripped away

وَإِذَا ٱلْجَحِيمُ سُعِّرَتْ

And when the hellfire is set ablaze

وَإِذَا ٱلْجَنَّةُ أُزْلِفَتْ

And when Paradise is brought near,

عَلِمَتْ نَفْسٌۭ مَّآ أَحْضَرَتْ

Every soul will then know, what it has brought with it. All good will be rewarded on that day, and all bad will be punished.

### The Destitute

It has been narrated by the prophet ﷺ. that there will be a man on the day of judgement. All this man ever did was lend money to other people. He was a money lender - and such people are usually black-hearted people. But when **this **man sent out his servants to collect the money that people owed; if the person was having a hard time returning back the money - if that's what the servant would return and tell him, he would say - forget about it. So on the day of judgement this man comes before Allah and he does not have any good deeds to his name, except that he used to do that. And Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى displays this man to everyone and says. Will I leave him to be more generous than me? He used to forgive people; and do you think I am not going to forgive today?

So on this day every single good deed will be balanced with reward from an immensely beneficent, loving and compassionate master. All our delusions of grandeur in this world will be gone. On that day the مفلس and the غنی - the destitute and the affluent will be separated. Rasul Allah ﷺ once asked his companions.

مَنِ الْمُفْلِسُ؟. Who is the مفلس, who is the destitute? They said

المُفلِسُ . مَن لا دِینارُ ولا دَرھَمُ عِندَنَا یا رسول اللہ

The destitute is the one who does not have any درھم or دینار to his name. The prophet said No! The greatest destitute of my امۃ on the day of judgment.

أِنَّ المُفلِسَ مِن أُمَّتِی. مَن یَأتِی یَومَ القِیَامَۃِ بِصلاۃٍ. وَصِیَامٍ. وَ زَکَاۃٍ.

The greatest destitute of my امۃ on the day of judgment will be the one who comes with all his prayers, and all his fasts, and all his زکوۃ and

وَیأتِی قَدْ شَتَمْ ھذا. وَقُذِفَ ھٰذا. وَأَکَلَ مَالَ ھٰذا. وَسَفَکَ الدَّمَّ ھذا. وَضَرَبَ ھذا

But in addition to all those deeds, he cheated this one, he lied to this one, he usurped the wealth of this one, he spilled the blood of this one, he struck this one.

فَیُعطٰی ھٰذا مِن حَسَنَاتِہِ وَ ھٰذا مِن حَسَنَاتِہِ

And so his good deeds will be taken from his account and put into the account of this one and this one and this one.

فَأِن فَنَیتُ حَسَنَاتُہُ قبَلَ أَنْ یُقضٰی مَا عَلیہ.

And when his good deeds run out, before justice is fully served.

أَخِذَ مِن خَطَایَاھُم. فَطُرِحَت عَلَیہِ.

The sins and faults of the remaining victims will be taken from their accounts, and added to **his** account

ثُمَّ طُرِحَ فِی النَّار

And so despite living an entire life full of worship, prayers, fasts, زکوۃ; because of his injustices with other people, this man will have to give up all his good deeds, and take on all the horrible sins of his victims, and finally be thrown into hellfire. This person, he said, will be the greatest destitute of my امۃ.

. نَعُوذُ بِاللَّہِ مِنْ ذَالِکَ

May Allah protect us all from being among these destitute on the day of judgment.

## Allah - Being vs Attributes

Now that you understand the linguistic meaning of the basic terms, it's time to start with the بسم اللہ which is the first verse of سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ.

بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ

Arabs had this way of shortening commonly repeated statements. So if they wanted to refer to بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ they'd refer to it by بسم اللہ. To refer to لَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّۃَ اِلَّا بِاللَّہِ they called it the حوقلا. Similarly they used حمدللہ for الحمد للہ رب العلمین, and so on.

### Being vs Attributes

If you look at the بسم اللہ, from a grammatical point of view, you'll see that this statement has a noun, and two adjectives, right? The word Allah is a noun representing an entity, a being, and then you have two attributes of رحمۃ that are mentioned, describing the nature of Allah, which are الرحمن and الرحیم.

So let's talk about this distinction between Allah's name and his attributes; which is essentially the distinction between the concepts of noun and adjectives. Attributes are the tools provided by language, for us to understand something. In other words, it is through the combination of the two (noun and adjectives; the name and the attributes) that we understand an entity, right?

If you want to gain a complete understanding of an entity, you need to understand its attributes. In Arabic, this is called ذات and صفات; being and its attributes. So for instance, we have a soul and we have a body, which constitute our ذات - our being. But then soul has certain spiritual attributes - kind, brave, intelligent, generous or weak, coward, dumb. In the same way the body has its own set of physical attributes - fat, thin, black, brown white, tall, short. Once we understand these spiritual and physical attributes describing the soul or the body, we essentially recognize the nature of their being, right? The union of the two, provide the identity and a complete description of that identity, enabling our intellects to understand the nature of that being. But the thing is, attributes cannot exist without the being. It is the ذات that gives existence to the صفات. Attributes can only exist after the existence of the being itself. If there is no اللہ there cannot be الرحمن and الرحیم. So the name Allah is greater than الرحمن and الرحیم on the basis of its precedence.

![](images/152/92005ff6-ab51-4acb-a733-01fbbe1e5f0e.jpg)

So now if we start breaking down this statement of the بسم اللہ the term Allah points to his ذات - a being. He is a deity named Allah. Allah is the title of his essence.

إِنَّنِىٓ أَنَا ٱللَّهُ لَآ إِلَهَ إِلَّآ أَنَا۠

No doubt, I am Allah; and there is none worthy of worship and obedience except me. Now this noun Allah has adjectives, which are his 99 صفات; his attributes, describing the nature of Allah. His various manifestations in his interactions with creation. So now you have,

الرحمن، الرحيم، الملك، القدوس، السلام، المؤمن، المهيمن، العزيز، الجبار، المتكبر، الخالق، البارئ، المصور، الغفَّار، القهَّار، الوهَّاب ، الرزَّاق ، الفتَّاح ، العليم، القابض، الباسط، الخافض، الرافع، المعز، المذل، السميع، البصير، الحكم، الحکیم، العادل، اللطيف، الحلیم، الخبير، العظيم، الغفور، الشكور، العلي، الكبير، الحفيظ، الحسيب، الكريم، المقیم، الرقیب، الجلیل، المجيب، الواسع، الودود، المجيد، الباعث، الشهيد، الوكيل، القوى، المتين، الولي، الحميد، المحصى، المبدئ ، المعيد، المحيي، المميت، الحي، القيوم، الواجد، الماجد، الواحد، الصمد، القادر، المقتدر، المقدِّم، المؤخر، الأول، الآخر، الظاهر، الباطن، الوالي، المتعال، البِر، التواب، المنتقم، العفو، الرؤوف، اَلْمَالِكُ الْمَلِكُ، ذُو الْجَلَالِ وَ الْإِكْرَامِ، المقسط، الجامع، الغني، المغني، المانع، الضار، النافع، النور، الهادي، البديع، الباقي، الوارث، الرشيد، الصبور ، الحنان، المنان، الدیان، البرھان

These are all attributes that describe the nature of Allah. To use the example of the prism and light that I stated in one of the previous sessions, you can think of Allah as one pure light - اللَّہُ نُورُ السَّمَوَاتِ وَ الْاَرْضِ. When this pure light of the name Allah comes into this physical realm, his divine attributes refract within creation. So we get all of these different manifestations of Allah's essence, based on his interaction with his creation. So in one instance we see that Allah is کریم (the generous). And in another instance he is جبار (the compeller); in another instance he is قھار (the subduer). In another instance he is عزیز (the powerful). In another instance he is مُزل (the one who humiliates and humbles). In another he is معز (the one who bestows honor). His essence manifests through all these different attributes within creation. This world, then becomes a theater for the divine manifestations of Allah's attributes, enabling us to know Allah. Not directly through his essence, but through the refraction of his attributes in his creation.

### Transcendence vs immanence

This is why it is said:

لَا تُفَکِّرْ فِی اللَّہِ . وَلَاکِنْ تَفَکَّرْ فِی خَلْقِ اللَّہِ

Do not reflect upon Allah, but rather reflect upon the creations of Allah. Why? Because it is creation, nature, that manifests all his attributes for us. But then Allah is infinite, and so are his attributes. This is why when we attempt to understand Allah we get bewildered by his attributes; because in a sense, we cannot know what God is, any more than we can know what god isn't. Because when we speak about Allah, we are speaking about the infinite creator of the heavens and the earth. And the word infinite means without limit - لا حُدودَ لہ.

Now the word in Arabic for definition is the same word that we use for limitation, or a boundary الحد. A definition is what defines or gives finiteness to a concept. It limits it; it demarcates it; it puts it into perspective. So when we talk about God we cannot define God. Because to define god would mean to give him a حد; a boundary; a limit - which is impossible you see? This is why scholars say that all of عقیدۃ (creed) can be reduced to two statements:

لیس کمثلہ شئی

He is unlike anything

لا یُشْبِحُ ذاتُہُ الذَّوات ولا مُتَعَاطِلَۃُ الصِّفات

That his essence is not like any other essence and his attributes are not any way metaphorical. He is

الغَنیُ عَنِ الجَمِیعُ و الجمیعُ مفتَقِرٌ الیہ

He has no need of anyone, and everyone is in need of him. This statement actually negates **need **for Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى, and it negates for us **independence**.

![](images/152/12b65610-07b1-45a5-be3a-0ebb449a5e2f.jpg)

Now here's where we get into another central problem to religion, and that is the problem of transcendence and immanence. Transcendence is when we speak about God without anything, any reference in the world that can guide us to god. There is nothing in the world for which we can say that is god

تَعَالَی اللَّہُ عَنْ مَا یَصِفُونَ

Allah is above and beyond anything they ascribe to. So when we talk about transcendence, we're talking about Allah being above and beyond everything that we know of - سُبحانک which is called تنزیح in Arabic. The word تنزیح means transcendence. It comes from نزح which means to purify; to get rid of filth; to transcend something. So Allah is transcendent; but then pure transcendence has a lot of problems. Because he becomes the God of the scientist. Like Einstein believed in a god, but he did not believe in a personal god. He believed in a Transcendent god. The god he believed in was the God of the deist, who has nothing to do with his creation, and that's a major problem.

Then the other problem comes with what is called immanence. The idea of god being in the world. For example like in the Hindu tradition, the Brahman class do not really believe in the idols. But it's kind of a little trick to help all the stupid people out there, that still need to evolve. There is an understanding in their tradition that the lower classes, or the caste systems, which they call the VERNA. If you are in a lesser "varna", you cannot understand the BRAHMA. Because he has gone through several lifetimes to get to the ability to perceive. Only he can perceive; and only he can understand the God Head, which they call the Brahma. So Brahma understands the god that has no attribute, and they call that the NIRGOONA. But they have this idea that the lower class people need to **see **something in order to understand it, and so they gave them all these idols, who they really knew were not gods. But it helps the common people understand. This is the problem of immanence in religion, which is creating likeness. The way Christianity deals with this problem is obviously through Jesus. Because Jesus becomes the Incarnation of god. God enters into the flesh, and this is what in our tradition we call حُلُول , where god enters the flesh. And some of the deviant Sufi claimed this state. Also as you know this group called نصیری claimed that for Maulana Ali. It also happened for اِمَامُ جَعْفَرٍ الصَّادِقِ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ.

So how do we understand this in our religion? What is the relationship between transcendence and immanence (تنزیح and تجرید)? Because you have to have access to your master. If you have a completely transcendent god, he becomes a cold distant uncaring, unloving god. On the other hand if you give god the attributes of creation you have a whole other problem. And that's what gives birth to concepts of anthropomorphism - falling into the idea that god is petulant; that god has emotions; that god has anger; that he has pleasure in any anthropomorphic way. And this is also a problem in some of the Muslim understandings. There are Muslims who think that when these words are used in HADEES or the Quran.....

الْمَعْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ

The ones who faced the anger of Allah, they think that Allah's anger is like OUR anger. That Allah becomes angry in an emotional sense; and that

تَعَالَی اللَّہُ عَنْ مَا یَصِفُونَ

Allah is above and beyond anything the ascribed to him. But we do believe that غضب has a meaning. We believe that رضی has a meaning. We believe that these words that Allah used in the Quran

رضی اللہ عنہ و رضو عنھم

They were pleased with Allah and Allah was pleased with them, but don't think **your** pleasure with Allah, is the same as Allah's pleasure with you. Don't think those two words mean the same thing in the context that they are being used. Each means a specific thing in relation to that thing that it is predicating. So when we talk about the attributes of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى we have to be extremely careful. Because we in a sense can only say about Allah, what Allah has said about himself.

But then now we enter into another problem, which is the interpretive problem. Because even though Allah is telling us things about himself, he is self-disclosing to us; but he is self-disclosing through language. And language is filtered through the human mind. And the human mind has the capacity to interpret what is being said to it in many many different ways. And this is why the prophet ﷺ is the purest filter. He is still a filter. The prophet ﷺ is a human filter. But he is the purest filter, and this is why the prophets have a capacity that other human beings do not have. And that is that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has chosen them to be these filters for revelation. And that is why the best understanding of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is the understanding that the prophet ﷺ articulates to us..... And this is why he said

اللھم لا نُحثِنَ الثَّناءَ عَلَیکَ..... أَنتَ کَما أِثنَیتَ عَلَی نَفسِکَ

We are not able to praise you. You are, as you praised yourself. In other words I cannot truly praise you, because one of the reasons that we praise is because of our knowledge, of the praiseworthy. If you don't know the qualities of someone or something, you cannot really praise them. And this is why in Allah's self disclosure to us, he is describing for us the reasons why he is the praiseworthy; because he is الحمید. He has told us about the praiseworthy attributes that he has. This is how understanding the attributes of Allah enables us to know Allah. Now these attributes are known through human experience, and this is where you get into the problem of immanence. That there has to be some correlation between what we understand about Allah, and what we understand about ourselves, or what we see in the world; otherwise we could not know Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. Its not possible. And so in dealing with the attributes of Allah, you are still working with things in the world. So when we say Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is قدیم, and قدیم is a problematic word, in that it is a technical word. It is not found in the Quran, it is not an attribute of Allah. It was used as a technical term. قِدَم is a سلبی attribute because it negates حدث; it negates occurrence. It negates the fact that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى حَدَثَ he came into existence. NO! The idea of قِدَم is that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is, as he always was; and he will always continue to be the same without any change, or any time factor. And this is where pre-eternity is the best we can do in English. When we say pre-eternity, I don't even know what that means. I really don't! It is a word that is the best that we have come up with as a way of describing his existence.

So we run into all these problems when trying to understand Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. This is also why Maulana Ali (AS) defined the recognition of Allah as:

الْعَجْزُ عَنْ دَرْكِ الْإِدْرَاكِ إِدْرَاكٌ . والبحث عن ذاتِهِ كُفرُ وإشراك

The inability to recognize Allah **is** recognizing him; and any attempt to discuss his essence is شرک. It will inevitably result in assigning partners with him. Accepting our inability to know Allah **is** recognizing him. This is why the most essential act of worship in Islam is سجدۃ - putting our intellects on the ground, and surrendering its capacity to know Allah.

### In-comparability Of Allah

Now Quran gives us an incredibly concise statement to understand how to understand God. The way in which we understand this world is through "things". The Arabic word for a thing, is شئی. The word شئی comes from شاء which is the "will" of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. Every "thing", every "being" that exists in this world has its own individual sets of attributes. All of which point to the منشاء (the will and purpose) of their creator. But when it comes to Allah himself, there is no "thing", no شئی that can be compared to him in any way shape or form. So the Quran beautifully explains this concept to us through one single word in the ayah.

لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ

No matter what you do, no matter how high your imaginations fly; no matter how deep your thoughts dive, there absolutely no way you can ever understand Allah, because there is no "thing " in existence (physical or spiritual) that is like Allah.

لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ There is absolutely nothing you can compare me to. SubhanAllah.

Now I want you to take a moment and think about this paradox. He created this magnificent universe all around us - every single entity reflecting his divine attributes for us. Why? So that we could gain a semblance of understanding of his powers, his glory, his majesty. And then once we do manage to get some feeble understanding of his attributes in creation, he says: لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ . Whatever little you now think you know about me - understand that I am not like it. I am way beyond it. And what's so amazing is that he does this linguistically through the use of one single word كَمِثْلِهِ. This is not a word that is commonly used - not even in Ancient Arabia. This is a word used only in بلاغۃ in the eloquence of the Quran.

From a grammatical stand point the word کمثلہ is actually 2 words. There is the word کَ and then there is مثلہ. These are both words used for comparison; but they are never fused together in this manner. So let's try to understand the subtly in the use of this word کمثلہ.

### Comparing Attributes

In Arabic when you want to compare someone in attributes, you add the letter کَ. So for example, if I want to compare the bravery of زید to a lion; if I want to say زید is brave like a lion, I will have to use the letter کَ to do that. I'll say

زیدٌ کل أسدِ

I'm saying زید is like a lion. What am I comparing here? I'm comparing the attribute of bravery of a lion with زید therefore I'm using the letter کَ

### Comparing Essence

But when I need to establish a comparison in the essence, I cannot use the letter کَ. Grammatically that would be wrong. I will have to use the word مثل. So for example I can say:

اَلشِّبْلُ مِثْلُ الْاَسَدِ

A cub is similar to a lion. The son of a lion is like the lion. Here I'm using the word مثل because I am not comparing in attributes; I am comparing the essence of two entities.
This is a rule of the Arabic language when establishing comparisons. Both words کَ and مثل are used very precisely. We find this distinction all over Arabic poetry. So for example this poet is praising the generosity of a king and comparing it to an ocean. Generosity is an attribute, so he says:

کل بحرِ یَقذِفُ لِلقریبِ لآلیۃ جوداً و یبَعَثُ لِلبَعیدِ سحائِبا

He says, the king is generous like the ocean. When I move closer to him he gives me pearls; and when I move away from him he sends clouds filled with rain. Here he uses the word کَ because he is comparing the attribute of generosity. But when the being or the entity needs to be compared, the word مثل has to be used.

أنا السُّروجِیُّ وَ ھذا وَلدی و الشِّبلُ فی الِمخبَرِ مِثلُ الاسد

I am a lion and this is my son. No doubt the son of a lion is like the lion. So now because the comparison is in being, the poet uses the word مثل.

![](images/152/5b739499-b576-4bf4-b91f-f4f906ded16a.jpg)

I hope you all clearly understand this subtly, because that's what makes this ayah so beautiful. Allah says.

لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ

To compare himself Allah fuses both the کَ and the مثل together to say کمثلہ. Why is that? We learnt that کَ is used for attributes and مثل is used for the being itself. So what Allah is saying is that whether you try to compare me to the **attributes** of creation - کَ ; or if you try to compare me to the **essence **of creation - مثل; in both cases you will fail. Because لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ There is nothing that exists in attribute nor in essence that you can compare me with. SubhanAllah! Can you appreciate the beauty of this word?

- کَ . there is nothing comparable to me in attributes

- مثلہ- there is nothing comparable to me in being

- کمثلہ- there is nothing comparable to me in attributes or in being

- لَيْسَ كَمِثْلِهِ شَىْءٌۭ there is no thing (whether physical, metaphysical, spiritual, conceptual, theoretical, practical), there is simply no شئی that you can use to create a comparison or a likeness for me - not in attributes, not in its essence. SubhanAllah!

So now that we understand this concept, the بسم اللہ condenses all the attributes of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى into two primary attributes - الرحمن and الرحیم. In other words, the two attributes that will always be present in each and every action of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى are the attributes of رحمۃ - intense mercy and consistent mercy. So when we say بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ - we are remembering the essence of Allah, manifesting for us through his two primary attributes of رحمۃ, which implodes all his remaining attributes. In other words, regardless of the way in which Allah is interacting with us right now, his رحمۃ is present in that manifestation. Even if he is manifesting in my life right this moment as جبار or قھار, his love, care, compassion, concern and mercy is present in his interaction with me.

InshaAllah we'll move this conversation forward in the next session.

## The ISM Of Allah

### Noun or Name

In the previous session we talked about the transcendence and imminence of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. We learnt that in one way he is immensely distant out of his loftiness; but at the same instant he is extremely close to our hearts and our consciousness. We also looked at the distinction between Allah and his attributes, and how he is incomparable by both - لیس کمثلہ شئی.

So now that we've covered the noun Allah, and his adjectives الرحمن and الرحیم let's talk about أسم. The word اسم in Arabic means two things. It means a "noun" as in the grammatical term, and it also means a name; because each and every noun has a name. So the Arabs used it interchangeably.

From the Quranic perspective, this concept of name is extremely important, because it is the "names", the اسماء that elevated Adam AS over the angels, and made him worthy of their prostration. Also, these names were something that were taught to Adam AS directly by Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

وَعَلَّمَ آدَمَ ٱلْأَسْمَآءَ كُلَّهَا

Allah taught Adam all the names. So the concept of name is extremely important in the human psyche. Because we are بنی آدم. We are the children of Adam.

Now what is name? A name is the ability of symbolic thought. That is the essence of a name. Naming something, is taking a symbol, which is linguistic in nature; and then applying it to something which is either material, empirical, conceptual, or theoretical. Those are the entities that are "named". Or a name is something that can be used as a means of abstraction, in order to create separation in things. So if you have a broad category named "cars", you can further break them down by "Mercedes, Toyota, Audi, BMW" etc. See animals cannot do that. Animals cannot use symbolic language.

It has been agreed by the Arab scholars. that human beings have basically three dominant modes of symbolic language. We have linguistics which is the highest form, and then we have mathematics, which believe it or not, actually comes under linguistics. It is really the 20th century, and the western civilization, that elevated mathematics over language. Language has taken a secondary place today, because people don't understand the power of language - especially the Arabic language. This is why even Muslims today try to place mathematics over the linguistics of Quran, when trying to prove its miracle. No doubt Quran is mathematically perfect, and I've shown you many examples of that. But the fact of the matter is, that what makes Quran a miracle is not its mathematical perfection, but rather its linguistic perfection.

And this need to prove the Quran mathematically comes from this feeling of inferiority before the God of modern science - which is mathematics. It comes from Newton and Einstein and all these other people who elevated mathematics, and said that it is the only real way of understanding reality. and the reason they say that is because mathematics is precise and exact. But then these people did not study classical Arabic! Because Arabic is precise and exact! In fact it is precise and exact to a degree that mathematics cannot come close to. This is why the miracle of Quran, the اعجاز of Quran is not in its mathematics; it is in its linguistic perfection and eloquence. Quran is a linguistic miracle; and the recognition of this, comes from our ability to recognize things through names. Because names have meanings. They help us recognize and identify things.

In fact, even the letters in the Arabic language have meaning. So when I say tree in English, you see something that has a trunk, branches, and leaves - that's what comes to your mind. If it's a pomade it has a trunk and then it has palm fibers. Or if it's a fur, then it's got the little spikes instead of leaves - because there are three types of trees. The fur-trees, the pomades, and then these with the leaves. So when we say شجر in Arabic... what is the difference between شجر and tree. We could argue that they're completely arbitrary and this is the dominant opinion among most linguists of most languages; that names have no connection to the thing named - other than, that they are signs that humans agreed upon. So they say that the Arabs saw this structure of a trunk with branches and leaves and decided to call it a شجر; whereas in English, they decided to name it a tree.

![](images/153/a12c19db-131b-4892-9838-c09bc76e3b15.jpg)

But the ancient Arabs would argue that there is a deep relationship between the word شجر and this thing out there that we see. When you have the letter شین it's called حرف تَفَشِّی. The letter شین is always used for something that spreads out like شمس has radiation; شعر - hair has radiation, you know, your hair spreads out; شجر has radiation, a tree has branches that spread out. And then the letter جیم is حرف الشِّدَّۃ. It is a strong letter. It's used in strong things because the basis of a tree is strong. It's the trunk. جسم is a the body, the main structure of the human being; جبل is a mountain because it's powerful by its nature. Then there is the را which is حَرْفُ تَکْرَارٍ. the letter of repetition. Trees are constantly regenerating themselves.

So the ancient Arabs would say, there's a deep relationship between the letters and the thing that's named itself. And this is something - I mean, these are philosophical debates about the nature of language. But the Arabs would definitely say that there is a secret in names. In the Arabic language, names are not arbitrary. There is actually a reason why a شجر is called a شجر. and a شمس is called a شمس with those particular sounds. This is also why they have an effect! The sounds of the Arabic letters have an effect on the human consciousness. The recitation of Quran has a powerful effect on the human consciousness - just by the nature of its sounds. Even if you don't understand a single word, just listening to a good recitation can deeply move your heart. So Arabic is an incredibly miraculous language and very different from other languages of the world; and all that power - boils down to the human ability to understand names, these اسماء that are provided for us within the framework of language.

### Unification

Now what is the purpose of language? The purpose of language is unification. Language unifies! This is the secret of the human beings, that we have the ability to abstract, differentiate, and identify, through names. We as human beings, we have the ability to see the *ONE* in the many. That's the only way we can communicate. Think about it. The only way we can communicate is by seeing the one in the many. When we talk about a lecture, for example when I tell you, that there is going to be a session next Sunday at 9:30 am. How do you know what a session means in this context?. Because the word session has many different meanings in different contexts. For example I could say…. I'm recording a religious session; or the school is in session; the courtroom is in session. Yasmeen had a great tennis coaching session. The Federal Act was passed in the session of 1908. The word session can have so many different meanings in different context. So when I say there's going to be a session tomorrow, how do you know which meaning of the word session I'm talking about. How are you are able to extract one particular meaning for a word out of so many possible meanings? But we do it instinctively all the time. This is the ability we human beings have. This is because we have this inherent capability to extract the one, out of the many. And when we are not able to do this. When we are unable to zero-in on the one particular meaning, we are forced to say. Wait a minute. Just want to make sure. Did you mean this? Or did you mean that? Every sentence, every phrase, every word needs to boil down to one particular thought from a sea of possibilities.

![](images/153/0afc12f6-52de-4003-bce7-6cefb9664849.jpg)

And so what language, and in particular names, and nouns enable us to do - is speak intelligently about the oneness, and the unified nature of words. Because that's who we are. We are all unifiers by our very nature. This is what we human beings were created to do; because we are موحدون. We were designed to make one out of the many - through language. This is why Adam AS was given the gift of names, this gift of language; because using names we can unify - and that's what we were created for. To perform the ultimate unification of توحید. That's what the word unification means in Arabic - توحید. We were created for توحید. To single out the one الہ - the one true اللہ from all these other deities in our lives, posing as gods.

### ISM - A Lofty Symbol

Now where does the process of this recognition of Allah begin? It begins with اسم اللہ. It begins with the "name" of Allah. The word اسم comes from سمو or وسم. The word سمو means lofty; like the sky in Arabic is called السمآء. The word وسم means a sign. So اسم is a sign for those who can see signs. That is what the root means - a loft sign.

![](images/153/72fefb64-1e7f-4756-b3e4-8aa9ceacb74a.jpg)

It's an علامۃ. It's something you put on to something to signify it. It's a signifier that indicates the signified.

In سُورَۃُ الرُّومِ Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says

> ‏وَمِنْ آيَتِهِۦ خَلْقُ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱخْتِلَفُ أَلْسِنَتِكُمْ وَأَلْوَنِكُمْ ۚ إِنَّ فِى ذَلِكَ لَآيَتٍۢ لِّلْعَلِمِينَ ‎۲۲

Surely and in the variance of the heavens and the earth and your tongues and your complexions are signs for people to think.

So Allah relates the tongues to the heavens, and the colors of our skin to the earth. This is called طباق in Arabic - which is - it's a type of antithesis where you use opposites to explain something. He talks about heavens and earth, and then he talks about tongues and colors - like our skin color and our tongue. So there's a relationship between tongues and heaven and there is a relationship between our skin-colors and the earth. Because tongues or speech comes from the heaven

عَلَّمَ آدَمَ اَسْمَاءَ کَلَّھَا

Allah taught human beings, or imprinted in them language.

الرحمن علم القرآن

That's literally what it means. علَّم means - to imprint something. Language was imprinted within the human being. This is why all human beings have the ability to learn language. Now complexions come from the earth because Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى said that Adam was created from all the top soil of the earth. From the white, the black, the brown, the yellow, the red, So you get all these variances in human complexions because Adam was created from أُدْمَۃ . The word أُدْمَۃ in Arabic means topsoil. Adam means the one created from topsoil.

So all humans have these different complexions, just as there are all these colors of the soil. You see white soil, pure white and then you see red soil; you see yellow soil, black, and ebony black. So complexions are related to earth. And language or speech is related to heaven. Language is meaning, complexion is sensory, you see? So there's a relationship between meaning and sensory. They're both joined in the human being. What is significant and what is palpable; what we understand and what we see touch and feel. So the sensory and the meaning are gathered in these two phenomena - the phenomena of language and body.

Because the essence of the human being is language, we are defined as الْحَیَوَانُ النَّاطِقُ. The speaking or the articulating animals. Because the essence of language is what? Rationality! Language is meaningful. So the essence of the human being is language; and that's why when you judge a person by their skin color, it's so wrong! because you're judging them by something that's incidental to their nature. Whereas when you judge them by what they believe; by how they speak by what they say; by whether they're truthful; whether their virtuous; whether they stand for the truth - that's how human beings should be judged. That's why Dr. King talked about being judged by the content of character. How do you know the content of character? By the essence of the person? What is the essence? Their belief! because that's what's going to manifest from them.

![](images/153/c0f35ef5-2e24-47de-9770-22647f9d900c.jpg)

So اسم means a lofty, or a heavenly sign. Now the statement of بسم اللہ begins with the اسم of Allah; the "lofty signs" pointing to Allah - because that is the only way we can know Allah - through the power of symbolic understanding. We cannot know Allah as we know things. We cannot know Allah the same way we know a tape recorder. or a laptop or a computer. Because essences are hard to understand - even with things. But when it comes to Allah, it becomes absolutely impossible. The only way we can know Allah is through symbolic language - symbols within language, and symbols within existence - which are his آیات. His آیات within language in the form of Quran; and his آیات within existence in the form of the heavens and the earth and everything in between.

The Quran directs us to his 99 divine names as familiar linguistic symbols, so that we can have some concept of who he is. So for example, Allah is called مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ. He is owner or king of the day of judgement. How are we able to understand that? We do that because we understand the concept of a مالک. We have a concept of a king. This enables us to relate to and appreciate the position that Allah will take on the day of judgement. He will assume the position of a king, or the ultimate owner. But if you think about it, we are only able to understand that because of that symbolic reference. But then, we also learnt that Allah is not limited to our concept of what a king means. He is way more exalted than that. But the concept of king at least give us some understanding of his position, and his relationship with us. It at the very least get the point across.

So there is this distinction between Allah and the names of Allah. There is a distinction between اسم and Allah. The اسم are all these lofty symbols he has created for us in the horizons and within ourselves, and in numerous other dimensions, that enable us to know him. That's an اسم - a name. I recognize you by your name! You recognize me by my name!

So now that we understand the linguistic meaning of the word اسم as a lofty sign, what we now understand from بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ - is the loft signs pointing to Allah, and helping us recognize him as الرحمن and الرحیم - the intensely merciful and the consistently merciful. InshaAllah in the next session we'll look at the preposition با and explore the depths of this entire phrase.

## In the Name Of Allah The Merciful

### The Preposition BA

Now let's talk about the letter با which is a preposition meaning "with", or "in". When you say بسم اللہ what you're saying is, I'm beginning in the name of Allah, but you're omitting the "I'm beginning" - that's just understood. It's called مَحْضُوفٌ مُقَدَّرٌ in grammar. Because the با of بسم اللہ is connected to a omitted verb - and this is very important to understand. Because verb or an action is the basis of all existence. Because if you believe the basis of existence is a noun, then you're simply talking about a state. Like for example if we look at our state of "being" as a human being, we're simply here - like I'm being here, I'm existing here, and there's no action involved in my being. But when you bring a verb into it; what you're doing is, you're bringing the time element into it. It's either past present or future. Nouns aren't past present or future. A tree is a tree. But once a tree begins to blow in the wind, there's a time with it. Did it blow in the past? Is it blowing in the present? or will it blow in the future when the wind comes? So the tree swaying, is an action of the essence of the tree - which, without that action is simply being. So this is really a very interesting debate about existence itself. Is the root of existence movement and action? Or is it being?

Now this is also true about Allah's relationship with the world, because Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has action, but his action is created. His actual essence is passive. There's no action in his essence when we talk about Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. Allah is essence and attribute - and both, essence and attribute are states of being. But action is a substantive that moves into time and that's why the understanding in theology is that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is beyond time and space. So his action is something that manifested as, and within creation. He brings things into existence through his فعل , but that فعل is محدث. So

وَإِذَا قَضَىٰٓ أَمْرًۭا فَإِنَّمَا يَقُولُ لَهُۥ كُن فَيَكُونُ

When he decides an affair, he says to it "be" and it "is". So Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى brings creation into existence through a command - which is a فعل.; and so the preposition با is connected to the omitted verb of Allah's action of creation.

So when we recite the بسم اللہ we're saying بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ - I'm beginning with the recognition of the lofty signs within Allah's creation - that are a witness and proof of his intense and consistent love, compassion, care and mercy. This is the context referring to the primordial act of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. In other words, this is the interpretation of that statement in the transcendent realm. But what about its meaning in the immanent or personal realm?

### Omitted Verb

In order to understand that, let's break down this statement into its grammatical components. First of all, when do we recite the بسم اللہ ? We recite it at the beginning of every action, right? So let's look at this statement from a grammatical point of view. What are the various grammatical components in this statement?

- It begins with the letter با - which is a preposition

- Then we have the word اسم which is a genitive masculine noun. This noun on its own is written as “اِسْم” meaning the name; but because its joined with the preposition, the الف is dropped and the با fuses with the اسم to become بسم making it an indefinite noun

- Then we have the proper noun Allah

- and finally the statement ends with two adjectives of mercy - الرحمن and الرحیم

So we have a proposition, we have an indefinite noun, we have a proper noun and two adjectives. What's missing? From a grammatical point of view what's missing? A verb! We know that grammatically a statement is incomplete without a verb.

So Allah left the phrase بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ as an incomplete sentence. He omitted the verb. Do you see that? Why? Because we conceptually complete the sentence by providing the verb through our action. This is why we recite the بسم اللہ at the beginning of every action, every task, thus providing the verb to complete the structure of this statement. SubhanAllah!

So now if I'm about to read the Quran, and I say بِسْمِ اللَّہِ االرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ, my action of reading is what completes the بسم اللہ. So now what I'm saying, is "With the name of Allah who is intensely and consistently loving and merciful, I begin reading this book.” And so now my action, no matter what it may be, gets empowered by the بسم اللہ.

But what's even more amazing, is that if we look at this linguistically based on everything we learnt, what I'm really saying, is that my ability to read, and the Quran that I intend to read, is a lofty sign pointing to Allah's incredible love and mercy - his رحمنیۃ and his رحیمیۃ. It puts Allah's love and mercy right at the center of my focus as soon as I recite this phrase. My very act (regardless of what it is), becomes a lofty sign and symbol; it becomes a witness and bears testimony to Allah's رحمۃ; and it changes my perspective on the action I am about to do, even before I've actually started doing it. SubhanAllah!

So now when I say بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ before I begin driving, the entire experience of driving becomes a reminder of Allah's love and mercy. My ability to drive, the resources enabling me to own a car, my mental capacity to follow the signs, my ability to assess traffic, my intuition of when to stop, how fast to go, all the facilities within the car, the scenery outside the car - every single things becomes a loft reminder that point me directly to Allah's intense and consistent love, care, concern and mercy for me!! SubhanAllah! This is the power of the بسم اللہ.

SubhanAllah! Its incredible isn't it? Allah's first act was based on this رحمۃ ; he gave us the ability to act; and every act we do becomes a witness to his love and mercy - all of that captured within the phrase بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. I begin this task, this action which is a lofty heavenly sign reminding me of Allah's intense and consistent love, care, concern and mercy

### Allah and his name

Now what is the purpose of reciting the بسم اللہ before we begin an action? As I said, from our perspective, it reminds us of Allah's love and mercy, and naturally makes us love him back; our love manifesting in the form of worship and obedience - our عبودیۃ. But what does it do from Allah's perspective? It fills our action with برکۃ. As Allah said:

أنی اذا اُطِعتُ رَضِیتُ. وأذا رَضِیتُ بَارَکتُ. وَ لَیسَ لِبَرَکَتِی نِھَایَۃ

When you obey me it pleases me. when I am pleased. I bless you with برکۃ and there is no limit to my برکۃ. What is برکۃ? It is abundance. Now what has abundance got to do with بسم اللہ ? What has it got to do with the اسم of Allah?

Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says in سورۃ الرحمن.

> ‏تَبَرَكَ ٱسْمُ رَبِّكَ ذِى ٱلْجَلَلِ وَٱلْإِكْرَامِ ‎۷۸
>
> Blessed be the Name of your Lord, the Owner of Majesty and Honor.

The name of Allah; the name of your master is glorious and benevolent - his name is full of برکۃ. But Allah does not mention in this verse which name is full of برکۃ right? He just said the name of your maser is full of برکۃ. But what did this Surah begin with? الرحمن! And what is the first attribute of Allah mentioned in the بسم اللہ? Again الرحمن. So Allah is telling us something about the attribute الرحمن, that it is full of برکۃ.

But we also know that Allah himself is full of برکۃ. Because he says it in the Quran.

> فَتَبَارَكَ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنُ ٱلْخَلِقِينَ ‎۱۴
>
> So blessed is Allah , the best of creators

Your Allah, who is the best of creators, is full of برکۃ

So the اسم of Allah is full of برکۃ and the name Allah itself is full of برکۃ. So the first two words of the بسم اللہ, the اسم and اللہ, they're both loaded with برکۃ. So what do you think will naturally happens to our action, when we recite these two powerful words before beginning whatever it is we're doing? It fills it with برکۃ. In other words, by reciting بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ, not only are we reminding ourselves of Allah's intense and consistent love and mercy, though the act itself, but we're also asking him to fill that act with برکۃ.

### Abundance

Now what is برکۃ?

There is no word in the English language that can fully capture the meaning of the word برکۃ. So we need to understand this word through certain concepts.

### Abundance

In classical Arabic the word برکۃ is defined as. البرکۃُ النَّماَءُ وَ الزِّیَادَۃ

It is defined as growth and abundance. The first is النمو which means growth; something that rises. Something that increases in size, makes progress consistently increasing in results; or something that goes further and further and increases فَوْقَ التَّوَقُّعِ beyond expectations. If you plant the seeds of one single fruit in the ground, how many fruits should you expect in return? Just one! But you take the seeds from that one fruit and after you plant it in the ground you get an enormous number of fruits, each producing hundreds of thousands of seeds - that is برکۃ. You planted one seed and eventually got thousands of seeds. That's a growth beyond expectations. That is what you call برکۃ.

Now what is the comparison of a tree with a seed? It is beyond comparison. A seed is tiny, a tree is huge. A seed is delicate, a tree is firm. A seed has no value, a tree bears hundreds upon hundreds of fruits, season after season, year after year. If you really think about it, what we get from a tree, when you compare it to a seed - there really is no comparison. Yet that entire tree came from that one seed. SubhanAllah! That is برکۃ unprecedented growth, unlimited increase.

### Never Ending

The Arabs say أَبْتَرَکْتَ السَّمَآءَ referring to rain that keeps on coming and keeps on coming. So continuity is another characteristic of the word برکۃ. Something that is extensively abundant

### Stability

The word برکۃ is also used in the Arabic language for stability. So for example the Arab would say أِذا أَناَخَ الْجَمَل . When the camel settles on the ground, and it doesn't move or budge - that is also برکۃ. And this is really beautiful. concept; because when you grow something, and you grow it too much.

I'll give an example so you can understand. When kids play with lego, they build up this huge tower - which is just block upon block upon block right? Now as the tower is getting tall, does it become more stable? or does it become less stable? It becomes less stable. The taller the tower, the less stable it becomes. It will eventually fall and break. and there will be no tower anymore. isn't that true?
So when something increases rapidly, and increase too much - it becomes less stable. But when something has برکۃ in it, it means that it will increases in quantity, and it will keep increasing beyond expectations. yet remains as stable as a camel sunk in the sand. SubhanAllah! In other words don't think this increase will go away. Its not an unstable increase.

We find unstable increase all around us in the world. Our economy goes up really fast, and then crashes really fast. There are governments that take over really fast, and then disappear really fast as well. Fashion industry! There are trends that pickup really fast, and then disappear just as fast. Something else takes its place. But Allah says برکۃ is something that increases but it remains stable. It doesn't go away.

![](images/159/300d20b6-e90b-4023-9d00-29a23096e187.jpg)

### Abundance through Allah's name

The name of Allah, the اسم اللہ is filled with برکۃ. The name Allah itself is filled with برکۃ. His blessed name is being mentioned since the day creation came into existence, and it only keeps increasing and growing with every single passing moment. And it will continue to grow till the very end of time. Worshipers come and go; but the one who is worshiped remains forever. People who glorify the names of Allah come and go; many came before us, many will come after us. We will all be gone one day, but the glory of Allah will always always remain. This is the برکۃ of Allah's name.

So now, to summarize, when I recite بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ before each and every action I do, my action itself turns into a reminder, a heavenly loft sign pointing to the loving care and mercy of الرحمن and الرحیم in my life - and what that does is, it creates برکۃ in the results of my action. There is a HADEES of Rasul Allah ﷺ in which he said

کل شئی أبتر بدون بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ

Every single act is sterile or infertile without بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. Every act this is done without the remembrance of Allah will have no برکۃ and no abundance in it. What does that mean? What does abundance of something do? Think about it? If you have an abundance of money for example, what does it free you of? It frees you of ھم and غم. It liberates you from the shackles of anxiety and depression. You're no longer concerned about that aspect of your life, you see? Now imagine if you have the abundance, a never ending supply of Allah's رحمنیۃ and رحیمیۃ in your life?! If you have an abundance of ذکر اللہ that close connection with Allah, will anything or any situation in life - no matter bad it is - will be able to affect you? Will it make you anxious or depressed? Absolutely not! Because no matter the outcome, you're experiencing the joy of closeness, and experiencing Allah's love, simply by the act itself. SubhanAllah! And so what Rasul Allah ﷺ meant by that statement that every thing is sterile and infertile without بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ , is that without the love of Allah, without a proximity to Allah, no amount of physical abundance will satisfy you in this world. And we find the greatest proof of this HADEES when we look at the world around us. Millionaires, billionaires who are extremely anxious and constantly in a state of depression. On the other hand we find certain true Muslims and believers, who despite the worst of situations seem the most content and happy - why? Because they're connected to Allah, and that connection creates برکۃ in whatever little they have to an extent, that they are extremely happy, content and satisfied with it - so much so that they don't even desire more! SubhanAllah!! This is the برکۃ of بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ.

May Allah fill our hearts with his love, and make every action we do from this day, an experience of his love and mercy.

## Linguistic Perfection Of HAMD

In the previous session we learnt that Allah created us as a noun - speaking in grammatical terms. We are all nouns in our essence. But from the moment we wake up, until the moment we sleep; and in fact even while we are asleep, what we're constantly doing through our actions, is creating verbs. So the power of بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ is that it provides a context for our verbs; our actions; by turning them into reminders; by turning our actions into these heavenly lofty signs; an اسم of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى; reminding us of his intense and consistent love, care, kindness, concern and mercy.

### A Composite Word

And once we experience his ربوبیۃ through our interactions with this عالم that he created for us - because all of our actions are only happening, because his ربوبیۃ enabled and empowered us to do so, right? I'm able to drink water because he gave me the physical capacity to pour water into a glass; and he made the water subservient through his laws of nature, so that it obediently falls into the glass when I pour it. It doesn't refuse; it doesn't change its direction, it doesn't start rising in mid air. It follows and obeys these laws of gravity, allowing me to drink it. What is this, if not a sign, of the mercy and love of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى? Where he enabled me and the water to interact in this manner. That is his action - his proof of ربوبیۃ executed with intense and consistent love. So now once I recognize and experience this love, what should be my natural response to that love? It has to be اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین. All praise and gratitude belongs to Allah the رب of the عالمین. This is why the بسم اللہ directly connects with the حمد اللہ.

In today's session I want to do a linguistic study of the word حمد to demonstrate the perfection of this statement; so that we can appreciate why Allah used this particular word? and in this particular fashion in this verse. Why didn't he choose a different style for saying the same thing. So let's begin with the word حمد because this form of his diction, veils many valuable lessons within it for us to learn from.

### Mutually Exclusive Terms

This word حمد is a composite word that means two things. It means praise and it means gratitude. Now there are two other words in the Arabic language for praise and gratitude. The commonly used word for praise is مدح and the word for gratitude is شکر. What's the difference? Praise is a gesture of appreciation. When you are moved by some thing beautiful, or colorful, or scenic; when something intrigues you in some way; when something impresses you, what do you do? You praise it! You verbalize your appreciation by praising it. That's the very natural human response to something that our soul finds beautiful.

Now gratitude is something entirely different. Gratitude is done in response to a beneficial act done for you. If somebody lends you money, or does something nice for you, you say شکراً. Gratitude is always done as a response to a beneficial act. You see the difference?
