Praise is done when something impresses you; or you appreciate something about someone, whereas gratitude is done in response to a favor or a kindness done for you.

![](images/160/9f1d99bf-6c97-4f5b-baaa-b62c9928ac0a.jpg)

Now even though the two terms are used interchangeably in our lives, they're both mutually exclusive. Someone you thank, is not necessarily someone you praise. Quran clearly distinguishes between these two concepts. For example, in the story of مُوسَی عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ, we know that he was adopted and raised in the house of فرعون. Years later when مُوسَی عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ returns to deliver Allah's message, فرعون says to him, how dare you? How dare you talk to me this way?

> ‏قَالَ أَلَمْ نُرَبِّكَ فِينَا وَلِيدًۭا وَلَبِثْتَ فِينَا مِنْ عُمُرِكَ سِنِينَ ‎۱۸‏
>
> ( 18 ) said :"Did we not bring you up among us as a child? And you did dwell many years of your life with us.

Did we not raise you as a newborn in this house? Did you not spend many years of your life growing up here? How dare you speak to me like that? In other words what فرعون is saying, is that aren't you grateful? I raised you for so many years and now you're going against everything I taught you? To that مُوسَی عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ responds:

وَتِلْكَ نِعْمَةٌۭ تَمُنُّهَا عَلَىَّ

You did in fact show me that favor, and for **that** I'm grateful. So even though فرعون is someone who will never be praised, especially not by a prophet, but even when he does a favor, he will still be thanked! So gratitude can exist without praise.

In the case of parents, Allah says:

> ‏وَوَصَّيْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ بِوَلِدَيْهِ حَمَلَتْهُ أُمُّهُۥ وَهْنًا عَلَىٰ وَهْنٍۢ وَفِصَلُهُۥ فِى عَامَيْنِ أَنِ ٱشْكُرْ لِى وَلِوَلِدَيْكَ إِلَىَّ ٱلْمَصِيرُ ‎۱۴
>
> And We have enjoined upon man [care] for his parents. His mother carried him, [increasing her] in weakness upon weakness, and his weaning is in two years. Be grateful to Me and to your parents; to Me is the [final] destination.

Be grateful to me and both of your parents

> ‏وَإِن جَهَدَاكَ عَلَىٰٓ أَن تُشْرِكَ بِى مَا لَيْسَ لَكَ بِهِۦ عِلْمٌۭ فَلَا تُطِعْهُمَا‎۱۵
>
> But if they strive to make you associate partners with Me that of which you have no knowledge,

But if your parents are working against you in forcing you to disbelieve and commit شرک - don't obey them. But you still have to be what? You still have to be grateful to them. In other words, if your parents are forcing you do disbelieve in Allah, you don't have to praise them, but you still have to be grateful to them. اِبْرَاھِیمُ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ is a good example for this. He does not praise his father, who was an idol worshiper, but he still grateful to him.

So الشُّکْرُ وَ الْمَدْحُ praise and gratitude are two separate things; they are mutually exclusive. You can praise without gratitude; and you can be grateful without praising someone or something.

But what's interesting about the word حمد that it necessarily means both things together! When Allah says اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ - he is saying praise is for Allah; and gratitude is also for Allah - both at the same time. The word حمد makes both concepts mutually inclusive.

### Praise & Gratitude

### Praise

Now let's look at why didn't Allah use these words مدح and شکر and used حمد instead. Let's start with مدح. Why didn't Allah say:

اَلْمَدْحُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ اَلْعَالَمِینَ

All praise is for Allah. The problem with the word مدح is that it is a praise that does not necessarily have to be genuine. We all know that praise can be insincere. A kid walks in the house with a really bad report card - as soon as he enters he goes, Mom! OMG! Your food! smells so delicious - and she hasn't even started cooking yet. So he's praising her, but it's not a genuine praise. You get it? Praise can be fake. There is fake praise done for kings, for bosses, for people in high positions all the time! People falsely praise looks all the time. Wife asks the husband, does this dress make me look fat? Oh no! you look beautiful! That's مدح. This is why in classical Arabic when you praise someone's looks, you don't say حَمِدَ, because everybody knows that the person being praised did not make their own face. If they're beautiful, pretty or handsome, Allah made them that way. So saying, "You're so beautiful" would be considered مَدَحَ because it's understood that praising looks and other external features, is an inherently false praise.

Now حمد on the other hand cannot be a false praise. It cannot be insincere. Why? Because حمد is generated in the heart by the awareness of the magnificence and the glory of the one being praised. I'll say that again. حمد the praise element of حمد occurs when the heart becomes aware of the magnificence and the glory of the one being praised. The heart and the intellect recognize that Allah truly deserves that praise - therefore حمد can never be a false praise. The حمد of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is not dependent on a personal need. It cannot be motivated by a personal agenda. It stems simply from being impressed by who he is - not based on what he does for us. You understand?

### Gratitude

Now let's think about شکر. What if Allah had said

اَلشُّکْرُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین

All gratitude is for Allah. What's the problem with that? Gratitude or thanks originates from the the acknowledgment a favor. Someone does something for you that you appreciate, you thank the person for that favor. You have a flat tire. Someone comes along and helps you, you feel grateful, and you express that gratitude by saying thank you! Thanks can only happen when you have a **reason** to thank someone. It is a reaction to a favor. It is never something you start with. You don't see a stranger passing by and say - hey you! thanks!! You never do that.

When Allah uses the word حمد, which is a combination of both praise and gratitude, there are certain rules that apply linguistically. First rule is that the praise aspect of حمد has to be genuine; it can never be fake. Second the gratitude aspect of it is not reactionary. Therefore linguistically saying اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ is far more powerful than saying اَلْمَدْحُ لِلَّہِ and it is way more powerful than saying اَلشُّکْرُ لِلّٰہِ; because it is a lot more comprehensive than both.

### Combining Praise & Gratitude

Now what if Allah had said

الْمَدْحُ وَ الشُّکْرُ لِلَّہِ رب العلمین

Praise and gratitude belongs to Allah. What would be the problem with that? Even if we remove the shortcomings of both words مدح and شکر, the problem now is that when you put the word "and" in between two things. which is the letter و it separates both words even in meaning. When I say الْمَدْحُ وَ الشُّکْرُ لِلَّہِ, linguistically what I'm saying is that praise belongs to Allah for certain things; and gratitude belongs to Allah for other things. They're not always combined; because the words aren't combined. This is a very cool feature of Arabic language. You don't say what you don't mean. So if we use the two words gratitude and praise and join them with an **and**, in classical Arabic that means, sometimes I am in the mood to praise him, but not thank them; and then there are other times when I'm in the mood to thank him, but not praise him. But when Allah says اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ that means for every act he does or doesn't do, for every blessing he gives or holds back; genuine praise and unconditional gratitude is due to him - both at the same time. We don't get to pick and choose which one.

So now if you own a nice car, and I say اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ , what have I just done? I genuinely praised Allah for giving human beings this knowledge and ability to create cars, to design and manufacture it; **and** I thank Allah for letting me own one. So now I am praising and thanking Allah both at the same time. All of that is eloquently captured in this beautiful word الحمد.

### HAMD is an Attitude

This is why حمد is the attitude of a true Muslim. Because it is optimistic in nature. You know sometimes you're going through a difficult time. and someone asks you, "how are you doing?", and you just say "Oh you know, life sucks. but اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ. You're not really praising him or thanking him when you say it that way. اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ is not something we just say, or throw around. It's a reminder of an attitude! Allah is not interested in what comes out of our tongues. He's more interested in what is in our hearts that is **connected** with what comes out of our tongues. So when a true Muslim says اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ, regardless of his situation or circumstances, the sentiment that resides in his heart is that indeed all praise and gratitude belongs to Allah, regardless of my situation. If I'm going through a difficult time, and I say اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ what I mean is, indeed my praise and gratitude is for Allah, because no matter what I'm going through, it could always be worse. Someone right now in the world is going through a much harsher version of what I'm going through. I'm grateful to Allah that he is lenient with me. I am grateful to Allah that he has provided me with the resource to fix my situation; whereas there might be some who doesn't have the same resources I do. I praise him, for his majesty, as how the one who gives health, can take it away, and restore it when he desires. Because my pain should remind me of all the times Allah had me living in comforts in which I forgot about him, right? So حمد اللہ - sincere praises and unconditional gratitude belongs to the one who reminded me of his grace and blessings by temporarily or permanently taking it away. حمد اللہ - sincere praises and unconditional gratitude is for the one who filled my life with undeserved blessings. So an understanding of اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ can really empower us and completely transforms our attitude towards life itself.

### Noun vs Verbs

Now let's look at this term from a grammatical perspective. In language you have nouns and verbs. Verbs have tenses, whereas nouns don't. There's no past tense noun, or present tense noun, or future tense noun, right? Now when I say: "*I praise Allah*", did I use a noun or a verb? I used a verb. It's a present tense. But when Allah say praise belongs to Allah; is the word praise a noun or a verb? It's a noun. You see? Allah could have used a verb. He could've said اَحْمَدُ اللَّہَ I praise Allah. He could've said نَحْمَدُ اللّٰہَ We praise Allah! But Allah did not use a verb. He used a noun - why?

![](images/160/c5cc90e2-d33d-4464-a9a9-070907dbb34e.jpg)

Because when I use a verb, there is obviously going to be a tense associated with it. So when I use a present tense to say, I praise Allah - then I am only talking about the present; I'm not saying anything about the past or the future. If I use the past tense, then I am not guaranteeing anything about the future or the present. Because a verb limits and confines the action within time. It makes the action temporary. But Allah used a noun, which by its nature is permanent. Therefore

اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین

Allah's genuine praise and unconditional gratitude is described with permanence; and what that means is, that I may be praising Allah now, but the praise of Allah has always been there, and will always be there. I am not going to be here in this world forever, but the praise and gratitude of Allah will always be here forever. His praise and gratitude is not dependent on me. A huge reality within اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ is this concept of permanence in the praise and gratitude for Allah. It is a recognition of the fact that his حمد is not dependent on my temporary existence. It is not because of me.

See, when we use a verb, somebody needs to **do **the verb. It's called the subject or the فاعل in Arabic. There has to be a doer for an action. You can't just go into a conversation and say - failed the test! Who failed the test? You cant just say. Disappeared! What disappeared? Oh my book disappeared. You understand? When you use a verb, you always need someone to do it. But a noun doesn't need a doer. It doesn't need a subject. A noun is independent all by itself. An apple is an apple. It can exist all by itself, independent of any doer. So when Allah used the word الحمد, he made it a noun. He made it independent. He doesn't need anyone to do the actual praising, and he doesn't need anyone to be grateful. Had he used a verb, that would have meant he needed someone to praise him. and he needed someone to be grateful to him. You see?
So he removed the constrictions of time and space by using a noun. This means that even if there was not a single creation in this world, the حمد of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى would still be there. Even after this world, this universe fades into non-existence, his praise and gratitude will remain. It does not require a doer or an object. SubhanAllah! All of this captured by one single word, used grammatically as a noun.

### Other Variations

### Command

Now let's look at some other possible variations. What if Allah said أِحمد اللہ رب العلمین. What if he used a command? Praise Allah! the master of عالمین. Wouldn't that have been better? After all, he is a master. So a command would make sense. So why didn't he make it a command? I'll give you an example to help you understand. I tell my daughter to bring me a glass of water. I say. Hey Erum! bring me a glass of water! I'm giving her a command. Once she hears me, she has two possible choices. She can either bring me the glass of water. OR. she can bring me the glass of water . Those are the only two clear choices as far as I'm concerned. Now if Allah commanded us to praise him - as a command. What are the two things that could happen? Some would praise him. Some would obey his command; and then there would be others who would disobey him. When you tell someone to do something, the ball is now in their court. It depends on them to comply with your wishes. Maybe they'll choose to do it, maybe they won't. Allah did not talk about praise and gratitude in a way that depends on us. He did not put the ball in our court. He said whether you do it or not; whether you are grateful to me or not; whether you praise me are not; my praise and gratitude (my حمد) is always there. It has been there forever, and it will be there forever. Human beings will come and go; nations will come and go; generations after generation will come and go; this universe will come and go; but the حمد of Allah will always be there. It is simply a matter of fact. SubhanAllah!

### Emotion vs Information

![](images/160/80c720a9-7ae8-4b6b-b674-701c4dfd0b3e.jpg)

Now what if Allah said انَّا اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین. Certainly! For sure. Absolutely, no doubt about it! حمد is for Allah. Doesn't that sound a lot more powerful than "All praise and gratitude belongs to Allah?". It does! So how come Allah didn't say انَّا اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین. If you remember from our sessions on the miracle of Quran, on the topic of conversation I told you there are two kinds of communications. A communication can either be informative or it can emotional. It can be جُمْلَۃٌ اِنْشَائِیَّۃٌ or it can be جُمْلَۃٌ خَبَرِیَّۃٌ. Either you have speech that is expressing your feelings and emotions, or you have speech that is communicating information. Those are the two kinds of speech that human beings are capable of. That's it! So for example, right now, as I'm teaching you about الحمد, am I delivering information? or am I expressing my emotions? I am delivering information. This is informative speech. But when I'm talking to my friend, and I'm telling my friend how I feel about someone or something, what kind of communication is that? That is an emotional conversation. So those are the only two types of speech we as human beings are capable of. The thing is, that in Arabic linguistics. when you use انا the statement can **only** be informative. It can only be factual. It can never be emotional. But if you don't use انا, the communication can now be informative and it can also be emotional. You understand? So by not using انا Allah made his حمد an open statement. We can now talk about Allah's praise and gratitude with others in order to give them information about it. But we can also make it a statement to express our own emotion. SubhanAllah! You see the beauty of it? Using this style, right now by saying, اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین I'm sharing with you about Allah's حمد. A few hours later, when I'm standing before him in صلوۃ, and I use the exact same words اَلْحَمْدُ لِلّٰہِ رب العلمین, I will be expressing my praise and gratitude to my master. It will be an emotional conversation with him - SubhanAllah!

So you can see how perfect the usage of this word is in the context of the فاتحۃ.

### Summary

![](images/160/00660fed-358b-498c-bb18-8cc7ff5c2952.jpg)

So to summarize the concept of praise and gratitude in grammatical terms - we are nouns and we create verbs through our actions. Allah is a noun and he creates verbs through his action. Now on a daily basis, we are interacting with Allah; and he is interacting with us - through his ربوبیۃ over his عالم. In this interaction, (listen to this carefully). In this interaction between us and Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى - for the recognition of the noun that is Allah, he is worthy of our مدح - our sincere and independent praise; and for his actions influencing our existence, he is worthy of our شکر - our ultimate and unconditional gratitude. Both these concepts are beautifully and eloquently encapsulated in this one single phrase - اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ اَلْعَالَمِینَ - all forms of sincere praise belongs to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى for who he is; and my gratitude (on a personal level) and the ultimate gratitude (on a general level) belongs to Allah for what he did, what he does and what he will do - which is constantly manifesting on the sage of this عالم - Therefore اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ اَلْعَالَمِینَ

## Abundant and Consistent Mercy

### Mercy

In the previous session we did a linguistic study of the word حمد. We learnt that it is a combination of sincere praise and unconditional gratitude, owed to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى for his constant, relentless, never-ending ربوبیۃ over his عالمین. And it's not just حمد but rather الحمد - meaning ultimate praise and absolute gratitude belongs to him by virtue of him being the رب. Then he says الرحمن الرحیم. The abundantly merciful, the consistently merciful. The word رحم as we all know, generally means mercy, but it is actually a type of mercy that brings two things together in a way, such that the principles of ربوبیۃ can be applied. This is why the womb of a mother is called رحم because it brings the baby and the mother together, so that the mother can nurture it; so that she can shower her love over it; so that she can help sustain the fetus and make it grow. So the word رحمۃ is so powerful, that our general understanding of the word "mercy" falls short in capturing its essence.

So now in this next verse, Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى highlights his رحمۃ by dedicating an entire verse just to mention the quality of his ربوبیۃ. What, according to him, is the essence, the defining attribute of his ربوبیۃ? It is رحم that manifest through his abundance, his رحمنیۃ and his consistency, through his رحیمیۃ. This is why, when we look around us; when we study the عالم we find this one thread of رحمۃ flowing through the entire fabric of his عالم. It is the glue that lovingly holds this عالم together. It is that magic magnetic force that just naturally pulls us towards Allah and keeps us connected with our master. So just to put this concept in visual terms, you can imagine his رحمۃ as this gravitational field; this force of love that pulls us towards him; and what we experience from that pull is حمد; our ultimate praise and our absolute gratitude owed this رَبُّ الْعَالَمِینَ who is is الرحمن and الرحیم.

### Gratitude

Now if we look at the gratitude component of الحمد; if we just look at the aspect of شکر ? What is the reality of شکر? What is the manifestation of sincere gratitude? It is تقوی; It is the constant awareness of Allah's presence. It is the constant recognition and awareness of his blessings; and this is a very high station; it holds a very lofty مقام, and not everyone is truly capable of achieving the highest ranks of شکر. This is why Allah says:

وَقَلِیلٌ مِنْ عِبَادِیَ الشَّکُورُ

Very few of my servants are شکور. The شکور is different from the شاکر. The شاکر (the grateful) is the one who is grateful for Allah's blessings, but the شکور is the one who's grateful for calamities and tribulations - not just blessings. He actually feels gratitude when horrible things happen to him. Because most people, at best, they'll be patient or resigned, but the شکور actually says اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ as he's experiencing these calamities, because he knows that Allah only does good for his believer, right? There's a HADEES

عجباً لِأمْرِ المُؤمِن فَأِنَّ أمرَہُ لَہُ کُلُّہُ خَیر

How wondrous is the affair of the believer; his affair all of it is good. If you're a believer, then it is all good. And that's why if you really do believe that, you enter into a state of constant gratitude, even for the tribulations اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ. Always a good thing to remember, is what ابن عباس said, that no matter how bad it is, it could always be worse - and there are people like that. There are people even non-Muslim. You'll meet non-Muslims that are like that. They always look at the good side. Well, it could be worse! think of this! count your blessings!

Now in this verse, Allah says he is a رب that is الرحمن and الرحیم. How does his رحمۃ manifest in our lives? How does the abundance of his love demonstrate in our lives? Through his blessings; his gifts; his نعمۃ. When I as a parent want to demonstrate my love for my children, how do I do that? By bestowing gifts upon them. Valuable, desirable, beneficial gifts, right? Gifts that the child understands that he or she could not attain by their own efforts. Now think about the blessings, the gifts that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has bestowed upon us. Incredible gifts; Infinite gifts

> وَإِن تَعُدُّوا۟ نِعْمَتَ ٱللَّهِ لَا تُحْصُوهَآ ‎۳۴

If you attempt to enumerate the blessings of Allah, you will never be able to enumerate them. لَا تُحْصُوهَآ. Gifts that are not only impossible to count, their value is simply impossible to measure. Just look at one single نعمۃ. Look at the نعمۃ for instance of the eye. This is one نعمۃ. First of all, think about this- blinking. Just think about blinking. You don't even notice that you blink. Now if you didn't blink - did you ever see pictures of Aristotle Onassis? When he got old? The richest man - one of the richest men in the world. He owned his own private fleet of ships. He had to us band-aids to hold up his eyelids, because he lost the function of the muscle that return the eyelid; and his eyelids just dropped all the time. So he used Band-Aids to hold his eyelids up. But then he had to put drops in his eyes all the time, because if your eyelids don't close they get dry; and you get dust in them; because one of the things that blinking does, is it brings the dust back down. They're like windshield wipers. They brings the dust down and then what happens is the dust moves to the side and accumulates in the corners, right? So when you wipe your eyes, you'll get some little dust in it. You don't even think about that. That's just one part of the eye! But then if you look at the intricate network of these nerves of the eye, and the myelination of that sheath - the fact that there's constant synaptic response; the fact that there's neural transmission; because that breaks down for some people. We don't even think of all this. Think of the electric work that goes in the function of an eye, right?

But then colors! just think of colors! just colors! Allah could have made everything black and white. Look at all the subtle colors that he put in the in the world that we can experience. The eyes can experience an incredible variety of colors, right? I mean, that's a نعمۃ. Then look how some colors - they just give you pleasure seeing them. You don't even think about it, but there's a vibration. If you look there are seven colors, right? All colors go down to seven primary colors. In color theory, they'll go down to three I think, but there's basically seven in a spectrum. So if you look at the visible light spectrum, each one of those colors has an effect upon your soul. Red has a certain vibrational resonance. It's a lower resonance than violet. Violet is at the High Spectrum; red is at the low Spectrum. Red, when you see red, it stimulates you. You don't even know that. They know that now in color theory, because they've done studies on people; if you put people in red rooms, it has a different effect on them, than if you put them in blue rooms, it will actually change their moods, because these colors have a resonance - there's a electromagnetic radiation. So it's all having a psychological effect on you, and you don't even know it. Now the thing about the world, if you go and look at green; green will make you feel good. That's why people love to look at grass; or love to look at trees. It has a یُنعِش that's what the Arabs say. It's called ربیع . This is why we pray Oh Allah make the Quran the spring of my heart and

اَللّٰھُمَّ جَعَلَ الْقُرْآنَ رَبِیعَ قَلْبِی

What does that mean? It means that thing which brings your heart back to life. Because spring brings your whole being back to life. People start feeling good. People get, what they call SAD - "Seasonal Affective Disorder". There's people who get depressed in the wintertime, but then spring comes around and they forget how depressed they were in the winter. They start feeling wonderful. Why are they feeling wonderful? Green! color start changing. There's photosynthesis. All that chlorophyll is starting to come back to life. Flowers start blooming again.

Flowers! Look at flowers. I mean, what is that? A flower? Why do people like smelling a flower? That's another نعمۃ of scent - but just the sight of a beautiful flower right? Beauty! when the eye looks at something beautiful, why do we all respond the same way? When you see something beautiful and most people share a common taste in beauty. if you see something beautiful, you're attracted to it - it's an attraction. Why? It's because beauty is an attribute of God; and what you're seeing in the عالم is a reflection of a Divine attribute of your رب. So you're essentially attracted to the divine, but you think you're being attracted to the flower. No! It's the beauty of the Divine manifesting through that flower. That's the real attraction. The real attraction is not that ephemeral beauty that fades. It's the manifestation of the divine in creation that's the real attraction! But people don't realize that. So there are all these things about the eye and and we don't even think about it. Look at the eyelashes; look at the eyebrows. They're serving a purpose; eyelashes serve a purpose right? I mean when anything comes near your eye, you instinctively close your eyelids and it protects the eye. That's just amazing. So there are all these things - just about the eye - if you really start thinking about it; look at the abundance! Look at the رحمنیۃ of this رب. And that's based on what we know so far - there's all these blessings we don't even know about yet.

What about light? The external factor that makes the eye useful; that allows sight; which in turn makes people happy or depressed. Why do people get depressed? One of the reasons they think is because serotonin levels drop, which is a key hormone in making us feel happy. One of its release factors is based on light. So you're taking in light during the day you don't even think about it. Melatonin is also based on light. Melatonin is what makes you get sleepy. That's why people that are constantly in artificial light have difficulty sleeping. But the eye is the one that's doing all that. The eye is dictating. That's why you get tired. It's all based on the eye. So even your sleep patterns are being affected by the human eye. We don't even realize that. When we feel awake when we feel drowsy, right? It's all a result of the eye. I mean this is just the eye alone. We just can't enumerate all of the blessings of the eye. That's one نعمۃ. That's just نِعْمَۃُ الْعَیْنِ we're talking about here. There are untold, infinite blessings in it. And it's just one نعمۃ.

So the شکور is the one who constantly experiences that gratitude - the presence and the lack of it. But this comes from absolute certainty and this is a مقام related to رضی, which is contentment with Allah; and it's a state of محبۃ; because once you fall in love, you can't see anything else. I mean, that's the beauty of Love. When you're in love, you can't even see faults, right? Even when they do exist. You just can't see them. If you love Layla and anybody tells you, you know, Layla did this or she did that - you get angry. What? Layla would never do that. No! No! I saw Layla did this. You're lying!! You're a liar. Layla wouldn't do that. Why? because he's in love with Layla. No matter what you say about Layla, he won't believe you. That's real love! people don't even know that type of love anymore. Right? So if you're in love with Allah, he can't do anything that would harm you, or hurt you. Nothing! Cause you'll say - it's me! I did it to myself. Allah didn't do that. Allah wouldn't do that. I did it to myself. Or that Allah is doing it because like the father that punishes the child. You see? I mean you see a father punishing the child. The father loves the child. So why is he punishing it? Because he knows that in the punishment is benefit for the child. It's disciplining the child. He's bringing the child back. Through that punishment he's reminding the child of something worse. If I see my child putting his hand in the fire, I slap his hand; Why am I doing that? Because I want him to taste a light pain in order to avoid a much greater pain. You see? Because I knows that the pain of slap is nothing, compared to the pain of the flame. And when Allah gives us great tribulations, those are nothing compared to the hellfire - nothing! So if Allah is purifying us to prevent us from doing something he doesn't want us to do, then the شکور realizes, that this tribulation is the love of his creator for him. The prophets ﷺ once saw a mother nursing her child - and this is a man, as they say - the man of Allah takes discernment from everything. He takes عبرۃ discernment a lesson. There is a lesson in everything

نظرُہُ عِبرۃ و کلامُہُ حِکمَۃ

His glance is discernment and his speech is wisdom. There's a reason for why he speaks. The prophet ﷺ once saw a woman nursing her child and he said to his صحابۃ. Do you think that this woman would throw her child into the Fire? And they said لَا وَللہِ یَا رَسُولَ اللہِ . She would never do that. He said Allah has more Mercy for his servants than this woman for her child.

اللہ أرحمُ بِعِبادِہِ مِن ھٰذہِ الأُم لِوَلَدِھا

Allah has more mercy for his servants than this woman does for her child. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى said about his prophet نُوحٌ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ

> إِنَّهُۥ كَانَ عَبْدًۭا شَكُورًۭا ‎۳
>
> "O offspring of those whom We carried with Nuh !! Verily, he was a grateful slave."

People today get fed up after a week of دعوۃ. You know these people they'll never listen. I mean, this is 950 years doing what Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى asked him to do. That's شکور. One of the things mentioned in سیرۃ is that نُوحٌ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ never once went to relieve himself, except that he came back saying اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ الذی اذھب عنی الازا; praise be to the one who removed this harm from me. Even just having شکر for something like relieving himself. Cause what a blessing that is!

You know, بحلول once went to visit ھَارُونُ رَشِیدٌ. He asked ھَارُونُ الرَّشِیدُ, would you like anything? ھارون said bring me a glass of water. So they brought him water. بحلول asked: If you were refused this water, and you were dying of thirst, what would you give for this water? And he said I would give half of my kingdom for it, if I couldn't have it. He said if you drank that water and then you couldn't release it, what would you give to release it? He said - the rest of my kingdom. بحلول said, then what is the worth of your kingdom? that is not worth a cup of water, or the release of it as urine? In other words, what do you really possess? If it doesn't equal a cup of water? or the release of it as urine?

So think of that; the blessing of just eating and the fact that you're digesting it. You don't have to tell your Hydrochloric acid to be released. You don't have to push any buttons here- really! You eat something and if it's protein, then it breaks down the proteins to amino acids; and then your body reassembles them as muscle tissue; if it's fats it breaks it down to fatty acids, then your body reassembles them. If it's carbohydrates, it breaks them down, takes them to the brain, producing energy right? If you have extra, insulin is released, takes it to the liver, and stores it as glycogen so that when you're out of energy, the liver can release the glycogen, break it down to produce energy for you. You didn't have to tell the body to do any of that. And yet it's all doing it for us. اَللّٰہُ اَکْبَرُ! Islam is about being grateful to Allah. That's what Islam is!

Right now, you're at 98 degrees. Who do you think's maintaining your body at 98.6 right now? Seriously who's maintaining that? Right now I could have a fever, I could be shivering. If I was sick right now - لَا قَدَّرَ اللَّہُ - if I was sick right now, even though it's a moderate temperature in the room, I could be shivering - feeling cold and it's not even cold. If I had a fever, I could be boiling right now in a moderate climate. You'd all be fine. But if I had a fever, I would be wanting be taking off all my clothes and... really think about that! Allah is keeping us at this 98.6. Even if it's a hundred degrees outside, our body is releasing fluid in order to maintain this 98.6 on average. It's going to be around there. If it's a hundred degrees outside, why don't you boil up to a hundred degrees? What created that inner thermostat? that keeps you at that balance? If it's cold out, why don't you drop down and freeze? Because you will! If you go out in ice-cold temperatures for long enough, you'll freeze. But for a period of time you won't. You'll stay at 98.6 - why? You can go swimming in cold water, and your temperature will maintain itself. That's all from Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

The شکور is constantly aware of the abundance of these blessings of the الرحمن. But then they also look at those that are less fortunate than them in their worldly affairs. They draw gratitude out of that! There is a HADEES in which Rasul Allah ﷺ said about دنیا that you should look at people who are less than you in دنیا because you'll always feel grateful. There's always people less than you. I guarantee you! No matter how miserable you think you are, you'll always find people in much more misery than you are right now. So you say اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ. I mean if you don't have a lot of money. In this country, we're rich! In Biblical times, to put a jar of honey on your table was a sign of great wealth. A jar of honey on your table was a sign of great wealth. Really! The majority of people living in America would be considered royalty in any previous time. They would be considered living in palaces.

Just the idea of having running water in this country. In the 1930's still about 70% of Americans did not have running water. The idea of having running water. The fact that you can take a hot shower just by turning a knob. People don't even think about this. There are places in the world that وللہ, there are women who have the sole job of of bringing water every day. It takes some about two hours to go get the water. It takes about an hour to fill it up and it takes them two hours to come back. Five hours every day, just to bring drinking water to their village! and that's their life. That's their entire life. They have to load the donkey's right? with the قِربا . Can you imagine what they must feel when they drink water? وللہ this الْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ لِنِعْمَۃِ الْمَاءِ. Their water - it's brown. It's brown. Most of them have kidney problems. Most of them have urinary problems; because of the sediment in the water. People have forgotten this. We could have been born in that village, drinking that brown water, having those kidney problems. We never think about these things!

Just think about the نعمۃ of the houses we live in. Think about that نعمۃ of water; the نعمۃ of food on our table; the نعمۃ of clothes to cover our nakedness, to keep us warm; the نعمۃ of a bed. The نعمۃ of just being healthy, I mean نِعْمَۃُ الْعَافِیَۃِ. that you got up this morning and you weren't sick. Think of how many people got up this morning, they had to empty out a bag that had their urine in it. A bag that they have to change every day. Think of how many people got up this morning that couldn't go to the toilet or that bled blood. Think of how many people with asthma, that couldn't breathe this morning; that woke up gasping for air - really! Think of how many people that spent their night coughing last night. They couldn't sleep because of a cough. Really! That's why - I mean just شکر, showing gratitude for Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

I mean - you have to wonder about our lives. Just the blessings that الرحمن has bestowed upon us and our complaints!! We say - what did I do to deserve this? You exist! That's is enough as a crime. Really! If you really think about it, that's enough as a crime to think that you exist, beside Allah?! To think that we share existence with Allah! We are a criminal. Because لَا اِلَہَ اِلَّا اللَّہُ. There's nothing worthy of worship, but Allah; and yet here we are walking around thinking - these little contingent creatures, living on this insignificant planet, in an insignificant galaxy, that are totally dependent for every breath at every instant on Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى, and we think we are independent. We think we are self-sufficient; we thinks we're in control. If I'm in such a state of heedlessness with Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى, I'm a criminal. If I forget Allah for one instant, I am a criminal and I stand condemned; and anything that happens to me, I deserve it! because I've forgotten who I am and who Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is.

> ‏يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلْإِنسَنُ مَا غَرَّكَ بِرَبِّكَ ٱلْكَرِيمِ ‎۶
>
> O man! What has made you careless concerning your Lord, the Most Generous?

What has deluded you about your master? What has deluded you about your Lord? Why are you in a state of غرور? Total delusion? Thinking that you don't need me at every instant of your life? Just to take the next breath? To have the next heartbeat? What's a heartbeat? It's an electrical impulse? Who's activating it? Its Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. You think it's your brain? It's not your brain. Your brain is not doing that. They don't even know what's doing it. You can cut all of your nerves to the brain and the heart still beats. Right? what makes the heart beat?

And yet here were are - these insignificant creatures complaining about Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. Judging him for his faults نَعُوذُ بِاللَّہِ. That's a crime! Or we sit on our high horses, right next him, judging others. Our علماء looking down upon others based on their knowledge, that Allah has blessed them with. That's not why Allah gave you that knowledge - to look down on others. He created us all as brothers - to embrace one another. See there's two ways of embracing. There's the embrace of an equal and then there's the embrace of a superior. Did you ever think about that? What's the Embrace of an equal? You Embrace somebody who's the same as you. Like if I embrace you, you're my brother, we have an equal embrace. I put my arms around you you put your arms around me. What's the embrace of a superior to an inferior? The child! The child can't put his arms around you. You pick them up and you embrace them; and you hold them. See that's Islam! The مزاج , the temperament of Islam is to take all those other sects and religions; and those who are inferior in knowledge, and just embrace them.

That's the embrace of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. He is so great, that He will even allow somebody that denies him to exist. I mean that's greatness! That's رحمۃ. He'll feed him! nurture him! Give him rain. Keep his body healthy. Give him a wife, children that love him. Allah will do all of that, even though that person denies his very existence. All that does is it shows the greatness of God.

There is a story of اِبْرَاھِیمُ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ . One night - he never ate alone - ever. He always found somebody to eat with. But one night he couldn't find anybody, so he went out looking for someone and finally found a man and he brought him home. He was a مشرک. And before the man ate he blessed his food in the names of his idols. And when اِبْرَاھِیمُ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ heard that he said you can't eat any of my food. You're an idol worshiper, Get out of here! The man left and then Allah revealed to اِبْرَاھِیمُ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ that man - I have been feeding him for 40 years and I've been patient with his idolatry for 40 years. You couldn't be patient with him for one meal? And so اِبْرَاھِیمُ عَلَیْہِ السَّلَامُ went and found that man and apologized to him and invited him back to his table. The man asked him why and he told him in the Man became Muslim. That's Islam.

We can embrace them. See دعوۃ, what is دعوۃ in Arabic? What does that word mean? it's an invitation. Now if I put a gun to your head and say you're coming to dinner tonight at my house - is that an invitation? Seriously? It's not is it? You're not inviting someone, you're forcing them to come - لَا اِکْرَاہَ فِی الدِّینِ. An invitation is only valid if you allow the person the possibility of refusing the invitation. Now who would allow the person that possibility? A generous host? Allah has made a house, and he called it دَارُ الْاِسْلَامِ. he invited his creation to it; and he gave them the option to refuse to come to the house. So if people refuse it, why are we so fearful of them? Why are we so afraid of those people? We should have no fear. See the thing about people that are ignorant, they fear! That's what a fundamentalist is. It is somebody who's afraid of the other. He's afraid of having his ایمان challenged. He's so afraid that he won't even allow for the other to exist. He can't. He's not going to allow that possibility. He wants them eliminated. Really! and that's a disease. That's why Islam is the only religion that's ever allowed other religions, other ideologies, to exist side by side with it.

### Praise

One of the great scholars of Islam said that the door that he came to his Lord through was تَعْظِیمُ الْمَخْلُوقَاتِ exalting the creation of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. He said

ما رأیت عالماً الا عَظَّمتُہُ لعَلمِہِ الذی یَحمِلُہُ

I never saw a scholar except I exalted him because of the knowledge that he was carrying. But then it got to a point where

ما رأیتُ مؤمناً او مسلماً مَھْمَا کان حالُہُ الَّا عظَّمتَہُ لی أِنتِسابِہِ العُبُودِیَۃِ اللہ

I never saw a believer or a Muslim man, no matter what his state was, but I exalted him just for being a servant of Allah. Attempting to submit to Allah. And then he said but then I didn't see anybody even the non-muslims

الا عَظَّمتَہُ لِأَنَّہُ مَحَلَّ الاِرَادَۃِ اللہ سبحانہ و تعالی حَیثُ أَوجَدُہُ بِقَولِہِ کُن فَیَکُون

except that I exalted him because in his existence I saw the will of the Divine manifesting before my eyes. Then he said I never saw a rock or a tree or anything, except that I saw it, as a manifestation of the Divine will. It was God that put that thing there. That's what he was acknowledging and then he said

وَمَن یُعَظِّمْ حُرُماتِ اللہ فَھُوَ خَیرُ لَّہُ عِندَ رَبِّہ

The one who exalts the حرمات the sanctified things of God, he is the one most loved by Allah; and what is more sanctified in this عالم than the creation of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. These thing that Allah has created as his تجلی his Divine manifestations that invite us all to marvel at it. Praise it. They draw out from within us the praise component of the حمد.

If you look at people - when you see people who can do something amazing. You see this beautiful singer singer who sings a song, or plays an instrument in a concert - what do people do? They clap, they whistle; they do all these things. They give that artist an ovation - to praise him or her - that is because of what's happening in their hearts. They see something extraordinary and they want to praise that thing. Because they just saw something that affected their soul. So when they see somebody who's mastered the piano, for instance and he gets up and play some extraordinary piece by Beethoven, they all stand up, and they clap and clap to say what an extraordinary thing you've just done before our eyes! It was truly incredibly! That's what they feel like doing because it's so amazing.

And yet we're in this incredible existence; this theater of amazing creation - stars in the heavens; galaxies of them! We've got Hubble telescopes that look at the far corners of the universe; into his massive expansiveness. We have flowers that bloom before our eyes. We can watch flowers that move across as the sun moves across the sky - flowers that follow the Sun - the Arabs call them عَبَّادُ الشَّمْسِ. We have animals that can do the most extraordinary thing. Elephants that can carry extraordinary weight on their backs, but then also these ants - even more extraordinary in their creation - that can carry many times their own weight. All of these things are happening right before our eyes. We have women with a fetus of a baby developing in their wombs - mitotic cell division happening at rapid rates, all of these things are happening everywhere - and we don't feel compelled to give Allah a standing ovation?! To stand up before Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى and say Allah Allahu Akbar Allahu Akbar Allahu Akbar. Really! to stand In ovation before our Lord, like people stand before some poor soul who has perfected some little trick on a musical instrument; who can pipe a tune out - and were compelled to jump up and clap and say Bravo Bravo! and we can't get up to praise the master of the worlds, the الرحمن and الرحیم and say Allah Akbar? What is wrong with us?!

مَاغَرَّکَ بِرَبِّ الْکَرِیمِ

what has deluded you? What has taken you away from the praise of your master? Our Prophet constantly went into سجدۃ out of gratitude. Our Prophet when he saw an eclipse, he went and and gave God an ovation. He prayed! he stood up and prayed; and went into سجدۃ as a testimony that he was witnessing a reminder; but these reminders are in every instant.

May Allah strengthen our hearts to witness, experience and marvel over the blessings of his رحمنیۃ and his رحیمیۃ and may he help us experience the joy of true and sincere praise and unconditional gratitude in our hearts for not only every thing that he has done for us; but also for what he has withheld from us. For not only the joy in our lives, but also for the grief and sorrows that humble us and pull us off our high horse and bring us down to the earth.

## Day Of Judgment - Linguistics

In the last few sessions we completed the discussion on الحمد للہ رب العلمین and اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ اَلرَّحِیمِ, both of which are a testimony to Allah's abundant and consistent love and grace, by virtue of him being the master of everything known in creation. For that he is owed الحمد which is sincere praise and unconditional gratitude. Today we're going to look at the next verse مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ.

Linguistics

There are two forms of recitation for this verse. The کوفی recite مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ whereas some other schools recite مَلِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ, and both forms are valid. So let's look at both variations.

### Owner and King

The word مالک means owner, and it comes from three root letters: مِیمٌ لَامٌ کَافٌ or مِلک which means property. So the one who owns property is the مالک of that property. Now there is another word in Arabic that shares these same root letters, which is مُلک and it means kingdom. The one who rules over a kingdom is called a مَلِک a king. Allah talks about both in the Quran. There is مِلکیۃ and there is مُلکیۃ. The word مِلکیۃ means ownership and مُلکیۃ means kingship, and Allah ascribes both these attributes to himself. He is not only the owner, he is also the king, because he's the the one who judges, who decrees, who legislates, who has the ultimate authority over everything in his kingdom. There is no one above the king that a plaintiff can plead his case with. So Allah says.

> ‏وَمَا قَدَرُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ حَقَّ قَدْرِهِۦ وَٱلْأَرْضُ جَمِيعًۭا قَبْضَتُهُۥ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَمَةِ وَٱلسَّمَوَتُ مَطْوِيَّتٌۢ بِيَمِينِهِۦ ۚ سُبْحَنَهُۥ وَتَعَلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ ‎۶۷
>
> They did not perform a just estimation of Allah such as was due to Him. And on the Day of Resurrection the whole of the earth will be grasped by His Hand and the heavens will be rolled up in His Right Hand. Glorified is He, and High is He above all that they associate as partners with Him!

Now what does he mean by having it in his right hand. It means it'll be under his control. This is مِلکیۃ - complete ownership.

In another verse he says

> ‏قُلِ ٱللَّهُمَّ مَلِكَ ٱلْمُلْكِ تُؤْتِى ٱلْمُلْكَ مَن تَشَآءُ وَتَنزِعُ ٱلْمُلْكَ مِمَّن تَشَآءُ وَتُعِزُّ مَن تَشَآءُ وَتُذِلُّ مَن تَشَآءُ ۖ بِيَدِكَ ٱلْخَيْرُ ۖ إِنَّكَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ قَدِيرٌۭ ‎۲۶
>
> Say :"O Allah! Possessor of the kingdom, You give the kingdom to whom You will, and You take the kingdom from whom You will, and You endue with honour whom You will, and You humiliate whom You will. In Your Hand is the good. Verily, You are Able to do all things.

Declare that Allah is the king of all kingdoms - مَلِكَ ٱلْمُلْكِ. Here he talks about him being the king. So both forms are used in the Quran. He is the owner of the day of judgment; and he is also the king of the day of judgment - both having the implication that nobody will be able to question him about his decisions; and he alone will pass all judgments and make the final decisions.

### Period

The next word is یوم which is quite an interesting word in Arabic. مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ. Generally this word is translated as a day - Owner of the day of judgment, but linguistically یوم does not actually mean a 24 hour day. It simply means a time interval. It means any period of time. So a یوم could be used for a moment; it could mean an hour, it could mean a day, a month, a year; and it could also mean several thousand years. It is simply a duration of time allocated for a specific purpose. So for instance:

> ‏تَعْرُجُ ٱلْمَلَٓئِكَةُ وَٱلرُّوحُ إِلَيْهِ فِى يَوْمٍۢ كَانَ مِقْدَارُهُۥ خَمْسِينَ أَلْفَ سَنَةٍۢ ‎۴

The angels and the Spirit ascend to Him during a یوم the extent of which is fifty thousand years. Here Allah specifies یوم will as being 50,000 years. In Surah مزمل he says.

> ‏يَوْمَ تَرْجُفُ ٱلْأَرْضُ وَٱلْجِبَالُ وَكَانَتِ ٱلْجِبَالُ كَثِيبًۭا مَّهِيلًا ‎۱۴
>
> On the Day when the earth and the mountains will be in violent shake, and the mountains will be a heap of sand poured out and flowing down.

That **moment **when the earth and the mountains will convulse, and mountains will turn into a heap of sand being poured down. Same word یوم is used here for a moment. So the word یوم is used for various periods of time, including time itself. In سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ we have

مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ

Owner or king of the "time" or the "period" of دین.

### Debt

Now let's look at the word دین. The word دین in the Arabic language comes from the root letters دَیْن which has several meanings. One of the meanings of دَیْن is a type of rain that falls again and again, until it brings the dead earth back to life.

Another meaning is to discipline something مَنْ دَانَ نَفْسَہُ whoever disciplines his soul. It also means a way of life لَکُمْ دِینُکُمْ وَلِیَدِینِ You have your way of life and we have ours. But the root meaning of the word دَیْن actually means debt. Our religion is called دِینُ الْاِسْلَامِ. The دین of Islam. Literally this can be translated as the **debt of submission**. In other words, the idea is that we have been endowed with innumerable, invaluable blessings by our master; and so for all those blessings we owe our creator a debt of submission. Allah says:

أَفَغَيْرَ دِينِ ٱللَّهِ يَبْغُونَ

Do they seek other than the دین of Allah?

وَلَهُۥٓ أَسْلَمَ مَن فِى ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ طَوْعًۭا وَكَرْهًۭا

While all creatures in the heaven and the earth have bowed down to him in submission - willingly or unwillingly. So what Allah is asking here, is that, do they seek other than repaying this debt? When in reality everyone and everything in existence is paying its debt to its creator. They are in a state of willing or unwilling submission.

Now there are areas of submission that the human being recognizes. One of them is our call to nature, right? We have to do certain things that we cannot avoid. One of them is to eat. That is part of our nature. When we get hungry we must eat, or we die. We need to drink water when we are thirsty. If we don't drink, we die. We have to relieve ourselves once we have eaten or taken drink. We don't have a choice in these matters - we have to do them whether we want to or not. We have to sleep. When sleep comes upon us, we can fight it for a while, but eventually we have to submit - or we go into acute psychosis. So there is this idea of willing or unwilling submission to nature itself.

But then there is also submission to our own beings - to our physical bodies. We have limitations. We can eat, but there is only so much we can eat. We can only drink so much. We can only do so much physical work before we're exhausted. We have limitations in our movements because of the structure of our bodies. So there is a type of submission of the self, given that we are temporal creatures **limited **by the physical aspects of our nature.

Now the Arabic word for a boundary, or a limit, is حد. So all of creation have حدود; we are all confined within our limits. Water cannot flow upwards; a rock cannot fly; a bird cannot bark; a dog cannot sing; honey will always taste sweet; citrus will always taste sour. So in the Quran there is this idea of limitations - that everything is in submission to its limitations; every creature is confined within its boundaries - it's حدود; willingly or unwillingly - طَوْعًۭا وَكَرْهًۭا. In other words, they're all in a state of submission; and then Allah follows that statement with a reminder that they will all be returned back to their owner

وَإِلَيْهِ يُرْجَعُونَ

and to him is their return.
If you deeply reflect upon creation, you'll see that things are emerging, and then they are returning into nonexistence. Everything that has life comes into existence, and then returns back into non-existence. The plants, the vegetables, the animal kingdom, and ultimately the mineral kingdom - they're all returning to nonexistence after they came into existence. In other words, everything that is in existence, are paying back their debt to their creator, **except** the human beings. You see? It is **only** human beings who are not actively paying back their debts of submission - their دِینُ الْاِسْلَامِ.

But the difference here is that they are also not being asked about their debts right now. So when will they be asked? And who will ask them?

مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ

The owner and the king of that time period when that debt of submission falls due. So یَوْمُ الدِّیْنِ is the time when the debt of submission falls due. Its payback time.

کیف ما تُدِینُ تُدان

How you transact with others is the way you will be transacted with. It is also called یَوْمُ الْحِسَابِ the day of accountability. Rasul Allah ﷺ says in a HADEES:

حاسِبُ قبل اَن تُحاسَبُ

Take yourself to account before you are taken to account.

So there is a debt of submission that we owe Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. But then there is also a debt that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى will owe us on that day. See, when the master issues a command to his slave, the fulfillment of that command is a debt that the slave owes his master. Now there are only two possibilities. He either obeys the command, or he disobeys it. In both cases, a return or a recompense falls due upon his master. If the slave obeyed his master and executed his commands properly, his master now owes him a reward. And if he disobeyed his masters command, the master now owes him punishment. That's یَوْمُ الدِّیْنِ. The day when debts fall due. This is why Allah calls upon people to owe him a debt. He says in the Quran.

مَّن ذَا ٱلَّذِى يُقْرِضُ ٱللَّهَ قَرْضًا حَسَنًۭا

Who will in-debt Allah with a beautiful debt. Allah is asking us for a loan. Why?

فَيُضَعِفَهُۥ لَهُۥٓ أَضْعَافًۭا كَثِيرَةًۭ

So that Allah multiplies it many many times over, and return it back to his slave? In this verse Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is asking his slaves - who among you will give me a loan; who among you will in-debt me with his willing submission of عبودیۃ, so that I can multiply its reward many many times over and pay them back?

وَلَهُۥٓ أَجْرٌۭ كَرِيمٌۭ

For he has the most noble rewards. SubhanAllah! Even on the day he is مالک he is full of love and mercy.

### An Unjust World

Now let's look at some of the implications of this verse. The most important aspect of this verse is that it addresses this notion of an "unjust" world. Many people have given up on faith and the existence of a creator based on the injustices they see in this world. They use that as proof against God. Either based on an injustice that happened to them, or an injustice that happened to another. Their argument is that if god is so loving and caring, then how come he let all of this happen?

Now what's interesting is that no where in the Quran, Allah claims or promises to give us a good life in this world. In fact, what he promises every human being is the very opposite. He says:

> ‏وَلَنَبْلُوَنَّكُم بِشَىْءٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلْخَوْفِ وَٱلْجُوعِ وَنَقْصٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلْأَمْوَلِ وَٱلْأَنفُسِ وَٱلثَّمَرَتِ ۗ وَبَشِّرِ ٱلصَّبِرِينَ ‎۱۵۵

And no doubt; surely we shall test you with fear, with hunger, with loss of wealth and life, and children, and crops. وَبَشِّرِ ٱلصَّبِرِينَ And for the patient ones are glad tidings.

> ‏تَبَرَكَ ٱلَّذِى بِيَدِهِ ٱلْمُلْكُ وَهُوَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ قَدِيرٌ ‎۱‏ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ ٱلْمَوْتَ وَٱلْحَيَوٰةَ لِيَبْلُوَكُمْ أَيُّكُمْ أَحْسَنُ عَمَلًۭا ۚ وَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلْغَفُورُ ‎۲
>
> Blessed is He in Whose Hand has the ultimate dominion, and is able to do all things.Who who created death and life to test you, and see which of you is the best in action - and He is the one most exalted in might and is extremely forgiving;

So this world was never meant for justice. This life is a test and this world we live in is an examination hall; and everyone is being tested. Some with poverty, some with wealth; some with sickness, others with health. In fact the healthy and the rich are going through a much more difficult test than the sick and the poor; because they will be held accountable for these gifts. I gave you all this, show me what you did with it. It's no surprise that the greatest man to walk the face of this earth - Rasul Allah ﷺ chose poverty over riches.

So this world is imp

Now if I asked you to describe the perfect world to me - what kind of a world would you design? Think about it? What components would your perfect world comprise of? There is complete justice? Perfect harmony? No one in the world would sleep hungry? There would be no disease, no pain, no suffering? Everybody is treated justly. Love and peace all over the world. There would be no ugliness in the world - just beauty. People would love and help each other. The lion and the deer would drink peacefully from the same river? Isn't that the perfect world you'd dream of? What did I just describe to you? I just described to you the design of جنۃ. Isn't it? Isn't all of this exactly the description of paradise?

This world is simply one side of the equation. The problem with someone who is only seeing this world, is that he's looking at just one side of the equation; and there are a bunch of imbalances on this side. If you do not believe in the day of judgment or a day of accountability, then you are left with a world that is completely unfair. But if you believe in a day of judgment, all the injustices on this side of the equation, get balanced with perfect justice on the other side of the equation.

So in this verse مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ, Allah talks about this transition that will take place from this world to the next; hence we see this transition in الفاتحۃ from اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ اَلرَّحِیمِ to مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ. There is a transition from ربوبیۃ from the love, care, concern and mercy of Allah in this world, to the one, in which he is the owner and the king of the day when the debt of submission falls due. There verse makes a transition from this دنیا to the آخرۃ.

mercy precedes his wrath

## Motivating Factors For Piety

In the previous session we learnt that مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ linguistically means - the owner or the king of the time period when the debt of submission falls due. We talked about the imperfections of this world, necessitating the idea of a perfect world which is the hereafter.

Today we'll to look at this verse in some more detail and try to understand its purpose and relevance in this سورۃ - especially from the point of view of this transition that takes place. We saw that from the beginning of this سورۃ, Allah's love and mercy was being highlighted

بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ - I begin with the lofty symbol of Allah pointing to his love and mercy

الْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِینَ - all sincere praise and unconditional gratitude is due to Allah for his mastery or lordship over everything that can be known in existence

الرحمن الرحیم - the abundantly merciful, the consistently merciful

So Allah's love was being highlighted in this سورۃ, and now we suddenly see the tone change to مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ. Owner of the time when the debt falls due. Why this sudden change in tone?

Scholars explain that this transition is extremely profound, and its purpose is to engender the desire for تقوی in the hearts. It is what the Arabs call الباعث, which is what stimulates you to do something. It's the motivating factor in something. Because if you think about it, if love is not balanced with some system of accountability, it is a human tendency to take advantage of that kindness. If you know anyone in your lives who is just love love love all the time; who will let people get away with murder, you'll see that as much as that person might be loved, they will never be taken seriously. In fact, they will be taken advantage of, isn't it? So understanding this weakness in human nature, Allah changes his tone to remind us, that even though I'm a very merciful and loving God, you better take me seriously. Don't try to take undue advantage of my love; because I am also your creator, your owner and your king; and I have set a time period, where I will take you to account for your debt of submission. So what this does, is one - it creates a sense of accountability; and two- it creates a sense of urgency to make the most of our remaining time; because no one know when and where he's going to die, except that he is inevitably going to die someday.

> وَمَا تَدْرِى نَفْسٌۭ مَّاذَا تَكْسِبُ غَدًۭا ۖ وَمَا تَدْرِى نَفْسٌۢ بِأَىِّ أَرْضٍۢ تَمُوتُ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلِيمٌ خَبِيرٌۢ ‎۳۴
>
> And no soul knows what it will earn tomorrow, and no soul perceives in what land it will die. Indeed, Allah is Knowing and Acquainted.

So the inevitability of death, and this idea of returning back to the creator for accountability creates this awareness of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى in our hearts which is called تقوی. The word تقوی comes from وِقَیَ which means a strong sense of fearful awareness. Therefore تقوی is the awareness of Allah's constant presence in our lives.

Scholars have stated that what this verse مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ does, is that it motivates us towards تقوی, in a number of ways.

### Fear Of Punishment

The first is a fear of punishment in this world. This is a worldly type of retribution which is عقاب. Human nature is such, that we tend to fear pain. In fact, fear of pain is probably a greater immediate concern than a desire for pleasure. So when given two choices, and the possibility of one or the other is equal, people will choose to avoid pain over the choice of acquiring pleasure.

They did a study in the US, which I think was a 30-year study. It was very interesting study, where they they took these five year old kids, and they offered them a cookie. They said you can have this cookie right now or if you wait 10 minutes you can have two cookies. So the children were very different in their choices. Well, what they did is they monitored these kids over a 30-year period, and they found almost consistently, that the children that delayed their gratification for a greater reward were far more successful in their lives, than the ones who wanted it immediately. It was a very interesting study. So the delay of gratification - some temporary deprivation for the hope of something greater, is very important. And this delay, is the basis of a lot of what's going on in the Quran; this idea of delaying gratification - because the people of دنیا they desire instant gratification.

> ‏كَلَّا بَلْ تُحِبُّونَ ٱلْعَاجِلَةَ ‎۲۰‏وَتَذَرُونَ ٱلْآخِرَةَ ‎۲۱
>
> ( 20 ) Not, but love the present life of this world, ( 21 ) And leave the Hereafter.

You put off what's coming and you want this immediate pleasure. We fall into lust. It's an immediate pleasure; it's an immediate satisfaction; and we often end up sacrifice doing the right thing to gain that immediate pleasure; and we don't think of the repercussions. But that's the way we are.

So مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ is essentially what the scholars say, خَوْفُ الْعِقَابِ الْأُخْرَوِیِّ which is fear of punishment in the next world. One of the things about the Islamic; and essentially all Abrahamic traditions, is that there is an idea that just as you have physics you also have metaphysics. So physics are laws that we can discern from the palpable world, right? This world that we can touch and feel; it has laws. There's laws of cause and effects. If you do something in the world - every action has a reaction. So if I hit this table, there is going to be a sound. If there was no sound - there's a suspension of a physical law; and we'd have to look into it; of why that's happened. Because according to the laws of physics, my hitting this table should produce a sound. If you jump off a building, there's going to be an effect from that cause. In the same way, there's metaphysical laws. There are laws of cause and effect that transcend the physical world, and move into the spiritual world; or the psychic world. If I shout at you there's a psychic affect. Where are our emotions? We don't really truly understand emotions because they're part of our consciousness. But if I shout at you, you feel something inside. You either become depressed, or you become angry - that's coming from another realm! that's not a physical realm. That's in the realm of the emotions. So just as there are physical effects, there's also spiritual effects.

So for instance the Arabs call something مَاءُ الْوَجْہِ. which literally means water of the face. But the idea is that it's a kind of purity or impurity that people have on their face. You'll see that people who have sinned a lot, their evil begins to show up on their faces; and you get a type of metaphysical darkness in the face. Similarly there are people that do a lot of good deeds, their faces are affected by that. You see this luminance, or a brightness on their face. The Arabs used to call a face the الصحیفۃ which means the register; because it registers everything. Somebody once said: by 50, everybody has the face they deserve. If you're 50 years old, you have the face you deserve. If you've had a good life, it's going to show up on your face. So that fear of another worldly punishment is one of the things that will compel a person to do good and to stay within boundaries.

### Desiring Benefit

The next motivating factor for تقوی is desiring a benefit. That's the next one. So people also desire benefits. This is at the most basic human foundation - stick and carrot. This is how children are disciplined - stick and carrot. It's how society works - stick and carrot. Really! much of society is based on these principles; and that's why the highest people; the most intelligent people are the ones that transcend these stick and carrot factors and they move into another realm. People like Maulana Ali who often said in his prayers:

یا الھی ما عبدتک بشوق جنتک ولا من خوف نارک بل وجۃ مستحقا لعبادۃ فلعبدتک

‏My Master, I do not worship you out of a desire for your paradise; nor do I worship you out of fear of your hellfire. Instead I truly find you worthy of my worship, and for that reason alone I worship you.

This is a very high station of worship that completely transcends the carrot and stick philosophy. But the majority of people are at this base level; and because the Quran speaks to all of humanity, it talks in this general manner. The prophet said: move according to the the level of the least amongst you. So you have to move with the least amongst you. You don't speed up and and leave people behind. No! That's the way Islam. This is why if you carefully study this religion, you'll see that most of it is designed to protect the weakest people. There are people who can stand alone - they don't have a problem; but there are also people that can't - they just have human weaknesses. And so the شریعۃ protects those people. This is why the Quran speaks against things like gambling. The Quran says that there's good in gambling.

يَسْـَٔلُونَكَ عَنِ ٱلْخَمْرِ وَٱلْمَيْسِرِ ۖ قُلْ فِيهِمَآ إِثْمٌۭ كَبِيرٌۭ وَمَنَفِعُ لِلنَّاسِ وَإِثْمُهُمَآ أَكْبَرُ مِن نَّفْعِهِمَا

They ask you concerning alcoholic and gambling. Tell them: "In them is a great sin, and also benefit for men, but their sin is far greater than their benefit."

So Quran says that there's some good in gambling. There's lessons to be learned in gambling. Risk is an interesting phenomenon in commerce. There's a strong relationship between gambling and commerce. But the harms of gambling are far greater than its benefits. It's a known social science fact that around 3% of people that go into a gambling casino becomes addicted. It destroys their lives. That's why if you go to gambling casino here - I've been inside a casino at Atlantic City many years ago. It was one of the most frightening things. It was like a circle out of Dante's Inferno and you just see all these people; in this zombie like seats, just pulling these levers; just putting in quarters one after the other and pulling this lever. It was a scary sight. There was a pawn shop in the middle of this casino and it said, we take wedding rings! It's just so sinister. What kind of people are these? How would you justify a job like that - just depriving people in that manner?! but that's the reality of our world. Gambling is based on the carrot. It's a carrot phenomenon. Gamblers think that if I put the money in, and pull the lever; and it's all designed so that the winning happens enough times to keep you there. So when people leave, they say, oh I made such and such - they never tell you how much they lost. They always tell you how much they won. So that's the phenomenon of the the carrot. It's used a lot and that's one of the worst things about it. But then obviously, for the worldly things, those things that are pleasurable; are always followed by a far greater pain. That's just the design of it. This is why our prophet ﷺ

حُفَّۃُ الجَنَّۃ بِمَکارِہ و حُفَّۃُ النارُ بالشھوات

Paradise was surrounded by distasteful things and the fire was surrounded by delightful things, pleasurable things. So if you're walking towards paradise, you will inevitable encounter distasteful things. It's just part of the design. So now it's for the عاقل; the person of intellect to recognize this and that's why the Quran frequently talks about تدبر which means to reflect deeply on something. Allah frequently asks us: اَفَلَا تَدَبَّرُونَ. Why don't you reflect deeply? The Quran uses that word a lot - reflecting. The word تدبر comes from a root which means to take something to its end. دبر is the behind of something. It's the end. ادبار is what's behind you. So تدبر is going through something until you get to the end. That's why when you have تدبر as a quality, you look down the road. Where as this person who's stuck in this عاجلۃ; this haste of instant gratification, doesn't get beyond the present.

### Divine vs Demonic Experience

There is this theory in theology about a divine experience vs a demonic experience. And the way you differentiate between a true divine experience, and a demonic experience, is that a demonic experience, is always accompanied with an expansion of the self. In other words it is a very exciting and joyful experience, but then it is always followed with despair. On the contrary, a true spiritual experience is exactly the opposite. The initial experience is incredible constriction and fear and dread; but then it is followed by a sense of expansion and joy.

### Day Of Reckoning

The next motivating factor of تقوی is that this verse provides the grounds for the fear of a reckoning - that you're going to be reckoned on the day of judgment. And so the fear of Reckoning should instill in you a desire to do good. You see, anybody who has a business - they have what's called internal audits. If you have a business and you live in a country like the US or England, where they have Revenue Services; these are agencies that will come, and they'll examine your businesses. And if you've if you've been fudging the books you get penalties. It's not just that you pay your back taxes. They add penalties and interest and they do all these things. So companies that are worried about that external reckoning or auditing by the Revenue Service; they do an internal audit. So if there's anything that needs correcting they correct it internally. Well that's called ‏مُحاسَبَۃ and it's part of تقوی where you actually take yourself to account. The HADEES says

حاسِبو قبل ان تُحاسَبُ

Take yourself to account before you're taken to account.

### Shame & Modesty

The next benefit of this verse مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ is that it creates the concept of shame or modesty from the awareness of God - that Allah is aware of you actions; that Allah is watching you. So we should have this shame or modesty before Allah. The word الحیاءُ in Arabic, is probably better translated as modesty which is related to shame. There's a sense, that you don't want to do something in front of a person or in front of your lord, out of love and respect. There are certain things children won't do in front of their parents out of respect. The prophet ﷺ said:

لِکُلِّ دینٍ خُلُق و خُلُق الاسلام الحیاء

Every religion has a characteristic, and the characteristic of my religion is modesty.

Anthropologist divide cultures into two types: **shame** cultures and **guilt** cultures. Western cultures tend to fall into the guilt culture. A guilt culture cultivates in people, moral components inside of themselves, where if they do something wrong, they feel bad about it - on the inside. And so one of the things a lot of people note about people in the west is that they will often find it very difficult to do wrong things, without feeling bad. In fact, there's a whole industry in America to help people overcome guilt. Self-help books and self-improvement courses; there's a whole industry that capitalizes on this idea of helping people overcome guilt. So in guilt cultures, there's an internal mechanisms.

In shame cultures, the mechanisms are external. And so people don't do wrong out of fear of others. They don't do wrong out of fear of others; which generally creates hypocrisy in cultures. India and Pakistan, and many Asian countries are very much rooted in a shame culture; where people don't have an internal component; and they're controlled only by external factors. So when such a person is in his country, he finds it shameful to do certain things, but when he goes to another country, it's no longer shameful to do those same things - they start doing them and they don't feel anything inside. This is why a lot of kids from India and Pakistan when they come to US or the West, they go completely crazy. They drink, party, do drugs - even though they never did those things when they were back home. It just becomes completely acceptable, because they're no longer in the culture where to do that thing was shameful. Also, you get the anonymity of a different country. When people move into different countries or environments, because back home, everybody knows you; they know your parent; so if you do anything, the entire community is going to hear about it. In a different city or a different country there is an anonymity. So it's difficult to sense that shame; which is why guilt cultures are very important in advanced societies. Shame works very well at the tribal level. It works very well in small cultures; but when you move to advanced cultures, it breaks down.

Now Islam is rooted in a shame culture. But what it does, is that it transcends the shame that is rooted in people; and it grounds it in shame before God. So it's not so much of an internal factor; it's an external factor, meaning it's more shame-based; but it has to do with this modesty, this حیاء before God and before his angels. Because you have two angels on on each side. So the idea is that you become aware of the spiritual presence; that then prevents you from doing wrong things. This is called مَقَامُ الْمُرَاقَبَۃِ which is a very high station. It is the station of vigilance or awareness of God's presence, based on his word

إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ عَلَيْكُمْ رَقِيبًۭا

Allah sees everything and is aware of everything

There's this very interesting story. Over a 100 years ago when the British ruled India, there lived a pick-pocket. He was Muslim, but he earned his living by picking pockets of people. In fact, he was a member of a gang who did this. One day he picked the pocket of an Englishman; and the wallet had a lot of money in it. He was quite happy initially, but then he thought of something and went back and returned the wallet with all the money to that Englishman. That night when he returned back to his gang, the leader would ask everyone how much they earned that day. When his turn came, he said, I picked the pocket of this rich English Christian man but then I decided to return it back to him. When asked why, he said, I thought that on the day of judgment, if Christ came to Rasul Allah ﷺ and said, a man of your nation picked the pocket of a man of my nation, my prophet would have to feel shame before him, on my account; and I can't be the reason to put my prophet to shame before Christ; and so I returned his wallet to him. And in his last sermon to his nation, our prophet said:

لا تُسَوِّدَ وجھی یوم القیامۃ
