Do not put me to shame on the day of judgment. When a class produces a bad result, doesn't the teacher feel ashamed? Isn't he asked, "What's the matter? Why did so many in your class fail?" Our prophet requested us in his last statement: لا تُسَوِّدَ وجھی یوم القیامۃ - My nation, do not shame me before Allah on the day of judgment.

وُہ جُو گِیت تُم نِے سُنَا نَہِیں، مِیرِی عُمر بَھر کَا رِیَاض تھَا

مِیرے دَرد کِی تھِی وُہ دَاستَاں جِسِے تُم ہَنسِی مِیں اُڑَا گَئے

Anyway, I don't know if this is a true story or not, but nevertheless it demonstrates the beautiful love that this man had for his prophet. He felt ashamed at the idea of being the reason for the prophet's shame. It was still an external factor, but it transcended the realm of people, and was rooted in a higher dimension.

### Gratitude

The next motivating factor for تقوی is gratitude over the blessings of Allah by being obedient. In other words, every blessing that you have; if God gave you sight, the gratitude for that sight is to use it for the sake of God. If he gave you hearing, the gratitude for hearing is to use it for the sake of God. If he gave you physical strength - everything should be used for what it was made for. Now Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى did not create his creation and desire disobedience from them. He enables disobedience, which is called خذلان. So Allah is the enabler, he allows disobedience to happen, but he is not content with it

لَا یُرْضِی کُفْرٌ لِعِبَادِہِ

He does not want his servants to be in a state of ingratitude; even though he allows it. So it's very important to have gratitude for the blessings by using those blessings in obedience. That is the definition of شکر. To use the gifts that Allah has given you, to perform things that are pleasing to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى.

### Knowledge

The next motivating factor is knowledge. So knowledge actually provides a reason to have تقوی. It creates piety that is based on علم. Because Allah says

إِنَّمَا يَخْشَى ٱللَّهَ مِنْ عِبَادِهِ ٱلْعُلَمَٓؤُا۟ۗ

Indeed the ones who are truly fearful of Allah are the scholars. The word خشوع is a type of تقوی that comes from a fearful awareness of God. It comes from awe before God, which is based on knowledge of who Allah is; and amongst his servants, it's the علماء that do this; and then the magnification of Allah's Majesty.

تَعْظِیمُ جَلَالِ اللَّہِ

This is called مَقَامُ الْھَیْبَۃِ which is the station of awe. مھیب is somebody that you have awe for. Like for the سلطان there's an awe that goes with it. You know, when you go visit a سلطان, there's a whole way that they have made things in order to instill awe in your hearts for the. If you go to the White House, you'll see these marine guards with guns having big bayonets. There's a reason why the bayonets on there. They could just have the gun; but the bayonet is there to instill that ھیبۃ. It's the same with the swords. It creates a type of ھیبۃ. And then you go in and there's these long corridors made of marble and stuff, and they make you walk through all these magnificent corridors with rich elegant decorations; and the purpose is to humble you; because you're about to meet the president or the prime minister or the king; and all these things kind of put you in your place, you see?

No matter where you go, they all have these same type of rituals. They're all designed to create an awe in your heart for the سلطان. Which is important. Because if the sultan does not generate this awe, then why should people obey him. This is also why traditionally علماء dressed a certain way. This is why you have the کسوۃ on the کعبۃ. Cause its a sanctified place. That black cloth - the کسوۃ illustrates the magnificence of the کعبۃ to create that awe in our hearts. Its a very important aspect of this تعظیم.

### Sincere Love

Then there is صِدْقُ الْمَحَبَّۃِ; the sincerity in one's love for God - it gives one تقوی. That's why one of the poet's مَحْمُودُ الْوَرَّاقُ said

تَعصِی الہَ وَتُظھِرُ حُبُّہُ ؛ ھذا مَحالٌ فی القِ‏یاسِ بَدِیعُ

You disobey Allah but then you claim that you love him

This is impossible and a very strange type of logic

لَو کان حُبُّکَ صَادِقاً لَطاعَتُہُ ؛ أِنَّ المُحِبَّ لِمَن یُحِبُّ مُطِیعُ

Had your love been sincere you would have been obedient

because a lover always obeys his or her beloved.

The nature of Love is that you want to obey the one you love.

وَقَائِلَۃً قَدْ سَأَلَتْ عَلَیَّ اَنَّ حَالَ عَاشِقِھَا

A woman asked about the state of the one who was in this burning passion for her. The عاشق is the one that is burning up with passion. So, she's asking this man about her lover.

للہ صفہ ولا تنقص ولا تزد

Don't stint in your description and don't exaggerate. I want to hear the truth. So he says says

لَوْکَانَ رَحْلُ الْمَوْتِ مِنْ ضَمَأٍ

If he knew he would die from thirst.

وَقَطَعَ قُفٌّ عَنْ وُرُودِ الْمَآءِ لَمْ یَرِدْ

And you said don't touch that water, he wouldn't touch it. So if he's gonna die of thirst and the woman said don't touch that water, he wouldn't touch it. He'd rather died than disobey. So the point of that, what the poet's emphasizing there, is that the nature of Love is obedience. So the more you love God the more you will obey God and that's the idea.

So these are the motivating factors of تقوی that the verse مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ alludes to. In these verses

الحمد للہ رب العلمین الرحمن الرحیم مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ

we get a complete picture of who Allah is. The next verses give us an understanding of what Allah wants from us, which we will begin exploring in the next sessions InshaAllah.

## Worship and Assistance

Then we have اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ أِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ - It is only you that we worship; or willingly enslave ourselves to. Now I want you to notice the change in tone here. All the previous verses were in the 3rd person. They were informational.

بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ With the name of Allah extremely merciful intensely merciful

الْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِینَAll praise and glory belongs to Allah who is the master of all that is known.

اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِیمِ the abundantly merciful and the consistently merciful

مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ Owner of the time when the debt of submission falls due.

These statements are all in the third person in which Allah is informing us about who he is. But then, suddenly, he changes the tone and turns it into a second-person دعا as if we're speaking to him - اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ It is only you we worship and it is only you we turn to for help. We're now in direct conversation with Allah. This is beautiful rhetoric tool in Arabic called التفات which is a sudden change in the pronoun of the speaker; or the one spoken to. Allah constantly changes the conversation from third person to first person; to second person, and then back to third person. He sometimes refers to himself using "I", sometimes he uses "We"; sometimes "him". These are all rhetoric tools to not only keep the reader alert and awake, but also a mechanism to highlight what Allah wants from us.

In the case of الفاتحۃ you can see that the tone changed to a دعا, a supplication - اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ it is **only** **you **we worship; and it is **only** **you**, we turn to for help. Who is the one saying that? It is you and I that are saying that. We are now in direct conversation with Allah. So this style of switching to second person, is a means of letting us know that this is in fact what Allah wants from us. Do you understand? When we say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ, Allah is expressing his intention of wanting us to say - that we willingly worship you and we freely giving up our freedom and desires to become your slaves. Allah does not command us into slavery by saying أِسْتِعْبَادُ أَنْفُسِكُمْ لِي. Enslave yourselves to me! Instead he wants us to say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ it is only you **we want **to worship. In other words, Allah is asking us to reach this conclusion of عبودیۃ on our own, through the study of the previous 3 verses. When we truly understand الْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِینَ; when we truly understand اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِیمِ; and when we truly understand مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ; when we recognize what an incredibly loving and merciful and powerful رب Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is, our natural and logical conclusion has to be اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ, you see? Our natural response has to be a willing desire of submission. So the idea here is that if you haven't reached that conclusion, then don't say it. Allah is not forcing us to say it.

### Free Slavery

Now let's study this verse linguistically. The first word is ایاک which means, **only you**! Linguistically ایاک excludes everything and everyone except the object of the conversation. It is a clause of exclusivity. So what we're saying is "you", "only you" and no one else but you.

The next word is نعبد, which means **we **worship. نعبد comes from the word عبادۃ which means worship, which in turn comes from the root word عبد which means slave. What is the connection between worship and slavery? What's the relationship there? The relationship is that **worship is a demonstration of ones slavery**. It is proof of one slavery. It is a an act of devotion and dedication that establishes one slavery. I'll give you a very very basic example, if I stood on top of a building and screamed at the top of my lungs for two hours, I am a slave of Allah. I am a slave of Allah. I am a slave of Allah! But then when it's time for prayer, I do not pray - I can say that I am the slave of Allah all day long, but did I back it up? Did I prove it? No! So our worship, our عبادۃ is proof and evidence of the fact that we are in fact the slaves of Allah. Otherwise, it's just an empty claim.

![](images/166/23fd8cf4-d085-43b5-a0e9-eb75f7b8751d.jpg)

So اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ means, It is only you and no one else but you that we worship; or willingly enslave ourselves to. Now what's really so powerful about this concept, is that by desiring this enslavement of Allah, we are essentially desiring freedom from everything else; because becoming the slave of Allah, provides freedom from every other slavery of this world. So essentially what we're recognizing through اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ is that if we do not become the slave of Allah, we will end up becoming a slave to everything that is other than Allah. By recognizing our عبودیۃ to Allah, we get the حریۃ - which is true freedom. In completing the عبودیۃ to Allah, we are no longer slaves to ourselves; and that's a truly free person.

If I can't control myself, I'm a slave to myself. Somebody can say, "I'm free!", but then when the food shows up, they can't stop themselves. That's not freedom as far as the Muslim is concerned; he's not عبد اللہ he is عَبْدُ الْھَوَی; he is a slave of his passions. So عبد اللہ is that when that situation arises, he has تقوی of Allah. It's not that the temptation goes away. The desire is still there, because we're all human; so the شھوۃ might be there, but the عبد اللہ can control it; because he's not an عبد of that passion; he is a سید of it. He is a master of it. That's someone who is free - that's the حر. But that mastery is only attained through the slavery of Allah.

The tongue is the same. There are some people who just can't stop backbiting; they just can't stop gossiping. You can tell them! You can point it out to them a million times, but they can't stop. It's amazing. In most cases there are not even aware of it. I'm sure I do things like that as well. But this exists within us - this inability to control our tongues; to speak badly about others; to say things that we really shouldn't be saying; and people can't control it. So learning to control the tongue is also a major thing in Islam.

So when we say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ - it is only you we desire to enslave ourselves to, what's implicit in that دعا is that we desire freedom from the enslavement of everything else that is not from your command; everything else that is considered دنیا - money, houses, cars, children, food, branded clothes, friends, relatives. There are a million shackles that we wear around our necks; and the slavery of Allah frees us from all those shackles.

### Plural Form

![](images/166/2f3e642f-eccf-48a3-8184-e170c2c0a613.jpg)

The next thing I want to point out is the fact that Allah uses the plural form here - اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ. It is only you **we** worship. Now Let me ask you a question. The majority of the time when we pray, are we praying by ourselves or in a group or a congregation? We're by ourselves. So then can I say اِیَّاکَ أَعْبُدُ? It is only you "I" worship? Wouldn't that be more logical? So can I change it to the singular form? No, I cannot! Not permissible! not allowed. I cannot change it. So that's very interesting. Why did Allah fix this statement in the plural? Scholars explain that by fixing this statement in the plural Allah is emphasizing the importance of unity. Allah is emphasizing the concept of unity - that even when I pray by myself, I do not speak just for myself. I speak on behalf of the whole أمۃ ; on behalf of my entire community, I say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ. Why? Because the ultimate way to establish Unity is through the worship of Allah. That's why they say that a family that prays together stays together. A community that prays together sticks together. So the message in this verse and the following verses is to be and stay united as one family, one community, one nation.

### Assistance

The next thing we say is اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ - it is **only you **that we seek **assistance **from. The word نستعین comes from استعانۃ which in turn comes from the root word عون and it means assistance. There are many other words in Arabic for help. There is نصر, there is مُساعَدۃ. there is نجدۃ. And these are all different kinds of help. نصر is a type of help that has a greater sense of urgency to it. It is for emergency type situations.

نَصْرٌۭ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَفَتْحٌۭ قَرِيبٌۭ

When the help of Allah arrives, victory is near

‏إِذَا جَآءَ نَصْرُ ٱللَّهِ وَٱلْفَتْحُ

When the help of Allah arrives and you are victorious in battles

وَثَبِّتْ أَقْدَامَنَا وَٱنصُرْنَا عَلَى ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلْكَفِرِينَ

Keep us steadfast and help us over the nation of disbelievers

When I'm in a situation where I can't do things on my own, and I need someone else to do it for me, the word I would use is نصر. The word عون is used for help in small matters. I need a glass of water. I could get up and get it myself, but if you went and got me a glass of water, you assisted me. That's عون. It is seeking assistance in daily tasks, little little things. So why is this distinction made in سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ where the word عون is used instead of نصر? It is because the quality and the sign of a true believer is that he is someone who turns to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى all times - for all the little little things in his life. He doesn't just think of Allah in major situations, he remembers him in all of his day to day things, like a lover remembers his beloved - all the time! This is exactly what we find in the daily routine of our prophet ﷺ. When he woke up in the morning, what's the very first thing he did? He praised Allah

الْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ الَّذِی اَحْیَانَا بَعْدَ اَمَاتَنَا وَ اِلَیْہِ النُّشُورُ

He went to the toilet

اعوذ بللہ من الرجس النجس الخبیث المخبث الشیطان الرجیم

He sought the protection of Allah from شیطان. He got on his horse

سُبْحَانَ الَّذِی سَخَّرَ لَنَا ھَٰذَا

Before he ate his food بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. As soon as he finished eating اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ. He left his house

بِسْمِ اللَّہِ تَوَکُّلٌ عَلَیْہِ اللَّہِ وَلَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّۃَ اِلَّا بِاللَّہِ الْعَلِیِّ الْعَظِیمِ

So عون is used for seeking assistance of Allah - constantly. So the first reason is constant remembrance.

The second reason we constantly seek his assistance, is because we recognize that we are not ideal slaves. We are weak human beings; who desperately require assistance every step of the way. We are dominated by the desires of this world. We are challenged by innumerable distractions every single waking moment of our lives. We constantly walking this slippery slope; so we know it's not going to be easy to maintain our عبودیۃ. We are surrounded by the worst 4 enemies that are constantly working against us: First is our نفس, our soul that is weak to begin with. Then we have this دنیا the temptations of this world. Then we have ھوی - our overwhelming desires and passions; and then there is شیطان himself. We are surround on all sides by these powerful enemies. So we recognize and admit that our عبودیۃ and servitude is not going to be worthy of the رب العلمین; but we're still willing to try; and in that trying, we're going to need our masters help. When we fail over and over again, we will constantly need Allah to pick us up again - because only he can protect and assist us. Therefore اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ ; it is **only you** we turn to for assistance in everything. So اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ is making a willing commitment to Allah; and اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ is asking him for assistance in getting the job done.

The third reason is to seek the closeness of Allah; the قرب of Allah. This is also the reason why the conversation switched from third person to second person. When we say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ, we are now directly speaking with Allah. It's a more intimate conversation. And we're using that for two thing - worship and assistance. Scholars tell us, what this verse teaches us, is we all want to be near and dear to Allah. We all want to be beloved to Allah. We all want the nearness of Allah - but the ultimate way to establish that nearness; that love of Allah, is by worshiping him, and by calling out to him for assistance. That's why in a حَدِیثٌ قُدْسِیٌّ very explicitly Allah says, my slave does not get closer to me more so than by doing what I have told them to do. And that is the five times daily prayer. You want to be close to Allah - pray 5 times a day and keep calling him for assistance.

### Sequence

![](images/166/1bd391c8-bf59-4171-9cfd-5c663dd0f904.jpg)

The next thing I want to talk about is the sequence in which these two words are used. What is mentioned first? عبادۃ - worship is mentioned first, followed by عون - the assistance of Allah. Why is that important? Because the sequence that you find in the Quran between things, is never coincidental. It always has a specific purpose. It's very precise. It's divine. So why is worship mentioned before seeking help? Because the best way to seek the help and the assistance from Allah; to ask Allah for anything and everything that we may need or want, is to ask after worshiping him. عَبْدُ اللَّہِ ابْنُ مَسْعُودٍ would tell his students

أِحْمَلُو حَوائجَکُم عَلی المکتُوبَۃ

Save your needs and your necessities for the فرض obligatory prayer. Whenever you need something whenever you're dealing with something difficult - don't worry. There is going to be a فرض obligatory prayer right around the corner. Use that as an opportunity to ask Allah to help you solve your problems. So worship and then seek the help and assistance of Allah.

### Repetition of ایاک

The last thing I want to point out is the repetition of this word ایاک. You can see that the word ایاک is used twice. اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ and then اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ. The word ایاک is repeated twice. Now grammatically speaking, if Allah omitted the second ایاک and just said: اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ نَسْتَعِینُ, - It is only you we worship and seek assistance from. That would have been grammatically correct. So then why did Allah repeat it when its not grammatically necessary? Does this mean that the second ایاک is not absolutely necessary to the meaning? No! So let's look at that.

When we say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ نَسْتَعِینُ, both words نعبد and نستعین connect back to the same ایاک. But when I say اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ, they both now have their own ایاک. So what that does, grammatically, is that it makes both independent of one another. Now why would Allah do that? Because you see, Allah said worship and then seek assistance. That's the optimal method. That would be ideal. But there might be some of us who have lost their way. They're not praying to Allah. They're not worshiping Allah; and yet, because of life, they run into some difficulties or some tragedies; and in that difficult moments they need Allah's help. So Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى very subtly separates the two - just a little bit. He makes them independent for the sake of these people, to let them know that even if you weren't worshiping me and praying to me, don't worry, you can still ask for my help and assistance. My door is still open to you.

Worshiping me and then seeking assistance would be the ideal way; but even if you're not worshiping me, I will not deny my assistance to you. You can always come to me. You can always talk to me. You can always turn to me!

### Guidance

Now considering the relationship between a master and a slave, what is the slave constantly in need of from his master?Commands! Instructions! If there are no instructions from a master, technically that means you are free. So as عبد we need some instructions. Yes? The Arabic word for instructions is ھدایۃ which comes from the root ھُدی. So the next logical progression in this line of thought is that یا اللہ, now that I have enslaved myself to you; now that I'm committed to you; now that I'm depending on your assistance to fulfill my commitment, what is it that you want me to do? How can I pay off this debt of submission? How can I move towards you? How do I get closer to you? Give me some instructions. Give me some ھِدایۃ - and so the Surah progresses into اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ. Guide us, instruct us towards the straight path.

Scholars point out something very important here, in the sequence of these declarations. As we just learned اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ is a commitment; it is a declaration; a promise. It's our sincere عہد شریف. That is followed by اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ, a call for assistance after we put in the effort. That is then followed by asking for instructions - اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ. If you look at this sequence, we're not saying, Give me the instructions first. If I think I'll be able to do them; if they make sense to me; if I feel I'll be able to find the time to do them, then I'll do them. NO NO NO. All of that has already been covered in the earlier verses. Starting from اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ we are committing; we're making a promise of عبودیۃ - that we will do whatever you tell us to do; cause that's all part of being a slave. You don't become a slave and then say, I'll do it if and when it makes sense to me. The making sense of it all was done in the first 3 verses

اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رب العلمین؛ اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِیمِ؛ مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ

Those were the verses that make us realize that Allah is worthy of our عبودیۃ. Those were the verses where we got convinced of Allah's mastery over us. Those were the verses where we completed our homework; did all our research. You understand? The first 3 verses complete the theory portion of this . Starting from اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ, it's practicals; it's action. Some people ask this questions, why do we have to take عہد? Why is that so important? Why do I have to make a promise to Allah? Because that is the action of commitment. You can think and ponder and plan all you want, but until you actually commit to something, you cannot produce change, isn't that true? Weight loss? The most common problem of our time! We can read all about weight loss. In fact, according to a study, obese people are usually the ones who know the most about losing weight; but until they commit to a lifestyle of good diet and exercise, they cannot lose the weight, right? Once they start their journey there are many resources available that can assist them in reaching their goal; because commitment and help go together. This is why we have اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ - I commit only to you and I seek only your assistance. The more sincere we are in our commitment, the more we can expect Allah to help us on our journey towards him.

## Guidance To The Straight Path

### Guidance

Now let's understand اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ. This verse is commonly translated as, "Guide us to the straight path". What does that mean? Think about this. When I say to you, "Guide me to your house", what am I asking for? Instructions, right? Directions. So that's what guidance essentially is - instructions or directions. Now how will you give me instructions? What are the different ways in which you can give me instructions? So for instance you can say "Take this particular road, and go 3 miles on it and then turn left. This is how we typically give guidance or directions to a particular destination.
Another way in which you can guide me is by saying, "Just stand at this bus stop. Take this particular bus and get off at this particular stop". This is guidance **through** the bus; it is guidance through a medium or through an intermediary. So what you're saying is that you don't have to do anything. The bus will do all the work for you. All you have to do, is make sure you're at the bus stop at the right time and catch the right bus; and the bus will get you to your destination.
The third way is that you say, "Look! I'm headed home right now. Why don't you jump in the car with me and I'll take you all the way. So these are the 3 ways by which you can guide me, or direct me to your house. First is through instructions; the second is through a medium, and the third is by literally taking me to that destination yourself. You understand the differences?

Now this is what is so cool about this verse - listen to this carefully. Asking for guidance through directions or instructions in Arabic is اِھْدِنَا اِلَی صِرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. Asking for guidance using a medium would be اِھْدِنَا بِالصِّرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. Asking for completely assisted guidance اِھْدِنَا عَلَی صِرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. Now what's interesting about the فاتحۃ is that we are not asking Allah for instructions by saying اِھْدِنَا اِلَی صِرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. We are not asking for instructions by saying اِھْدِنَابِالصِّرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. We are not saying اِھْدِنَا عَلَی صِرَاطِ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ. We are not using any of those words. We are just directly saying اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ. We're omitting the الی, the ب and the علی in our request for instructions. You see that? Why is that relevant? Because according to the rules of Arabic linguistics, when you don't use any of these words, what you actually mean is **all** of them at the same time. You understand? The principle of Arabic is that if you are using none of them, you're linguistically using all of them; otherwise you would have to use three separate sentences. So now when we say اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ what we're really saying is: direct me to the straight path through your instructions - which is the Quran. We're saying guide me to the straight path through your intermediaries - through the example of Rasul Allah ﷺ, Maulana Ali, Imams and the دعات; and finally, hold my hand and walk me along this path until I reach my destination; grant me your constant companionship as I walk along this path. SubhanAllah! Isn't that amazing?!

A school counselors guidance is information. A traffic officers guidance is information. The GPS guidance is information. But Allah's guidance is more than just information. Allah's guidance has to do with the personal choices that we have to make at every given moment. So we're not just asking for directions, we are also asking him for the strength and his assistance in helping us making those right decisions. These are two very different things. Because sometimes we can have all the right information and still make the wrong decisions.

Also, if guidance was just information, how many times do we need information. One time. Once I've been given the right information I do not have to ask for it again. Once I know the direction to your house - that's it! I'm not going to ask you again. How many times do we ask Allah for guidance? Over and over and over again. As a standard. It's like drinking water. You cannot say I drank water yesterday, I don't need it again. It does not work. If you want to survive you have to drink again and again every single day. Just as our bodies keep getting thirsty for water, our hearts keep starving for guidance. So we keep coming back to Allah with اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ.

![](images/167/7500cce9-7ea7-43ad-a8ea-d087e3ba3830.jpg)

Now let's look at this word اھدنا which comes from the root word ھُدی . In this context the derivative is ھدایۃ which means guidance. It is also the root word for ھدیۃ. What does ھدیۃ mean? It means a gift. Now for an Arab living in the desert, when he's lost, what is the greatest gift you can give him? More than water! Water is not good enough; because water will last him one more day. Food will last him couple more days. So from an Arab's perspective - an Arab who is lost in the desert, what is the greatest of all gifts for him? Guidance! Directions back to his home; back to his family - that's the ultimate gift. So we're asking Allah, not just for any gift; we're asking Allah for the ultimate gift. Allah's guidance is the ultimate gift for a believer! It is so much more than just instructions or information. It completely changes the perspective of a believer from just a set of rules, to this sense of gratitude; because instructions and directions are now perceived as a gift.

### Path

Now let's look at صراط. What's a صراط? It's translated as a path, right? But in the Arabic language there are certain rules that go with the word صراط. The first rule is that it can only be used for a straight path. If a road is bumpy, or if it curves, or wavy, it cannot be called a صراط. By definition, the word صراط means the path has to be straight. صُراط is actually a perfectly straight sword. If the sword is curved, it's called سیف. So صراط means a perfectly straight path.

The second rule is that صراط can only be used for a **wide** path. By definition, it cannot be a narrow path. It's is a wide path that allows multiple people to travel on it at the same time. So think of it like a major highway with multiple lanes - except that it's a perfectly straight road. You understand?

Now there are other words in Arabic that can be used for a path. There is سبیل, there is فَج, there is طریق. Now what's interesting about these words, is that there are plurals available for all of these words. For سبیلthere is سُبُل. for طریقthere is طوارق. For فَجthere is فِجاج but there is no plural for the word صراط. It is a singular word; and this is why صراط also means - the **only path** that is available. So imagine there are two islands that are connected by a single bridge. The water between these two islands is very turbulent. You cannot take a boat to go from one to the other. The only way for you to go from one island to other is this one single, wide bridge. There is no other path you can take except this bridge. So if there is no alternative route available, then that bridge, that one singular wide path would be called صراط. So linguistically, the word صراط has a few peculiar characteristics. It is a wide path. It is a straight path; and it is the only path to a destination.

![](images/167/8d9fab28-3712-415b-982e-31adf5393271.jpg)

Now what does that means for us? It means that there can be many many paths available to us in terms of how we choose to live our lives. There can be many religions, there can be many belief systems - all of which can be correct; but the path that directly leads to Allah can only be one. It means that even though there are many different religions and philosophies - all explaining how to reach god - there can only be one true religion. A religion (a path) that will be the shortest distance between point A and point B - connecting the slave to his master.

Now what's even more interesting is that in this verse Allah did not say صراط, he said الصراط. He used the definite article - one particular path. صراط already meant one single path by definition. Then why say الصراط? Because even though Islam is the one true religion; there can only be one true sect within Islam itself. Isn't that incredible? Just by understanding the linguistics of the word, it answers ALL the questions we have. People ask all the time - Why does there only have to be one true religion? Why can't every religion be the true religion? Because the word صراط does not have a plural. So it can only be singular. It must be straight, which means it must take the shortest route to connect us with Allah; because the shortest distance between two points is a straight line. Also it must be wide, which means there must be acceptance for others along the way. But then there is also an الصراط, which means there is only one sect that will directly lead to Allah using the shortest distance. The most direct path. SubhanAllah! All these meanings encapsulated in the single word صراط. Not to say that the rest of Islam and all the other sects are garbage! No! Of course not. It'll just take them longer to get there; but they will all eventually get there. And so this is why all Muslims of all sects pray for the knowledge of this direct path to Allah by saying اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ.

### Straight

Now the word مستقیم. How is مستقیم translated? Straight path? But wait. We know the word صراط already implies straight; because if it wasn't straight, it wouldn't be a صراط. So why is Allah repeating himself? He's not. Because مستقیم does not just mean straight, it means upright. The word مستقیمcomes from قام or أستقامۃ which means to stand up. Not to go forward but to go up. Its from قیام, which means to stand upright. Like in our prayers we have قیام , we have رکوع and سجود, right? What does قیام mean? To stand up straight, right?

So when we say اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ, we are asking Allah for guidance on a path which heads which way? Straight up. A path of spiritual elevation! Now what happens when I travel on a path that puts me on an elevation? The more I travel on it, the higher I will rise over دنیا. Isn't that true? And as I rise higher, who am I getting closer to? I'm getting closer to Allah!

Now what happens to the view as you rise higher? It becomes better! You start getting a better perspective on things. We already established that walking this path is about gaining knowledge - so the more we learn, the more aware we become of the realities of this world and of the afterlife.

But there's a negative side to this as well. Because the higher you climb, the more danger there is of falling, right? If you are only 1 foot high and you fall you'll be okay. If you're 20 feet high and you fall, you're not going to be so okay. If your 100 feet high and you fall? اِنَّا لِلَّہِ وَ اِنَّا اِلَیْہِ رَاجِعُونَ right? In other words, the person who is traveling on this path for a long time, is he in less danger or is he in more danger? He is in more danger. This is why you'll see that the higher the rank of the person the more خشوع he has in his صلوۃ. The more fear he has in his heart. The more he cries on his prayer mat during صلوۃ. This is why we find these صحابۃ exercising extreme caution at every single step of their lives. Not because they loved to be miserable, which is the impression we get sometimes reading books of سیرۃ. Constant prayer, constant fasting. Allah never demanded that! yet we find these people striving to nth degree. Why? Because of this caution and this superior perspective. We look at them and think they're suffering, but if you asked them, they'd say they're enjoying - because they're excited about their destination and so the work to get there no longer feels like work, you understand?

Think about a rocket that is headed towards space. That's الصِّرَاطُ الْمُسْتَقِیمُ. It is on a path that is directed upwards, right? Now we know that this rocket needs a lot of force to overcome the gravity of this earth, isn't it? But once it breaks through the gravitational field and enters space, there's no effort required for movement. All it needs is a bit of thrust here and there and it'll just glide to its destination. That's what the صحابۃ aimed for. That's why they put in so much work; but then once they perfected it, once they rose above the perspective of this دنیا; once they became aware of this unlimited and infinite space of the solar systems, galaxies and the universe - this insignificant دنیا became worthless to them. If you look at it from this perspective, you can reconcile a lot of their behavior in this world.

![](images/167/e1618e1c-a163-4415-9af3-b9d008a48ba0.jpg)

So we pray اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ what we're actually saying is grant us the gift of guidance, grant us the gift of true knowledge through your book. Guide us through the practical examples of your beloved and chosen people; and as we begin our own spiritual journey towards you, accompany us every single step of the way. Assist us as we walk this this path that is straight, wide and the only path, that elevates us towards you. Help us see things clearly as we rise over this دنیا and move towards you; and protect us from falling back into this دنیا. This is the عون the assistance that we beg for daily in our 5 prayers.

### The 3 Paths

Then we say صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ The path of those upon whom you bestowed your favors. This verse uses the past tense. Why past tense? Because all these role models exist in the past. When students want to get some good career advise, who do they seek that advise from? From their fellow students or from graduates? Obviously the graduates. You're not going to ask your friends who are studying with you in school about career advise. If you want to become a doctor, you'll take advise from graduate doctors. What courses did you take? How did you get into that program? What are the things that I need to focus on? So the verse says صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ The path of those on whom you **showered** your blessings. The path of the prophets; the path of the messengers; the martyrs; and the righteous people. The word انعمت comes from the word نِعَم which means something very smooth and soft. You know the word in Arabic for cattle - cows and sheep, they're called انعام because they walk slowly; they move in a gentle manner. So this verse is identifying and specifying the path requested in the previous verse - اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ. O Allah guide me to the path of spiritual elevation! Which path? The path of those who were successful in reaching you; those who you got through the trial of the world in a soft and smooth manner and achieved spiritual elevation. That's صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ.

Then he says غَیْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ Not on the path of those who received your غضب; who became the recipients of your wrath. وَلَا الضَّالِّینَ and not on the path of those who are lost.
This is so interesting. When the verse talked about blessings نعمۃ it read - on whom **you** showered your blessings; but when it came to غضب the verse reads الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ. The one's who received غضب. It leaves out the subject. It just says those who received anger; not those who received your anger. No! You see the difference?

When I say "He got beaten". The question is who did the beating? It could be one; it could be many, right? So what this is telling us is that those who receive Allah's anger, they're not just receiving Allah's anger, they're receiving the anger of messengers; of angels; of other believers; the heavens, the earth. They're receiving the wrath and the anger from all directions. And so the verse does not mention Allah when it talks about غضب. But it specifically mentions him when it talks about blessings اَنْعَمْ تَ عَلَیْھِمْ the ones you showered blessings upon. But غَیْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ not on those who received anger. Because anger or غضب was never the intention of Allah to begin with. Because he's الرحمن and الرحیم. So whatever they did - they did it to themselves. They earned it themselves. But the ones who received blessings. Allah helped them all along - towards the right path; with the help of instructions; with the help of intermediaries; and by helping them along the way with his ھدی; with his knowledge. So in other words صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ means - on the path of those who you granted knowledge. The knowledge of realities; the examples of how to act upon that knowledge through role models like Rasul Allah ﷺ and Maulana Ali; the Imams; and then that personal divine companionship itself along the way. SubhanAllah!

So صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ The path of those who you gave knowledge to and who acted upon that knowledge.
غَیْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ. Not on those who you gave knowledge to but they chose not to act upon it.

وَلَاضَالِّینَ and not on the path of those who act, but they have no knowledge of what they're doing. ضالین means the ones who are lost. Who do not know any better. Notice the word صراط is not used with مَغْضُوبٌ عَلَیْھِمْ and it's not used with ضالین, because they're the ones who did not choose this path.

### Wrath vs Lost

Now let me try to explain the difference between مَغْضُوبٌ عَلَیْھِمْ and ضالین. I'll give you an example. I have two kids Erum and Amir. Erum is 13 years old and Amir is 7 MashaAllah. They both love chocolate chip cookies. I buy a fresh box from the store. I show it to Erum and I say - look guys are know these are your favorite cookies. See how nice and fresh they look? Here! Smell it. Smells good? You can't have them! I'm going to place these right here on the table, OK? I'm going to go downstairs in my basement to record these sessions on فاتحۃ. When I come back, we will eat these together; but until I return don't touch these cookies; and also make sure that Amir does not eat them. OK? you understand? Good girl.

I go in the basement. I record my sessions; and when I go upstairs. what do I find on the couch? Two cookie monsters. GHUP GHUP GHUP. They're both wolfing the cookies down. Now who am I going to get really mad at? Erum or Amir. Obviously Erum. Why? Because I explicitly; very clearly told her not to eat the cookies. Amir did not even know about it. So obviously I am going to be a LOT MORE angry with Erum. You knew better! I clearly told you not to eat these cookies and you still disobeyed me. You are grounded till you're 30; and while I'm scolding her Amir is looking at me innocently and still eating the cookies .

But you understand the difference? The difference between مَغْضُوبٌ عَلَیْھِمْ and ضالین is that مغضوب are the ones who were given the نعمۃ of knowledge, and they still disobeyed Allah. They still did the wrong thing. But ضالین are the one's who had no clue that they were doing the wrong things. Doesn't mean they're innocent. Doesn't mean they will not be taken to account. Because Allah gave us all intellects - which is a proof and حجۃ for and against each and everyone of us. That we need to seek the knowledge. So these people should have strived to gain the necessary knowledge of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. and then do the right thing. They should have made the effort to know better. So they're not off the hook in any sense. But they're still better than the مَغْضُوبٌ عَلَیْھِمْ. You understand?

Some people when they learn this, they say - you know what - I'm simply not going to gain any knowledge. I'm going to stay ignorant; I'll just stay among the ضالین. Wait wait wait. That's not a good idea buddy. Because Allah says غَیْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ وَلَضَّالِّینَ not on the path of those who received anger; nor from the ones who remained lost. So the ضالین will lose out and will be punished for not trying to find the right path. They're both groups of losers. So its really NOT a smart idea to be from either of those groups. The group we want to belong to is from the اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ group.

This is the real message of the Fateha - the balance between knowledge and action.

بِسْمِ اللَّہِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِیمِ. اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِینَ. اَلرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِیمِ. مَالِکِ یَوْمِ الدِّینِ

These are all about the knowledge we need. Then

اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ

This is our recognition, and our acceptance of this knowledge being the one true knowledge; and our commitment to Allah. After that

اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ

Guide us to the ONLY straight and wide path of knowledge that elevates us towards you. Guide us to the path of ایمان. Give us the ULTIMATE gift of ایمان.

صِرَاطَ الَّذِینَ اَنْعَمْتَ عَلَیْھِمْ

Through the path of Islam which is your true blessing. The path that is the perfect balance between knowledge and action. Through the examples of role models of the people of the past that you have favored. The ones who were wise; who had wisdom. Remember definition of wisdom?. To have correct knowledge and then act upon it. So make us among those who when you provide them with knowledge, they acted upon it.

غَیْرِ الْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَیْھِمْ

Do not make us among those who gain knowledge, but then do not act upon that knowledge. For those are the people who will receive wrath from all directions on the day of judgment because of their bad decisions

وَلَا الضَّالِّینَ

And do not make us from among those who act without knowledge. Because they are the ones who are lost.

So the essence of سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ is 3 things. Gaining the knowledge of Allah. Striving to acquire the knowledge of his Tawheed. Making a commitment, a promise, a عہد, which has to come first before action. And only after that comes the instructions, the ھدی. And then we ask Allah to make us among those who created a balance between their knowledge and action.

Maulana Ali says.

اَلعِلمُ بِدُونِ العَمَل وَبَالٌ . وَالعَمَلُ بِدُونِ العِلمِ ضَلالٌ

Knowledge without action is a calamity on the soul. And action without knowledge is ignorance - it's a perversion.

Success lies in the middle. It lies in the balance of knowledge and action. That is the true manifestation of عبودیۃ . A slave who obeys through knowledge; who strives to understand the wisdom of his masters commands; and then practically acts upon it. He demonstrate his حمد for its master.

## Staying Steadfast On The Path

Now that we've completed سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ I wanted to do a session on this idea of استقامۃ which is remaining steadfast on the straight path. This is one of the major challenges of walking the path. We all want to walk the straight path, but we're not always able to, for various reasons; and then we end up getting frustrated with ourselves and sometimes with religion itself. This is something that I have personally struggled with extensively in my life, but then AlHamdulillah I came across some material and some perspectives that has helped me a lot - and so I thought I'd share it with you, hoping and praying that'll it'll do the same for you.

### Foundation

In سُورَۃُ الْفَاتِحَۃِ we request Allah for guidance by saying اِھْدِنَا الصِّرَاطَ الْمُسْتَقِیمَ - Guide us towards the straight path. This verse is the essence of the Surah. The initial verses basically build up to this one. It's the prayer of all prayers - Guide us! Guide us through instructions, role models and through your divine companionship. What is this صِرَاطُ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ exactly? Allah defines it in سورۃ یس. He says:

> ‏وَأَنِ ٱعْبُدُونِى ۚ هَذَا صِرَطٌۭ مُّسْتَقِيمٌۭ ‎۶۱
>
> And Indeed you should worship Me. This is a Straight Path.

So عبادۃ or عبودیۃ - willing submission is how Allah defines the straight path. The person who willingly submits himself or herself to Allah is the one who has found the straight path - which AlHamdulillah we all have. But how do we walk the path? Because we don't get to your destination by just finding the correct path; we get to it by walking that path, right? So how do we walk this path? What's the term for walking this path? That term is تقوی - a strong mindfulness for the presence of our master in our lives. تقوی (piety) is essential for عبودیۃ - for attaining the ranks of servitude. That's what we asked for when we said اِیَّاکَ نَعْبُدُ وَ اِیَّاکَ نَسْتَعِینُ. It is only you we worship (again, worship being the straight path), and it is only you we seek help from. What is the method of استعانۃ - seeking Allah's help? He explains that in سُورَۃُ الْبَقَرَۃِ.

> ‏وَٱسْتَعِينُوا۟ بِٱلصَّبْرِ وَٱلصَّلَوٰةِ ۚ وَإِنَّهَا لَكَبِيرَةٌ إِلَّا عَلَى ٱلْخَشِعِينَ ‎۴۵
>
> And seek help in patience and prayer. Truly it is extremely heavy and hard except for the ones who possess knowledge of Allah.

Same word ٱسْتَعِينُوا۟. So now we just solved this equation. We all desire صِرَاطُ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ the straight path - مستقیم as I said earlier, is a path with استقامۃ built into it. A path of spiritual elevation. How is it achieved? Through عبودیۃ. Willing servitude to the will of Allah. How do we walk this path? By developing piety - تقوی. - an awareness of who Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is; by establishing a connection with him. How do we connect with him? بِٱلصَّبْرِ وَٱلصَّلَوٰةِ - Through patience and prayer. Now we all know about the daily prayers, but what does صبر mean here in this context? It means to be steadfast and consistent with prayer وَإِنَّهَا لَكَبِيرَةٌ - which is truly an extremely difficult task for most; إِلَّا except; ٱلْخَشِعِينَ, the one's who possess the knowledge and awareness of the majesty of their lord; those who have تقوی.

![](images/168/afef5a3f-8851-4eea-9ec8-f3a9bdc1d0ee.jpg)

So it all ultimately boils down to consistency and being steadfast - استقامۃ? What is استقامۃ? It comes from the same root word قام for مستقیم. The word أِستَقِم means to stay firm. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says in the Quran

> ‏وَأَقِيمُوا۟ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَآتُوا۟ ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَٱرْكَعُوا۟ مَعَ ٱلرَّكِعِينَ ‎۴۳

He's telling us to establish; to be firm in our صلوۃ. So استقامۃ is to establish something firmly or to be solid in something; to be firm in it. Resilience is one of the words; Grit - some people use the word grit. So استقامۃ is the ability to stand firm and be consistent in our in our ایمان; in our worship and our belief.

![](images/168/59d175a9-8e91-4862-8d53-491dab98e134.jpg)

To give you sort of an illustration from the physical world, you can learn about استقامۃ by observing a solid tree. Why? Because it stands firm and solid, even when the wind blows. Even if a storm hits, it stays firm. Now the question is, how can we be like that? How do we develop the ability to stay strong on this path despite the storms of our situations and environments? You must have noticed that in many verses Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى mentions two things when he talks about faith

الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ وعمل الصلحات

He always couples ایمان with عمل. He couples faith with action. So we know that there are two parts to what we're supposed to do. There is the inner part; and then there's the outer part. What's the inner part? The belief - the ایمان. Another way we can think about this in terms of عبادۃ is that there is worship for the heart; and then there is worship of the limbs; the body, the tongue, the ears, the eyes. You understand? There are the internal عبادات and then there are the external عبادات. The internal part has to do with our belief system; our ایمان. - how we think of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى ; how we view Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى ; how we respond internally with the things that we face in life. That's internal - our certainty or doubt. Certainty is یقین Doubt is شک.

What are the external عبادات? the prayers, reading Quran, guarding our ears, guarding our eyes - all of that are the external type of عبادہ.

### Heart is in Control

Now what do you think is essential for استقامۃ? The internal or the external? Our prophet ﷺ said in a HADEES:

قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّہِ صَلَّی اللَّہُ عَلَیْہِ وَ آلِہِ وَ سَلَّمَ

> أِنَّ فِی الجَسَدِ مُضْغَۃ۔ أِذَا صَلُحَتْ صَلَحَ الجَسَدُ کُلُّہ وَ أِذَا فَسَدَتْ فَسَدَ الجَسَدُ کُلُّہ۔ أَلا وَھِیَ القَلْبٌ
>
> Indeed in the body there is a lump of flesh. If it is set right, then the entire body will be set right. And if it is corrupted, then the entire body will be corrupted. Indeed it is the heart.

اِنَّ فِی الْجَسَدِ مُضْغَۃً- indeed in the body, there's a lump of Flesh

اِذَا صَلُحَتْ صَلُحَ الْجَسَدُ کُلُّہُ - If it's set, right then the entire body will be set right

وَاِذَا فَسَدَتْ فَدَسَ الْجَسَدُ کُلُّ and if it is corrupted then the entire body will be corrupted

اَلَا وَھِیَ الْقَلْبُ indeed it's the heart.

So what are we learning from this HADEES? We're learning that the master of the body, isn't actually the mind - which sounds a little strange - because many people believe that the mind is what is in control. Well, newsflash! It's actually your heart that controls your mind. What do I mean by that? The type of heart that you have, will control your thoughts, your mind, your actions. Whatever your heart desires, your mind will think of ways to fulfill those desires, and then the limbs will make it happen. So the mind becomes a tool, to execute whatever is in the heart. This is why the prophet ﷺ said, that if you take care of this lump of flesh, which is the heart - *everyone be clear that when I say the heart, I'm not talking about the physical heart. I'm talking about the spiritual heart - the *قلب. So if that heart is purified - when you take care of the heart, when you feed the heart; when you rectify the heart - then all your actions will automatically become pure. But when the heart is corrupted; when the heart is starved; when the heart is not taken care of; when the heart is deprived of its spiritual sustenance then the rest of the actions will naturally be corrupted.

Now what's really interesting is that if you look at the chronological theme of revelation, the first verses to be revealed were not about drinking alcohol; or not committing fornication. They were not about the rules of حرام and حلال. The first verses to be revealed were all about building تقوی of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى ; building an attachment with Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى ; about understanding the day of judgement - learning that we will be taken to account for our actions; that we're not just living in this life without a purpose. That there is a purpose to Allah's Oneness.

So the initial verses of revelation were about the day of judgment; about retribution; about life after death; about who Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is and what he wants from us. Why? Because all those initial verses of revelation were building a foundation. They were setting the foundation of استقامۃ.

See if you go back to the example of that solid tree - Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says in Surah Ibrahim when he gives an example of a good word - he says it is like a good tree.

> ‏أَلَمْ تَرَ كَيْفَ ضَرَبَ ٱللَّهُ مَثَلًۭا كَلِمَةًۭ طَيِّبَةًۭ كَشَجَرَةٍۢ طَيِّبَةٍ أَصْلُهَا ثَابِتٌۭ وَفَرْعُهَا فِى ٱلسَّمَآءِ ‎۲۴
>
> Do you not see how how Allah sets forth the example of a good word, which is like a beneficial tree, its root are firmly fixed, and its branches to the sky.

Its foundation is solid ثابت and then its branches rise towards the sky. See you can become elevated. You can grow; you can can get higher - but only if you have solid roots. Does that make sense? The taller a tree gets, the deeper its roots have to go. If you study the way Allah has designed a tree, you'll find that the taller it is, the more deep and wide its roots are spread within the earth. Even mountains have foundation; and the taller it is, the more it has to be grounded within the earth. Everything in existence - in order for it to be solid; and be firm; and to have استقامۃ, it has to have a solid foundation. And so Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is telling us - even in the training that he gives us, he did not start out with rules. He started out with building our foundation.

This is why سورۃ الفاتحہ which is essentially the summary of the entire Quran, it begins by creating an understanding of who Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى is.

> ‏ٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ ٱلْعَلَمِينَ ‎۲‏ٱلرَّحْمَنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ ‎۳‏مَلِكِ يَوْمِ ٱلدِّينِ ‎۴

All about knowing Allah. Once this foundation is built, engendering تقوی, engendering توکل our trust in Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى, only then we are taught to ask for rules and guidance

> ‏إِيَّاكَ نَعْبُدُ وَإِيَّاكَ نَسْتَعِينُ ‎۵‏ٱهْدِنَا ٱلصِّرَطَ ٱلْمُسْتَقِيمَ ‎۶‏صِرَطَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَنْعَمْتَ عَلَيْهِمْ غَيْرِ ٱلْمَغْضُوبِ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا ٱلضَّآلِّينَ ‎۷

The beginning verses are designed to build a solid foundation so that when the rules are revealed, we can have استقامۃ. You understand?

Let's go back to the example of the tree. When the wind blows, if the roots of the tree are deep and firm within the ground, what happens to the tree? Well, it's leaves might move, the branches might shake, but the tree stays standing right? It's solid! But what happens to a tree that doesn't have deep roots. It'll get knocked over, right?

### Unhealthy Shame

I think the problem with Islamic training these days, is that we are raised in such a way that we don't have solid roots. Our training method is the "rule shaming" method - the rule-oriented / shaming method. This is the method where you growing up as a kid, the only thing you really learn about is حرام and hellfire. The emphasis is on - if you do حرام you will go to Hell Fire, right? That's how many of us were raised, and that's how many of us are raising our children. Well it is true that there are things that are حرام. It is true that there is hellfire. It is true that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى has anger and wrath - this is all true. But why are kids being taught that, **above** his Mercy? Why are we not teaching our children that Allah is the most merciful - and that even if you make a mistake you can repent, and you can try again. Why is this not the message that is being taught? and the answer unfortunately - is because this is to control the behavior of the child; and so many of us use fear as that control mechanism. The only problem is that it backfires.

Do you know how it backfires? I'll tell you how it backfires. When you're using false fear, or false shame, unhealthy shame to control someone's behavior, you're not building solid roots. You're just building avoidance.
Imagine that you grow up in a household where you're always being told - you better watch out or the police is going to throw you in jail. You better not make a mistake, you better not do this; you better not do that - basically you're taught to be afraid of the police. Why? Because the police is made out to be this very wrathful, scary entity. Now if you grow up with this kind of training - best case scenario - you will learn to to avoid the police. But are you going to want to be close to the police? Will you want to be best friends with the police? Are you going to confide your problems to the police? Are you going to ask the police for help when you're in trouble? No! Because you don't trust the police; and you've been told that the police is just there to punish you. You see?

So what's happening is that this type of training is not building استقامۃ in our youth and in our own selves. It's building avoidance. So many people raised in this way, when they grow up, they don't want to have anything to do with Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى. They actually want to avoid Allah and religion. Why? Because they say you know what? Everything is حرام and no matter what I do, I'm going to end up going to hell anyway. Everything I do is wrong anyway, so what's the point in trying? Oh, you know I if I make any small mistake Allah is going to punish me, so I should just avoid this whole Allah thing. I should just avoid this whole religion thing. You see what's happened? It has backfired. Why? Because we are trying to grow this tree of Islam within ourselves, and our children - without any solid foundation. So even if the tree of such an Islam miraculous grow, it's not going to survive the smallest earthquake.

We need to focus on building a solid foundation - so that when life hits us - because we are going to be shaken in life - look at the times we live in - all this temptations around us. The tree of Islam within our hearts will be shaken from time to time through tests and trials. Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى says in the Quran

> ‏أَمْ حَسِبْتُمْ أَن تَدْخُلُوا۟ ٱلْجَنَّةَ وَلَمَّا يَأْتِكُم مَّثَلُ ٱلَّذِينَ خَلَوْا۟ مِن قَبْلِكُم ۖ مَّسَّتْهُمُ ٱلْبَأْسَآءُ وَٱلضَّرَّآءُ وَزُلْزِلُوا۟ حَتَّىٰ يَقُولَ ٱلرَّسُولُ وَٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ مَتَىٰ نَصْرُ ٱللَّهِ ۗ أَلَآ إِنَّ نَصْرَ ٱللَّهِ قَرِيبٌۭ ‎۲۱۴
>
> Or think you that you will enter Paradise without such as came to those who passed away before you? They were afflicted with severe poverty and ailments and were so shaken that even the Messenger and those who believed along with him said, "When the Help of Allah?" Yes! Certainly, the Help of Allah is near!

‏أَمْ حَسِبْتُمْ أَن تَدْخُلُوا۟ ٱلْجَنَّةَ وَلَمَّا يَأْتِكُم مَّثَلُ ٱلَّذِينَ خَلَوْا۟ مِن قَبْلِكُم

Do you think that you will enter Paradise without going through that which those who came before you went through?

مَّسَّتْهُمُ ٱلْبَأْسَآءُ وَٱلضَّرَّآءُ وَزُلْزِلُوا۟

They were struck with calamity, adversity, struggle and they were shaken. The word زلزل in Arabic is from the same root as the word زلزال which means earthquake. .

حَتَّىٰ يَقُولَ ٱلرَّسُولُ وَٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ مَتَىٰ نَصْرُ ٱللَّهِ

They were so shaken so much that even the messengers, and those with the messengers asked when will the help of Allah come? And Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى

أَلَآ إِنَّ نَصْرَ ٱللَّهِ قَرِيبٌۭ

Indeed! the help of Allah is near. The help of Allah isn't just there, it doesn't just exist as a concept - it's there right now with you as you're going through the test. Allah says that we are closer to us than our jugular vein.

So we are going to be shaken from time to time. How does a building withstand an earthquake? The answer all goes back to the type of foundation it has - that will determine its استقامۃ. So the question we have to ask ourselves is... What type of foundation do I have with Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى? When I am tested through pain or pleasure, will I be able to withstand it and be firm on the straight path?! If the answer is no... then I need to work on my relationship with Allah, not on the rules.

This is why scholars said that the first verse that were revealed didn't say - give up alcohol and give up fornication- because people would have said we'll never give up these things if it started with that - Why? Because they had not yet built a foundation. They had not yet built the roots of the tree. So if you come to a person from the very beginning and you give them rules, they're not going to follow the rules if it goes against what they want.

![](images/168/dbd071d9-0c31-4c3e-9250-c83d94f7a905.jpg)

That is the mistake many of us are making. We're not building a foundation - instead we're constantly throwing rules at them. We're not talking about building an attachment with Allah. We're not teaching them to love Allah. We're not teaching them what it really means to put our reliance on Allah. Where is that being taught to children? The only thing being taught to children is that it's حرام to eat this; it's حرام to drink this. It's حرام to dress like this; it's حرام to say this - That's it! And the problem with that method is it's being rejected. Just as the scholar said - that's exactly what they said. That if you start off with the rules; if Allah had started off with the rules - even with the companions - they would have rejected the rules. But Allah did not start with the rules. Look at جز عم - the 30th chapter of Quran - it's basically Meccan. Majority of the verses in that جز were revealed in the beginning of Islam. Do you find any rules in it? Not really! It talks about Allah, his love, his mercy, the day of judgment. It talks about human beings and their relationship with Allah. It talks about human psychology. It talks about تقوی. It talks about توکل, it talks about retribution. It teaches about جنۃ جھنم. It focuses on how to treat one another- that's what's in جز عم. because that was the foundation building. If you want استقامۃ you have to build a foundation in the love of Allah.

See when we generally talk about تقوی we think in terms of shame and fear; and you might say well isn't that a good thing? What's wrong with fear? Today psychologists are telling us that there is healthy fear and there is unhealthy fear. Okay, there is healthy fear and there's unhealthy fear. Let me give you an example.

You know when you do something really really bad. And you're really scared of your parents, or your loved ones finding out. Sometimes what do you do? Do you run to the parents or to your loved ones? Or do you run away from them? You run away and hide right? So there's basically two ways to respond when you hurt someone or you make a mistake. Imagine that you hurt your closest friend or your spouse. You know, there are some people who feel so bad about it, or so ashamed for what they've done - they hide! They try to avoid the person; they try to hide from the issue; they avoid the conversation. They distance themselves from the person right? Is that a healthy way to deal with it? Does that solve the problem or does it make it worse? It makes it worse. This is exactly what some of us do with Allah. When many of us feel like we've made a mistake, and we feel ashamed - instead of going to Allah, we run away from Allah. We try to hide from Allah. We don't want to pray anymore, because we feel ashamed; or we don't want to talk about Allah, or maybe we want to leave religion completely because we feel ashamed.

But what's a healthier way to deal with that? When you've hurt someone? What's the healthiest way to fix it? To go to the person and what? apologize. To go to the person and say I'm sorry. I regret it! I admit I made a mistake! I'm going to try to do better! That is استغفار and توبۃ. That's what we're supposed to do. That's healthy fear. That's a healthy response, when you make a mistake - that instead of trying to hide from Allah; instead of saying I want nothing to do with religion, I want nothing to do with God. I'm not even going to try; I'm not going to pray because I've committed so many sins - I feel too ashamed to face Allah. That's an unhealthy type of shame. But تقوی is healthy fear. It is the kind of healthy shame that will help you try to stay away from what displeases or angers Allah. And when you do what angers Allah, you will rush to apologize; you will rush to repent; to show your regret and to try to make it better.

The reason I'm mentioning this, is because I feel what's happening, is that we are feeding the wrong kind of fear and shame. Because when you teach about Allah like a scary policeman, نعوذ بللہ, you're encouraging yourself, your children, and the people around you to actually avoid Allah - you're teaching them to run away from Allah; not to run towards him - to apologize; to fix it.

So healthy fear will motivate us to get closer to Allah and repent. Unhealthy shame will make us run away from Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى and avoid him out of shame. So in order for us to have this ability to be firm in our دین to have استقامۃ we need to develop this healthy fear of Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى called تقوی.

### Myth of Perfection

Now how do we build healthy fear? You know what the seed of healthy تقوی is? This is interesting. Let me put it this way. Let me back up. What's the seed to unhealthy shame? We know that a seed grows into a tree right? But it all starts at the seed level. So if you want to address something go look at the seed, the root cause, right? Now if you look at the seed of unhealthy shame, you know what it is? It's thinking that you're supposed to be perfect. It's this myth of perfection that lies at the root of our problems. It's when you're taught, or you believe that you're actually supposed to be perfect. If you believe that, then you will you will react with unhealthy shame when you make a mistake. Why? Because you feel like I was supposed to be perfect and I'm not perfect and I am so ashamed of myself. I'm not even going to try anymore. It leads you into despair. But what was the root cause? what's the seed of it? That you thought you were supposed to be perfect - because that's what we're taught - by our family and our teachers.

![](images/168/3580abad-67b3-473e-a156-a52a38c1306f.jpg)

Now, let's go to the root or the seed of healthy تقوی and healthy استغفار and healthy توبۃ - going back to Allah. Do you know what the root is? You know what the seed is? knowing that all human beings will make mistakes. It's the opposite. It's realizing that the human being is imperfect. Understanding that we have have been created as imperfect human beings. When you know that, when you understand that, then you will respond with repentance - because you know that you're human - and so you messed up. OK so I'm going to try to fix it. I'm going to try and repent. I'll try again. Even if I make the same mistake over and over and over and over again, I will not give up on trying to do better. That's the essence of توبۃ.

The prophet ﷺ said

قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّہِ صَلَّی اللَّہُ عَلَیْہِ وَ آلِہِ وَ سَلَّمَ

> كُلُّ بَنِي آدَمَ خَطَّاءٌ, وَخَيْرُ اَلْخَطَّائِينَ اَلتَّوَّابُونَ
>
> All children of Adam will make mistakes; and the best of them are the ones who repent

We have to let go of this myth of perfection because it is the root cause of so many of our problems. It is the reason why we keep falling into despair. Because if you're told you're supposed to be perfect, and you end up realizing that you're not perfect - that's frustrating!! But when you understand that we are imperfect by design - how can you go against the divine design? Tell me! How can anyone go against the divine design. Allah said that he created human beings imperfect. Look at the descriptions in Quran about our creation

خَلَقَ ٱلْإِنسَنَ مِن صَلْصَلٍۢ كَٱلْفَخَّارِ

خَلَقَ ٱلْإِنسَنَ مِن صَلْصَلٍۢ كَٱلْفَخَّارِ

وَخُلِقَ ٱلْإِنسَنُ ضَعِيفًۭا

خُلِقَ ٱلْإِنسَنُ مِنْ عَجَلٍۢ

إِنَّ ٱلْإِنسَنَ خُلِقَ هَلُوعًا

These are not descriptions of a human being - weak, built from a filthy liquid; hasty, anxious. So how can you go against that? Think about it. It's impossible. And when you try to go against that you will end up hitting with despair. But then Allah balances out our weaknesses by telling us

> ‏ قُلْ يَعِبَادِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَسْرَفُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ لَا تَقْنَطُوا۟ مِن رَّحْمَةِ ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَغْفِرُ ٱلذُّنُوبَ جَمِيعًا ۚ إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلْغَفُورُ ٱلرَّحِيمُ ‎۵۳
>
> Say, "O My servants who have transgressed against themselves [by sinning], do not despair of the mercy of Allah . Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. Indeed, it is He who is the Forgiving, the Merciful."

Tell them - O my beloved servants - who have wronged their own selves. When we commit sin, we aren't hurting Allah. We're just hurting ourselves. We cannot wrong Allah. We cannot take away from Allah. Allah's kingdom is is infinite. You all understand that right? By doing wrong, by committing what's forbidden, we only hurt our own souls. You know, it's like if you drink poison, are you hurting yourself or the doctor? If you refuse to take your medicine, are you hurting yourself or the doctor? yourself Right? So realize that Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَتَعَالَى isn't the one we're wronging. It's ourselves that were wronging. So Allah says

قُلْ يَعِبَادِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَسْرَفُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ

Oh my slave who have wronged; who have transgressed against their own selves.

لَا تَقْنَطُوا۟ مِن رَّحْمَةِ ٱللَّهِ

do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Allah is telling us not to despair - but many of us we despair because we have the wrong concept of ourselves and the wrong concept of Allah. When we think of ourselves, we think I'm supposed to be perfect. I better be perfect. And if I'm not perfect, then I'm worthless. We have this all or none mindset. I either have to be an angel, otherwise I'm going to be a devil right? It's black or white. We have have to stop that. بنی آدم is not one or the other. بنی آدم is not an angel; and he's not a devil. A believer - is actually someone who is flawed, but who continually strives to repent and who tries hard to get it right. Who constantly strives to gets back up after he falls. Who never gives up.

This is why the prophet ﷺ said that all the children of Adam will make mistakes, but the best are those who repent. Letting go of this false sense of perfection will allow us the ability to get back up when we fall.

And you want to hear something else that's really counter-intuitive? شیطان feeds off of this false sense of perfection. He uses it as a weapon against You know how? When you make a mistake, or I make a mistake, he will come to us, and he'll say - look at you! How shameful is you! you're going to go pray now?! after you did this?! You're such a terrible person. You're just a hypocrite. Look at you! You're going to stand before Allah now after doing this? He's not even going to look at you. You think you deserve his mercy? You think you deserve his love? Does any of this sound familiar? This is how شیطان uses this tool of false perfection against us. So he'll put you through this unhealthy shame. He'll drag your face through this unhealthy shame; and then when you're done shaming yourself and bashing yourself up, he'll come to you and say - Do you really want to believe in a God who puts you through this mental torture? Think about it! Allah created you imperfect and is now demanding perfection of you?! What a horrible master!

Look how شیطان works. He's the one who put us through this unhealthy shame, now he's coming at us from a different angle. How could Allah do this to you? How could be put that alcohol in your presence? Didn't he know you're weak. Didn't he know you'd drink it? Why would he do that to you. Doesn't this whole religion thing seem stupid?! And we go... screw religion!

But if we realize that we are not perfect ; and that we were never created to be perfect. Then when شیطان comes at us that way, we say yes! I know I made a mistake but my Allah is the most merciful. My Allah is most forgiving. He will forgive me if I try again; and even if I fail again and repent, he will forgive me again. I will keep on trying until I get it right. That's what Allah wants us to have - Hope in his Mercy.

قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّہِ صَلَّی اللَّہُ عَلَیْہِ وَ آلِہِ وَ سَلَّمَ

> قَالَ اللهُ تَعَالَى : يَا ابْنَ ادَمَ ، إِنَّكَ مَا دَعَوْتََنِي وَرَجَوْتَنِي ، غَفَرْتُ لَكَ عَلَى مَا كَانَ مِنْكَ وَلَا أُبالِي . يا ابْنَ ادَمَ :لَوْ بَلَغَتْ ذُنُوبُكَ عَنانَ السَّماءِ ثُم َّ اسْتَغْفَرْتَني ، غَفَرْتُ لَكَ . يَا ابْنَ ادَمَ : إِنَّكَ لَوْ أَتَيْتَنِي بِقُرَابِ الأَرْضِ خَطَايا ثُمَّ لَقِيتَني لَا تُشْرِكُ بِي شَيْأً ، لَأَتيتُكَ بِقُرَابِها مَغْفِرَةً
>
> I heard the Messenger of Allah (?) say: Allah the Almighty said: O son of Adam, so long as you call upon Me and ask of Me, I shall forgive you for what you have done, and I shall not care.
> O son of Adam, were your sins to reach the clouds of the sky and were you then to ask forgiveness of Me, I would forgive you.
> O son of Adam, were you to come to Me with sins nearly as great as the earth and were you then to face Me, ascribing no partner to Me, I would bring you forgiveness as great as it is.

According to this HADEES, Allah says that if someone goes to Allah with sins that filled the area between the heavens and the Earth - do you know how much that is? Think about it! The area between the earth and the sky... filled with sins! who has that many sins? That's a lot of sins. That's a lot! and you know what Allah says... He says if anyone comes to him with sins filling the area between heavens and the Earth - and they sincerely repent to Allah - Allah will give them forgiveness as big or more. See that's who Allah is! Allah isn't a policeman who's waiting to throw you in jail. Allah is the most merciful. But the problem is that we're not teaching his Mercy. We're just teaching his Wrath. We're not teaching the attachment to Allah; we're just teaching rules. We're not talking about the roots. We're just fighting over the leaves. You have to eat like this, you have to dress like this, you have to walk like this, you have eat, sleep and spit like this... This is allowed this is not allowed. These ingredients in حلال food are really حلال , and which are not like really really حلال. I've actually seen a bottle of water that says Halal certified on it. It was water! This is missing the forest for the trees. This is missing the forest for a leaf on the trees. We aren't focusing on the roots. So what ends up happening - is either this aversion and hatred for religion, completely abandoning it; or this external shell of عبادۃ that crumbles to the ground with the first wind of trial.

I don't belittle the importance of the outer worship. As I started by saying our دین is the outer worship and the inner worship. That's why I began with that. It's both and أمنو and عَمَلُ الصَّالِحَاتِ. But what I'm saying is that you cannot do one and not the other. The problem is that many of us are focusing only on the outer and if you focus only on the outer it's like growing a tree or building a structure without a a foundation or roots. When wind comes it knocks it down; when an earthquake comes it knocks down that building. We have to have solid roots.

### Practical Foundation Building

So how do we build those solid Roots practically? First I told you we have to remove the myth of human perfection. We have to set a different understanding that the human being wasn't created to be perfect. The human being was created to keep repenting and keep trying again.

Can you imagine if a child learning to walk... the first time the child falls just stops trying to walk! Just sits there - and that's the end of walking, right? the person will never walk. Can you imagine a person driving and the moment they make a wrong turn, they just switch off the car, put it in park and stop driving forever. I'm done - that's it! I made a wrong turn. Imagine your GPS saying - you made a wrong turn - sorry! Just stop trying and turn off the car. Your trip is over! But it doesn't, does it? It says something different. What does it say? Recalculating! Rerouting. What is that? That's توبۃ. That's actually literally توبۃ - why? Because توبہ means to return - re+turn. توبہ is your internal mechanism, rerouting you back to Allah. That's the process of استغفار. The word توبہ in the Arabic language literally means to turn around and return. How many times does the GPS of your car re-route you? Just once? If you take several wrong terms, does the GPS say... you know what! You're an idiot! Its pointless trying to give you directions, you keep making the wrong turns. I'm not going to guide you anymore. Does the GPS do that? No! It'll keep rerouting you - million and billions of times if it has to. Infinite number of times. A GPS is capable of doing that! You think Allah cannot do that?! SubhanAllah! So we have to change the way we think. We have to be able to get back up after we fall, to repent, and to keep going and try again. That's the first thing for استقامۃ.

Now what can we do to develop this foundation? It's actually very simple. It's a simple 3 ingredient recipe. The first is صلوۃ. That's like the oxygen of the heart. It's what keeps you spiritually, psychologically and mentally alive. No matter what you do; even if you're doing the worst things possible, don't ignore your prayers. If you can't do 5, do what you can. At least try to pray once a day. And rejoice in that prayer. Be really happy and proud of that prayer - no matter how distracted or imperfect you think it was. This is another problem we all face that stems from this myth of perfection. If I asked you to list out for me all your sins and all your mistakes that you committed today, you'll probably be able to give me a long list. But if I asked you to list out for me all your good deeds of the day, you'll struggle with that. Yes? Because we don't appreciate the good that we do as much as we beat ourselves over the wrong that we do. So from now on, every good deed that you do, I want you to be mindful and conscious of that. I want you to remember Allah after that good deed and be grateful to him; and praise him by saying اَلْحَمْدُ لِلَّہِ. Keep reminding yourself of Allah after every good deed; and feel really happy and proud of it. From remembering to say بسم اللہ before you eat, to saving someone's life and everything in between. I want you to build your relationship with Allah by thinking about him. Consciously realizing that you're pleasing him every time you do something good.

The second is دعا, the daily اذکار, the remembrance of Allah through supplications. That's the GPS rerouting us.

The third is the recitation and understanding of Quran. You have to stay connected to it daily - even if briefly. Even if a verse a day. If you do this daily and consistently, you will build استقامۃ - this resilience on the straight path - the صِرَاطُ الْمُسْتَقِیمِ.
